The entries decide makes into journey 7 (an any-time row, a door, or an owner's command; K-02 `enter`), with the fields they carry, each name K-01's answerField pattern admits (`^[a-z][a-z0-9_.]*$`) and each re-parsed by code under limit 6: the move and cancel rows (rows 15 and 14) and the question and update rows carry no field (the booking by its target); running late (row 16, A7-70) late_minutes (integer); the pro's LATE (A7-14) late_minutes (integer) and the pro's sick text (A7-15) none; the contact change (row 9, A7-98) new_contact (text, never read back in full); the owner's MOVE (A7-23, door S11-12) new_time (time) and new_person (text, left out when the person stays), as v1.4 default 116's offered_time and offered_person; CALL OFF (A7-66) none; NOTE (A7-83) note_words (text); the third-party and death rows (rows 8 and 5) none.

### Named rule added (`tool-api.rules.mjs`)

The twelve earlier rules (v1 to v1.4's nine, v1.6's two and v1.8's one) stay as they are; v1.9 adds one under a new name, with its tables exported beside it.

- **`journeySevenGivesNone`:** an exchange that states a step of journey 7 or of the fee door (`J7_STEP_CALLS`) states no key and its outcome wrote none (journey 7 gives none); its call is one that serves that step, or `gateAndCommit`; its request's stage names that step; a response answers the stage it was sent, with a result that stage gives (`J7_STAGE_RESULTS`, which round 2 gives worseAnswer's offerSooner); every change the customer asked for names, as its approval, the consent the same commit wrote from their own yes (default 147); and, from round 2, a someoneElseAsks request names only a booking of the caller's business: a K-04 bookings row of the fixture's seeds (`seededRows`, which reads `given.seeds` and each seed's `after` files beside this file) whose business_id is `caller.businessId` (default 171). Like v1.6's and v1.8's rules it reads only the contracts' own files, synchronously.

The rules file's name carries no version, so the frozen-contract guard does not protect it (STATE note 25's class): QA's contract test (T-121) pins it.

### Producers and consumers of v1.9

- **Producer:** action-layer engineer: C-074 Tool API, with C-099 bookings and quotes (moves, cancels, stand-ins, what changed, S13-56's one path, the record S13-57), C-100 reservations (times reserved and released), C-106 fee question door (S11-22 to S11-28), C-101 payments and ledger (refunds, the fee's charge and its one pay link), C-102 messages and consent (each customer yes as its consent, the contact switch), C-094 identity and access (who answers, the contact change and its code), C-104 safety and hand-offs, C-105 block and pause (S11-62), C-077 any-time table, C-098 scheduling (open times that keep the gap), C-138 calendar writes, C-087 one commit per turn, C-075 frontier builder (the choices above), C-088 slot filler (the money words, visit.code to its person), as journey 7's tasks land.
- **Consumers:** runtime engineer: C-046 handle actor, C-051 guard (a bare CANCEL), C-054 clock rows and C-055 clock arming (every deadline and silence of default 169), C-124 outbox dispatcher (each row's send class), C-044 gateway (K-14's results through K-05 v1.1, unchanged); AI engineer: C-114 context bundle, C-115 and C-116 decide (the choices and entries above), C-118 composer (each row's master line and default 148's words, the money words); frontend engineer, through K-21's journey 7 version (T-064): C-199 secure page (the customer's changes and answers on their own page), C-192 owner console (Today, Needs you: the fee question, late moves, call-offs), C-195 staff page; action-layer engineer: C-110 Agent API (default 170); architect: C-186 Tool API mock; QA: the contract test `tests/contract/tool-api.v1.9.test.ts` (T-121), C-140 eval harness, C-147 scenario tests. Round 2 adds no consumer: C-075 and C-115 already own the choice whose target `someoneElseAsks` takes (default 171), and the walk that copies it is C-087's.
- **Frozen before it, read here:** K-09 v1.8 (T-1219), which carries v1.6 (T-1173), v1.5 (T-1142) and v1.4 (T-053), K-30 v1 (T-039), K-31 v1 (T-040), K-32 v1 (T-041), with v1.4's own (K-01 to K-05, K-07, K-08, K-14, K-16, K-17, K-22 to K-29, K-34 to K-37) and v1.8's K-02 v1.2.
- **Not here:** QA's contract test `tool-api.v1.9.test.ts` (T-121); journey 7's K-21 screens (T-064); building the calls (the action layer's journey 7 tasks and the fee-door tasks T-886, T-887).

### Fixtures, v1.9 (in `fixtures/index.json` with version v1.9)

30 accepted and 20 refused, each refused for its one fault (with that fault fixed it is accepted: checked 2026-11-09, and round 2's on 2026-11-13). Every accepted fixture names the rule `journeySevenGivesNone`. Round 1's 45 were listed as v1.5; round 2 lists them as v1.9 and renumbers the defaults and open gaps their notes and entries cite, nothing else in them changing but `j7-death-report-turn.json`'s named booking.

- **The reminder and the customer's own changes:** `j7-reminder-job.json` (A7-01), `j7-move-offer-turn.json` and `j7-move-pick-turn.json` (A7-03, a free move), `j7-late-move-pick-turn.json` (A7-03 after free cancel: the owner asked), `j7-cancel-ask-turn.json` and `j7-cancel-confirm-turn.json` (A7-05), `j7-late-cancel-confirm-turn.json` (A7-06, the fee door walked to S11-25), `j7-whats-booked-yes-turn.json` (A7-31), `j7-running-late-turn.json` (A7-70), `j7-plan-price-keep-turn.json` (A7-55), and from round 2 `j7-final-count-turn.json` (A7-89's count, read back) and `j7-final-count-yes-turn.json` (A7-89's yes, the new count's line bound to its consent).
- **The fee door:** `j7-fee-owner-charge-turn.json` (S11-25 then S11-26: charging, bound), `j7-fee-charge-paid-job.json` (charged), `j7-fee-charge-declined-job.json` (one pay link), `j7-fee-no-fee-page.json` (S11-27 from Needs you), `j7-fee-never-job.json` (S11-35), `j7-fee-nothing-taken-job.json` (S11-24).
- **The business can't give it:** `j7-pro-out-turn.json` (A7-15), `j7-stand-in-take-turn.json` (A7-17 then A7-18), `j7-pro-late-turn.json` (A7-14), `j7-owner-move-yes-turn.json` (A7-23), `j7-business-call-off-turn.json` (A7-66), `j7-class-count-job.json` (A7-68), `j7-first-steps-check-job.json` (A7-74).
- **Questions, notes, contacts, someone else:** `j7-relay-question-turn.json` (A7-12), `j7-updates-switch-turn.json` (A7-98), `j7-note-send-turn.json` (A7-83), `j7-someone-else-asks-turn.json` (A7-80, naming no booking), `j7-death-report-turn.json` (A7-81; from round 2 its stage names the booking decide's choice names, `booking-alex-haircut`, never echoed).
- **Refused:** without the customer's own yes or its consent: `invalid/refuse-j7-cancel-without-yes.json`, `invalid/refuse-j7-cancel-no-consent.json`, `invalid/refuse-j7-stand-in-without-yes.json`, `invalid/refuse-j7-plan-price-no-consent.json` and, by the rule, `invalid/refuse-j7-change-not-bound.json`, and from round 2 `invalid/refuse-j7-final-count-yes-no-consent.json` (a final count's yes with no consent) and `invalid/refuse-j7-final-count-not-integer.json` (a count of 10.5); money: `invalid/refuse-j7-late-cancel-charged.json` (the fee charged on the customer's text), `invalid/refuse-j7-fee-charge-unbound.json` (a fee charge with no bound yes), `invalid/refuse-j7-fee-on-silence.json` (silence as the owner's answer), `invalid/refuse-j7-fee-answer-from-agent.json` (an AI answering for the owner), `invalid/refuse-j7-fee-paid-from-page.json` (a page saying it is paid), `invalid/refuse-j7-fee-pay-link-url.json` (the pay link's url for the caller); who may act and what an answer shows: `invalid/refuse-j7-move-pick-from-job.json` (a move on nobody's word), `invalid/refuse-j7-updates-switch-from-page.json` (updates moved outside the customer's own chat), `invalid/refuse-j7-someone-else-shown-booking.json` (a booking id to someone else), `invalid/refuse-j7-reminder-with-code.json` (the check-in code in an answer); and, by the rule, `invalid/refuse-j7-stated-key.json` (a key stated for journey 7) and `invalid/refuse-j7-answers-other-stage.json` (a pick answered as an offer), and from round 2 `invalid/refuse-j7-death-other-business-booking.json` (a death report to Lee's Barbershop naming `booking-alex-workshop`, the yoga studio's booking).

Names and values come from the master's sample cast and fixture set v1's demo shops (Alex M., Sam, Riley, Avery, Ben T., Owen H.; Lee's Barbershop's $35 haircut with its $20 fee, Sat Oct 24 at 11:00am with free cancel until Fri 11am, as K-30's seed-booking-flow-a holds it; the beard trim's $20; the Sat Nov 14 9:00am class needing 4; Owen's plan from $35 to $40 a visit from Wed Nov 25; A7-01's to A7-98's and S11-22's to S11-35's own sample times and lines; October and November 2026), never a real person (rule 9). Ids reuse K-30's, K-04's and K-29's seeds where the rows are theirs (`booking-alex-haircut`, `visit-alex-haircut-1`, `rsv-alex-haircut`, `res-riley`, `line-alex-haircut-1`, `consent-alex-ticked-terms-haircut`); every other id (Ben's and Owen's rows, the fee, payments, pay link, changes, consents written here, questions, outbox and clock rows, effects, the class session and Alex's Oct 30 booking) is an example, not from the sources (v1.3 default 48's rule), and so are the email `alex.m@email.example`, the times of day the steps do not print, and Alex's card saved for the fee (`card-alex-haircut-fee`).

### Defaults used (T-054)

146. **New, journey 7 writes no key** (02-contracts K-09's row v1.5, "Keys written: none"; section 8; A-J7.md, B-J7.md, A-S11.md, A-keys.md, B-keys.md): every v1.9 call answers `gives` empty; in a turn a journey 7 part gives nothing (`partResult.gives` empty); `journeySevenGivesNone` checks every journey 7 fixture; the keys journey 7 reads are written by their own calls (booked and code by v1.4's `writeBooking`).
147. **New, the customer's own yes, bound** (the lead's note (a); T-052 round 3, DL-257; v1.3 default 109; RULES 6.7, 'Each yes is kept with the words shown'; K-29 v1 consents; arch 09 line 457, 'Consent: bound to read-back'): every journey 7 answer by which the customer commits (a new time picked, a cancel's second yes, a late cancel's 'Call it off anyway', a stand-in, another time or a free cancel after the business changed the plan, the owner's move taken or refused, a later start moved or called off, a change of what's booked, a plan's skip, pause, end, stop or new price, a final count, another class or money back, the switch of where updates go) carries `yes` (v1.2's readBackYes: in their own chat the open question and the reply's event, whose stored words are the words shown; on their own secure page the tap with the words the page server showed), and its commit writes it as one K-29 consent with those exact words and their sha256: kind ticked_terms naming the booking ('the exact words shown when the yes was given (the read-back, the card or the terms ticked)'), plan_terms for a plan's terms (K-32 memberships), texts for a new contact's yes (A7-98; RULES 9.1). The answer names it (`consentId`), and each booking_changes row the customer asked for names it as approved_by (K-08 consentRow), which the schema and `journeySevenGivesNone` hold. A 'keep' or a 'no thanks' commits nothing and writes none; a question asked writes none. The owner's commits carry his own yes in K-08's ownerYes shape; a deadline's call-off carries none (K-30 approved_by null: 'Silence never moves anyone' but to a call-off, S13-56). This is stricter than v1.4 default 117's 'a time change at the same price and terms keeps the money step's yes': under the lead's note every customer commit of journey 7 writes its own.
148. **New, how each row reaches its reader** (v1.4 defaults 112, 123): as v1.4's messageWritten, with one more reader, other (A7-80's asker, A7-81's teller), answered in their own chat in the same turn and needing no person row. Send classes: quick_reply for the reply to the reader's own text (Q-15); visit_now for rows about a visit today (A7-14's later start, A7-70's word to the pro, A7-17's and A7-23's offers for a visit today: 'A visit today: sent at once, any hour'; B7-08's job sent now); routine for the rest; safety lines are v1's `recordSafetyOutcome`'s. Each row names the step whose line it carries; where a step prints no line for a reader, its own words for that reader are the master text (v1.4 default 123's way): the stand-in's ask (default 154), a late move's question to the owner (A6-02's options, default 150), the count's read-back (default 161), the note's read-back (S11-102's options, default 165), S11-25's line for a late cancel (its no-show line with the reason 'cancelled late'), A7-05's notice to the owner after no reply ('Sam is told what was asked, and when'); open gap 133.
149. **New, the reminder** (A7-01, B7-01, B7-55; S13-35; A7-54; K-30 booking_visits.cutoff_at): the reminder job (C-054's sweep) calls `remindBeforeVisit` once per booked visit, at least 3 hours before free cancel ends and inside the customer's text hours (9am to 8pm), or with no cutoff at 10am the day before; never after free cancel ends as the first notice of the cutoff. None goes (and the answer says why) for a booking made after that hour ('its confirmation carried these facts'), a cancelled or paused booking, one with a death report, a job sent now (B7-55), or while a crisis is open (A7-87). A plan charge due that day or the next rides as one line (A7-54). Each later day gets its own reminder with its own code (A7-01). The check-in code goes only to its person through the slot filler (K-03 visit.code; v1.4 default 119), never in an answer; flow B's reminder carries none (B7-01; the code goes on the way, B8-02). Prep that names a medicine, the body or the service is a link, never in the text (open gap 137).
150. **New, a move** (A7-03, B7-07, A7-19, B7-65; K-30 rule 6; K-05 v1; v1.4 defaults 113, 122): only the booked person's open times (C-098; 'Only Riley's times are offered'), only times that keep the plug-in's gap, a pair moving together, at the line's copied price (v1.4 default 122: fails closed; open gap 110), as one question whose times are choices by option id. A pick before free cancel ends, or where the business changed the plan, moves it at once in one commit: the new time reserved and the old released, booking_visits.reservation_id moved and, for a free move, cutoff_at with it ('A move before free cancel ends is free'); 'The code stays the same: it's still the same visit'; money taken moves with the booking; a form owed moves with its due time. After free cancel ends it is a request to the owner: K-30 owner_oks about late_move, 'accept, another time, or decline. Silence keeps the booking as it is', its question with A6-02's own options ('1 Accept, 2 Another time, 3 Decline': A7-03 prints none), its deadline as v1.4 default 113's (4 of the owner's text hours, the plug-in's shorter one where set, always before the visit); 'No free cancel' shows before the customer's yes, and an accepted late move keeps the old free-cancel time ('Nothing paid becomes refundable by a late move'). The time is saved only as K-05 v1 allows (v1.4 open gap 109). B7-17's earlier time is the one offer of another pro's time, at the same price, beside a keep (default 172).
151. **New, a cancel** (A7-05, A7-06, B7-08, B7-09, B7-10; S13-55; RULES 3.10; K-32 refunds): in time ('before free cancel ends, or any time when the booking can't keep a fee'; a fee-free customer or a reason on the owner's fee-free list too) Rosie asks once more, two linked visits 'Cancel both?' first and several bookings v1.1's `checkBookingFirst` first; late, the fee warning. The second yes cancels: in time everything paid comes back as the booking took it (refunds cancel_in_time; one visit of a series, its share only), a card nothing needs is removed (S13-55), and a prepaid plan session gets a make-up credit (open gap 138); late, nothing is charged ('Never: charge on this text'): the fee door is walked in the same commit (default 152), and the customer hears by when the owner decides. The owner sees the cancel on the Today page, with no text. B7-08's job sent now is in time until the pro texts on my way, its clock 5 minutes, visit_now (B7-08's vary).
152. **New, the fee door** (S11-22 to S11-28, S11-35; C-106; K-32 booking_fees, payments, refunds, credits; RULES 3.3 to 3.5; QUESTIONS w2-m678 question 1): open runs only once a welfare check, where one applies, found them safe (`welfareFirst`; S13-10), and after a stop or a bare CANCEL only once a person reached them (`waitsForPerson`; judgment rule 24). Never (S11-35: a fee-free customer or reason, a time set on silence, sensitive work unless the owner set its own fee, a visit the business couldn't give or the owner couldn't answer for; 'Rosie matches a reason to Sam's list. It never judges one'): the owner told why in one line, anything paid back (fee_rest), a credit given back. Nothing taken (S11-24): the owner and the customer told, nothing charged. Else S11-25: one booking_fees row asked, its kind, the amount the customer agreed to (the booking's one fee, the largest of its lines' fees, never more than what was paid now: S13-55), what it comes from (a payment, the card saved for fees, or a pack credit; on a credit visit only the credit, never a card), the owner's question naming the reason, the amount, what it comes from, the deadline and what silence does ('1 Charge or keep it, 2 No fee'; two fees waiting: 'Which one?' first, by letter, S13-12), its deadline 24 hours after asking and one reminder's clock row in his text hours. Charge (S11-26): out of a payment the fee is kept and the rest refunded (fee_rest); on the card a K-32 payment of purpose fee, pending, with one stripe_charge, never with the customer present; a credit used and not given back, the customer told how many are left; a card the network replaced is not charged (open gap 140). No fee (S11-27): dropped, anything paid back, a credit given back. Silence (S11-28): the reminder, then at 24 hours no fee (dropped, no_answer) and the customer told, a signed schedule's share of money already taken kept (S13-55), never a new charge, by the question's clock rows, never a call. A decline: never retried; one pay link (a new fee payment with its stripe_pay_link) only to the payer's own ways, never an AI, the owner or a pro, the owner told; unpaid by its deadline its clock row closes it and the fee is dropped (K-32 closePayLink).
153. **New, the fee's bound yes** (the lead's note (a); K-32 v1 rule 10; v1.4 default 127; S11-22, 'Never: ask Sam about a fee the customer never agreed to'; S11-25, 'Ben agreed to the shop's no-show fee when booking'): every fee payment, the charge and its one pay link (`feeChargeBound`), names as its effect.created row's approved_by (K-08 consentRow) the K-29 consent of the words the customer agreed to the fee with, the booking's terms snapshot's (K-30 booking_terms.terms_consent_id: 'then a $20 fee may apply'); for a late cancel the cancel's own consent ('Call it off anyway', or a late skip's yes: default 147) is named on the open request (`cancelYesConsentId`, required there for reason late_cancel and null for every other reason from round 2: security note N1) and kept as the cancel change's approved_by (K-30 booking_changes, written in the cancel's own commit), since no K-32 v1 booking_fees column holds it; so C-018's hourly check finds the customer's yes by either road. The owner's yes is his own answer in booking_fees (K-08 ownerYes), never the customer's. A booking whose terms consent holds no fee words has no fee that may be asked (S11-35).
154. **New, the business can't give it** (A7-93, B7-90; S13-56; A7-15 to A7-21; B7-19 to B7-22, B7-65): the pro's day blocked for the visit (C-098); the owner asked only where someone with the skill and any license the business needs could stand in, never where the booking named a person, a child's too, or someone in care ('Sam calls them, never a stand-in'), and an apprentice only with a supervisor working; with nobody who could, the owner told with no question and the customer offered new times with the same pro or a free cancel. The stand-in is asked first and offered only on their own yes ('Avery only once Avery says yes'); A7-15 prints no line for that ask, so its words are the step's own ('Can you come in early Sat Oct 24 for Alex M. at 11:00am? / Reply 1 Yes, 2 No'; open gap 133). Every choice is free and the customer's own free cancel is kept; 'Never say why a pro is out'; the time stays saved until they answer. Deadlines: by 7pm the day before (2 hours before for a visit today) the owner is asked to reach them their way; 2 hours before, after every way they picked was tried, the visit is called off with no fee and everything paid for it back (a clock row's commit: booking_changes cancelled, asked_by rosie, approved_by null; refunds called_off). 'The other visits, and the money for them, are never kept or refunded for one miss.'
155. **New, the pro's LATE** (A7-14, B7-18; K-31 late_reports, visit_events; any-time row 16): one row, fanned out by code to the worker's later visits that day, each customer told their own new start and nothing about another's visit (the lead's note (c)); B7-18's LATE comes by text (Q-05: no voice); no reply: the later start stands. 'A start the business set or moved, kept on silence, carries no no-show fee unless a person reached them first': journey 8's fee door reads it (`feeDoor` open's setOnSilence).
156. **New, the owner moves it or calls it off** (A7-23, B7-28, A7-66, B7-43, S12-33; S13-56; S11-12): the command is read back first; MOVE's time and person re-parsed by code from the owner's words under limit 6 (v1.4 default 116's way); CALL OFF only ('A bare CANCEL from any role is a stop to texts, never this'). A closed day tells everyone booked that day; a tool or room out only the bookings that need it; weather sends nobody into it. Free cancel stays the later of the old and new times; money paid ahead for a smaller service is lowered to match (price_lowered). A call-off: no fee, a full refund (called_off), a class or a group everyone on that session, a prepaid plan session a make-up credit (open gap 138), and the reason never said unless the owner writes it. The customer's deadline is default 154's. The customer's own call-off of A7-23's question is `ownerChangesIt` customerAnswer callOff: no fee and everything back; in flow B it is a step of its own, B7-29, 'Cancels; gives back any money' (B-J7.md line 23), the answer's step (round 2, QA's FAIL 2).
157. **New, too few in a class** (A7-68, A7-69, B7-62, B7-63; K-27 sessions): counted at the cutoff only where the plug-in or the owner set a minimum to run; the owner alone decides; no answer in 1 hour: it runs and nobody is told anything new; 'Never: run a class over that limit on silence'. A call-off cancels every booking on the session by the business, each customer offered another session with anything paid kept, or everything back; A7-69's reply time is the plug-in's, and with none, the step's own (the evening of the next day: 'No reply by Sun 6pm'); no reply: the refund goes out.
158. **New, what's booked** (A7-31, B7-31; RULES 3.8; K-30 booking_lines): the person's day checked for the time; the price first, with every term that matters before the yes; a cheaper service before free cancel ends lowers money paid ahead to match (price_lowered); 'Nothing paid becomes refundable by a late change'; a credit pays an added service only where the plan allows; a new or higher amount needs the customer's own yes in their own chat. A price made just for them (the owner's price OKs first) and another place are not offered by v1.9 (`notOffered`; open gap 134). 'Never: move or add on a maybe.'
159. **New, running late** (A7-70, B7-27): the owner's one rule (a grace time, then a shorter visit or a late cancel) is in the plug-in's pf.rules text, which no frozen field types (open gap 136); with the owner silent, 'a shorter visit in the time left, at the same price, or a later time'. The price stays; the pro is told; not there by the end of the grace time is journey 8's no-show; 'Never: a fee on silence for being late'.
160. **New, plans** (A7-32, B7-32, A7-54, B7-52, A7-91, B7-86, A7-55, B7-51; S13-39; RULES 3.9; K-32 memberships, plan_charges): skip, pause or end in the customer's own chat or page; a skip or a pause before free cancel ends costs nothing, later it is a late cancel through the fee door; ending refunds unused prepaid visits by the contract, and visits already booked stay unless they cancel them. The charge notice goes the day before, alone where no reminder carries it ('No buttons here'). The renewal reminder goes 30 days ahead (15 to 45 by the plug-in; once a year for a running plan): 'No reminder sent: no charge'; 'A free trial never turns paid without a new yes'. A new price applies only on the customer's own yes in their own chat, written as its plan_terms consent; K-32 v1 writes a plan's price once, so until a K-32 minor version nothing is charged at the new price (open gap 138: fails closed). A renewal's '2 Don't renew' is the customer's own stop, written as its consent and named in the answer (round 2, security note N2); a price's '2 No thanks' commits nothing, the date's clock row ending the plan.
161. **New, the final count** (A7-89, B7-77): one reminder before the due date; the count typed (decide's field final_count, K-01 answerField type integer) and read back with the rest repriced by the contract's price per person; A7-89 prints no read-back, so its words are the step's own ('Final count · 10 people · $25 each / Reply 1 Yes, 2 No'; open gap 133); a count over the owner's or the room's limit goes to the owner before any yes (`toOwner`; open gap 134); the new count's line replaces the old, bound to the consent; no count by the due date: the contract's count stands; a lower count lowers the price only where the contract says so; 'Never: offer to move the date for a late count'.
162. **New, first steps** (A7-74, B7-61): one reminder before the due time, inside text hours, never after it; papers to upload a checklist on the secure page; done in time: the visit stands; not done or not OK: later times or a call-off offered (A7-74's second line), the owner told with no question; the call-off is A7-05's in-time cancel, everything paid for it back; 'Never: put a result in a text'; a document to OK: nothing ordered or made until the customer OKs it.
163. **New, questions and a worse problem** (A7-12, B7-16, A7-13, B7-17; judgment rule 58): A7-12's routes, each a result of `relayQuestion` ask: answered from the booking; relayed with quick answers; waits for the morning (the owner texted at 8am); the shop's line for a question only a pro may answer; 'We can't advise on legal questions; a lawyer can help.'; urgent words ring the on-call person at once, any hour; a report that someone at the business harmed a person to Rosie's team that day, never to the one named (v1's `handOff`); abuse aimed at the pro never passed on. Words holding something private reach the pro as 'Read it on the secure page'; staff never quote prices by text; no answer in 10 minutes goes to the owner. A7-13: the danger words first (the safety read); an update about a body or health only on the secure page; the reviewer the plug-in names a private flag the same day, never the words; the pro's '2 Better to move it' offers new times with no fee. B7-17, 'Checks for danger again', is flow B's twin; its own question to the customer, the earlier time and the keep, is default 172's.
164. **New, where updates go** (A7-98, B7-88; D75, D100; S13-45 row 3; MC C9; RULES 9.1; the lead's note (c)): only in the customer's own chat (a turn); asked from any other number, email or chat, nothing changes and the reply says how in the same words whether or not a booking exists (`notOwnChat`; A3-43); the code only to the new contact (v1's `sendProofCode` newContact: a mobile that has not texted Rosie is asked to text from it, an email gets it by email), never the old; 'Lost the phone on file?' the code goes to the email on file and is typed back in the new chat (D100: v1's emailOnFile route); words that also say stop stop texts to this number at once (v1's `recordStopOrStart`), and this chat's one reply still goes. The switch: the contact on file moved (K-28), the old contact's consent withdrawn and a texts yes for the new one (K-29), every secure link sent before ended (K-30), the confirmation to the new contact, the owner and the pro told; the new contact is never read back in full ('al***@email.com'). Staff updating it at the desk is K-21's (open gap 135).
165. **New, a note** (A7-83, B7-79): the owner's NOTE is checked: a private detail goes on the secure page and the text says only that a note is waiting there; a part known not to be true is dropped; a change to money, the time or the person goes back to the owner (`backToOwner`), then needs the customer's own yes through that change's own call; links are checked. A7-83 prints no read-back, so it takes S11-102's options, '1 Send it, 2 Change it' (MASTER-FLOW-A line 5706): 'Safe default: send nothing until Sam confirms the words'. Sent as written, in the business's name; no ads or offers; the reply goes to the owner.
166. **New, someone else, and a death** (A7-80, B7-72, A7-81, B7-73; any-time rows 8 and 5; the lead's note (c), T-061 round 1, DL-296): a caller who is not the booking's person proves nothing, so the answer is the one reply, the same words every time, true or not, naming no booking, person, time, row or count: `someoneElseAsks.response` holds only its stage, step, reply, gives and repeat flag (`refuse-j7-someone-else-shown-booking.json`). The owner is told in the same commit only where the request names a booking of this business, the one decide's choice names (default 171) ('Never confirm or deny it to them, even on the call back'), every link of it held; a court or police request goes to the owner, one about the owner or staff to Rosie's team only; in danger, 911 at once and still nothing about the booking. A death: on anyone's word the named booking's deceased flag (K-04 bookings.deceased, as v1's `setRecordFlag`), reminders and sales texts stop, nothing new is charged, a bill for work given waits for the owner, the owner told; the owner's own death goes to Rosie's team. Every journey 7 read-back is the same words for every number: no answer to a caller whose identity is not proven says whether a number is stopped or holds a time or an end time, and no journey 7 call answers another mobile's holds (v1.2's savedTimeCap is not used).
167. **Q-01 and Q-30, stop, crisis and threat** (A7-11, B7-15, A7-87, B7-70, A7-88, B7-71; MC C1; RULES 7.2 to 7.6; S11-62): A7-11's 'Texts from Lee's Barbershop are off' is Q-01's default in C1's meaning: Rosie's texts to this number are off for every business, the visit is still booked, and how to cancel it ('reply START, or call the business': its own listed phone, C1); the opt-out line goes as Q-30 places it. A bare 'Cancel' also flags the visit for the owner (C-051), whose fee waits until a person reaches them (`waitsForPerson`). These are v1's `recordStopOrStart`. Crisis words: v1's `recordSafetyOutcome`, 988 and 911 at once, any hour, in their language, the owner flagged 'Urgent: open the secure page now', never the words; no fee, reminder or rating while it's open. A threat: v1's `recordSafetyOutcome` and v1.1's `setCustomerBlock` (S11-62): the people at risk told, visits with that person paused until the owner decides ('1 Keep it paused, 2 Call off, no fee, 3 Go ahead'), its deadline's call-off with no fee; '2 Call off, no fee' is `ownerChangesIt` callOffYes (A7-88).
168. **New, the choices of journey 7** (the lead's note (b); T-053 round 2, DL-275; K-02 v1 optionOffered, choice.branch; v1.4 defaults 114, 116; v1.2 default 86): the table "The choices decide picks in journey 7's turns" names each question's choices in full: its id (the option's id the open question stores), its label and branch, its definition, its fields and its stakes, and the walk code takes; the entries paragraph names each entry's fields. A question the bundle holds no split for keeps option choices until the K-01 minor version of open gap 133 gives it one. From round 2 the table also names B7-17's own question to the customer, the earlier time and the keep, each with its id, label, definition and walk (default 172).
169. **New, every lapse is a clock, never a call** (v1.4 default 121's rule; C-054, C-055; NQ-RT-4; arch 02 line 127): each silence journey 7 and the fee door name is a question's clock or a clock row armed in the same commit, firing through `gateAndCommit` (input.kind clock): A7-03's 24 hours and a late move's deadline (the time stays), A7-05's one more ask then the owner told, A7-06's no reply (the visit stays), A7-15's 30 minutes (the customer offered), the 7pm-the-day-before and 2-hours-before deadlines of A7-17, A7-19, A7-21, A7-23 and A7-74 (the owner asked to reach them; then called off, no fee, everything back), A7-14's no reply (the later start), A7-70's start (the shorter visit), A7-68's 1 hour (it runs), A7-69's reply time (the refund), A7-12's 10 minutes (to the owner), A7-55's date (the old price, then the plan ends), A7-91's date (it renews only as its terms say, and only if the reminder reached them), A7-89's due date (the contract's count), S11-25's reminder and 24 hours (S11-28), the fee pay link's deadline, and S11-62's pause deadline. No clock runs on a message never delivered (MC C2, C9).
170. **New, the customer's own AI on journey 7** (K-20 v1; MC C3; S13-55, 'Never a yes relayed in a chat by someone else's AI'): v1.9's customer stages take the customer's own chat or own secure page only (`journeySevenCustomerSide`), not an agent caller, so a bound own AI asking to move, cancel or change is told to use the customer's chat or the booking's page until K-20 and K-09 minor versions give the agent route its binding (open gap 135). An AI never answers for the owner, the pro or a stand-in.
171. **New (round 2), the booking someone else's words point to** (DL-308 ruling 1, the action-layer engineer's FAIL; A7-80, A7-81; arch 03 lines 175, 183, 185; arch 01 lines 25, 29): `someoneElseAsks`'s two stages take an optional `bookingId` (`someoneElseNamedBooking`), the booking the sender's words point to. **Who fills it:** decide, from its choice: the target booking (K-02 v1 `choice.target.bookingId`) of the any-time row it entered, row 8 (third party or privacy) for asks and row 5 (death) for death, which the walk copies into the request (C-087); never code reading the words (the ruling's rule 4: 'The AI decides inside choices code computes' and 'Route by state, not by text', arch 01 lines 25 and 29; code confirms decide's quote and re-parses values from it, arch 03 line 185, but picks no booking from free words). It is null, or absent, when the choice names none. **The person:** the sources need no field for one: the deceased flag is the booking's (arch 03 line 175, 'Deceased (booking)'; K-04 bookings.deceased), A7-81 means 'a death of the person served, or of a child or pet the booking is about' (MASTER-FLOW-A line 4057), whom the booking names, and A7-80's notice to the owner names no person (line 4048). **On the server:** the action layer reads the booking under the caller's business (row-level security, arch 10 line 546): for asks, the owner is told and that booking's links held (A7-80 lines 4040, 4041); for death, its deceased flag is set and the owner told (A7-81 line 4056); a booking of another business, or none, is no booking, so nothing is written about it, and the reply is the same words in every case (default 166). **Never echoed:** `someoneElseAsks.response` has no field for it, as before. A valid request names only a booking of the caller's business, which `journeySevenGivesNone` checks against a fixture's seeds (`refuse-j7-death-other-business-booking.json`); the schema cannot. Which bookings the frontier offers these rows as targets when the sender holds none is open gap 141.
172. **New (round 2), B7-17's question to the customer** (DL-308 ruling 2, the AI engineer's FAIL; MASTER-FLOW-B lines 3919 to 3934; MASTER-FLOW-A lines 3909 to 3924): after a worse problem the pro is asked A7-13's question, '1 Yes, come in', '2 Better to move it', '3 Call Alex' (MASTER-FLOW-A line 3922; flow B prints no line to the pro), whose Means 1 is 'come in, at the booked time or sooner' (line 3919). Where an earlier open time keeps the gap, 'Casey can come sooner, so that's offered' (MASTER-FLOW-B line 3922): the pro's comeIn answers `offerSooner` (`relayQuestion.response`, next offerTimes), and the walk goes on to `moveBooking` offer at step B7-17, cause business, whose one question is B7-17's line (line 3933): 'Casey can come sooner, at the same price. / A) Wed Oct 21 · arriving 2–4pm · Casey / Reply A. / Reply B Keep Thursday.', the earlier time's price shown first ('An earlier time shows its price first', line 3927; its money word read from the booking's line). Its choices (the choices table): the earlier time, an option choice whose option id the pick carries, and 'B Keep Thursday', an option choice whose walk is nothing. **The customer's reply:** 'A' is `moveBooking` pick at B7-17 with their own yes: moved at once, free, at the same price, the change bound to its consent, 'Jordan and Casey are told' (line 3927); 'B Keep Thursday', like no reply, is no call of journey 7: the turn's `gateAndCommit` answers the open question and nothing else is written ('No reply: the visit stays as booked', line 3927; 'Safe default: the visit stays as booked', line 3931). The earlier time may be another pro's, as the line prints, the one exception to default 150's 'Only Riley's times are offered'. **No 'come in sooner' answer:** `worseAnswer` keeps comeIn, move and callCustomer, since the sources print the pro's options as A7-13's three (line 3922), 'come in sooner' (line 3913; MASTER-FLOW-B line 3923) being what Means 1 covers, not a fourth printed option, and flow B prints no pro line; a sooner time is comeIn's walk, never a value the pro taps.
173. **New (round 2), the fee door reads its facts from the record** (security notes N3 and the action-layer engineer's note 2; RULES 3.5, no fee 'even if the owner asks'; S11-22; judgment rule 24): `feeDoor` open's `shownCutoffAt`, `setOnSilence`, `welfareFoundSafeAt` and `personReachedAt` are the sending step's word (S11-22) and hints only: C-106 reads each from the record (K-30 booking_visits and the terms snapshot, the changes approved by safeDefault, K-31's late rows, the welfare check and the person's reach mark) and decides by the record's; and at ownerAnswer it checks `waitsForPerson` and `welfareFirst` again, so an owner's charge after a STOP that nobody followed up is refused, as at open. The schema cannot see the record, so this is a README rule; QA's T-121 adds an exchange of an owner's answer after a STOP (N3).

### Open gaps (v1.9)

For QUESTIONS.md, each with its default where one is in use:

131. **K-08 kinds for journey 7's writes** (CQ-56): a move, a cancel, a stand-in, a change of what's booked, a fee asked, charged or dropped, a late report, a plan change, a contact switch, a note, a death flag; CQ-56's default applies.
132. **The K-05 reads journey 7 needs** (CQ-44 restated; K-05 open gap 11): a booking's visits with their cutoff and reservation, the worker's open times that keep the gap, the lines' fees, what the booking took (payments, saved cards, credits), the terms consent, the fee row, the owner OKs, the late reports, a session's count, a membership and its charges, the customer's handles; and K-05's commit with no turn for the pages and jobs. A K-05 minor version adds them before the action layer's journey 7 tasks; until then those tasks wait (NEED), with no default read.
133. **Words and splits the steps do not print** (defaults 148, 154, 161, 165, 168; v1.4 open gap 107's class): the stand-in's ask (A7-15), a late move's question to the owner (A7-03), the count's read-back (A7-89), the note's read-back (A7-83), S11-25's line for a late cancel, A7-05's notice after no reply, and splits for A7-01's, A7-31's, A7-70's, A7-32's, A7-14's, A7-19's, A7-21's, A7-69's, A7-91's, A7-55's, A7-74's, A7-12's, A7-13's and A7-88's questions. Default: the step's own words and option choices, as named. Proposed: the K-01 minor version of v1.4 open gap 107, through the compiler or an overlay Jay approves.
134. **A price made just for them, another place, and a count over a limit** (defaults 158, 161): K-30 v1 owner_oks.about has no value for a changed line's price or a count over a limit, and another place may move the visit to the other flow. Default: not offered (`notOffered`), or handed to the owner with nothing written (`toOwner`). Proposed: a K-30 minor version and the K-09 minor version that takes it.
135. **The customer's own AI, and the staff desk, on journey 7** (defaults 164, 170): K-20's agent route for moves, cancels and plan changes, and staff updating a contact at the desk with the same code. Proposed: K-20 and K-09 minor versions, and K-21's journey 7 version (T-064) for the desk.
136. **The owner's one rule for running late** (default 159): the plug-in's pf.rules text holds it, and no frozen field types the grace time. Default: the owner silent. Proposed: a K-26 minor version.
137. **The reminder's prep link** (default 149): A7-01's 'It's a link, How to get ready' has no K-03 v1 slot (v1.2 open gap 70's class). Default: no prep link until a K-03 minor version adds one.
138. **K-32 for a plan's new price and a make-up credit** (defaults 151, 156, 160): K-32 v1 writes a plan's price once, and a make-up credit's deadline has no column. Default: nothing charged at a new price; a make-up credit is a credit given back with no deadline. Proposed: a K-32 minor version.
139. **Journey 8's fee callers** (default 152): A8-16, A8-56, B8-12 and B8-60 open the fee door (`feeDoor` open's `from`), but their own steps are journey 8's freeze's (the plan's v1.6 row, which now takes a number above v1.9: open gap 142). Default: K-31's no-show clock (a job) or a turn's walk calls `feeDoor` open, as `j7-fee-never-job.json` shows; journey 8's freeze adds their own calls.
140. **A replaced card at the fee's charge** (S11-26, 'A replaced card: nothing goes on the new card without asking the customer first'; K-32 saved_cards replaced; K-14 open gap 13): the fee door has no stage to ask the customer. Default: a fee whose saved card was replaced is not charged; the owner is told and may send one pay link, as S11-24 allows. Proposed: the K-09 minor version that answers K-14 open gap 13.
141. **The bookings a stranger's words can name** (round 2; default 171; arch 02 line 115, 'No target: the doors'; arch 03 lines 159, 167): `someoneElseAsks` takes the booking only from decide's choice, whose target the frontier sets (K-02 v1 choice.target), but nothing says which bookings the frontier offers the third-party and death rows as targets when the sender holds none of their own (a family member texting from a number the record does not hold, as A7-81's sample does). Default in use here: K-09 takes only what decide's choice names, read under the caller's business; with none, nothing is written about a booking, the owner is not told of one and no flag is set, and the reply is the same (fail closed for the record; A7-81's flag then waits for the owner, whom a person tells). Proposed for QUESTIONS.md: a K-02 and C-075 rule for those rows' targets (for example this business's bookings before their visit, labelled by the person served and the visit's day, as arch 03 line 159 labels other bookings, collapsed past 60), decided before journey 7's someoneElseAsks task.
142. **The plan's K-09 rows after v1.4 and their numbers** (DL-322, DL-355, DL-471): 02-contracts K-09's version table plans journey 7 as v1.5, journey 8 as v1.6 and on to journey 11's other calls as v1.9, but v1.5, v1.6 and v1.8 went to the walking skeleton, compose input and gateAndCommit's end, and journey 7 is this v1.9. Default: each later freeze takes the next free number above the newest frozen one, as this one did, and names the plan's row it freezes (this README's "Why" lines). Proposed: the lead updates 02-contracts' table or adds a note there (`ops/` is the lead's).
143. **S11-24's one pay link** (security note N4; S11-24, MASTER-FLOW-A line 5762; RULES 3.13): where nothing was taken the owner may send one pay link, but `feeDoor` has no stage for it, so none is sent (fails closed); and where the payer is not the customer, sending the link only to the payer rests on the slot filler (C-088), which reads the booking's payer. Default: no pay link from S11-24 until a K-09 minor version adds its stage; T-121 pins that no v1.9 answer at S11-24 holds a pay link. Proposed: that stage, with the payer named on the server, in the fee door's next version.

## v1.5 (T-1142): the walking skeleton's turn 1

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): the skeleton's fixtures spanned two turns (CQ-156 a, CQ-157). `compose-input-s1-02.json`, `finalize-pass.json`, T-563's send proof and D-017's human test are turn 1, Sam's first text (S1-01), whose reply is S1-02's question as `outbox-s1-02-sam-1`; `build-frontier-s1-02.json`, `commit-s1-02.json` and K-02's `context-s1-02.json` and `decide-s1-02-choose.json` are turn 2, Sam's answer, which walks to S1-11 and S1-04's question, steps bundle v0 does not hold. The lead's ruling (DL-322): the skeleton's end-to-end turn is turn 1, as D-017's card states ("text JOIN ... Rosie answers once, with the first sign-up question (S1-02 ...)"; "decide (two reads)"; "gate and one commit (state, the open question and its clock row, one outbox row ...)"); turn 2 leaves the skeleton for journey 1's lane. Turn 1 lacked a frontier and a commit fixture, and its frontier's one choice, a door to S1-01, is no door K-02 v1 holds. **Kind:** additive, a minor version. `tool-api.v1.5.schema.json` holds every v1.4 definition under its own name, the unchanged ones by `$ref` to `tool-api.v1.4.schema.json`, which stays as it is; the changed ones (`buildFrontier.response`, `closestEntries.request`, `closestEntries.response`, `gateAndCommit.request` and the exchanges `buildFrontier.exchange`, `closestEntries.exchange`, `gateAndCommit.exchange`) take only K-02 v1.1's context (`../model/model.v1.1.schema.json`), whose one change is the door value `signUp` (K-02 README "v1.1"). So every v1 to v1.4 exchange is a v1.5 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks every earlier fixture against v1.5). No call, key, error or named rule is added: S1-01 gives `thread`, a v1 key, and `commit-s1-01.json` names v1's `givesStepKeys`. Round 3 (DL-333) adds two optional parts, each new under its own name and each taking every earlier value: `gateAndCommit.turn`, the request's turn, K-05 v1's with the service and the chat id of the turn's last text (default 135), which `gateAndCommit.request` takes; and `gateAndCommit.given` with `statedWrites`, a fixture's statement of the rows its stated step writes (default 136), which `gateAndCommit.exchange` takes. **Scope:** S1-01 and S1-02 of flow A on bundle v0 (`contracts/bundle/fixtures/bundle-v0-skeleton.json`); the design record that names each skeleton hop's turn 1 fixture is ADR-013 (`docs/adr/0013-walking-skeleton-turn-one.md`).

### The turn

| Field            | Turn 1's value                                                                                             | Source                                                                                                                      |
| ---------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |

### Defaults (T-1142), numbered after T-053's

128. **Turn 1 reaches the commit through decide.** No keyword or door routes a new number's first text in code: JOIN, the text the sign-up link types (S1-01: "By plain text, the link types JOIN for Sam"), is none of K-01's keywords (`lists.keywords`: START, HELP and the 16 opt-out words), so the guard acts on nothing and lists the handle's targets (arch 02 line 115); with none, "No target: the doors"; the frontier offers the sign-up door (K-02 v1.1 default 9); decide's two Haiku reads enter it (D-017: "frontier from the Tool API → decide (two reads) → gate and one commit"); the gate passes it, and the walk after the choice (arch 03 line 191) writes S1-01's thread and stops at S1-02's question, the first step that asks someone. K-02 therefore changes (v1.1).
129. **Turn 1's frontier on bundle v0: one choice.** A door is offered only when the pinned bundle holds its entry step (K-01 README: "a step a bundle does not hold is not reachable"): of the doors, only S1-01's (the five of arch 03 line 165, A3-51, A3-43, S11-12, S11-42 and S11-21, are steps bundle v0 does not hold). No any-time row is offered: rows 1 to 3 are set by the safety read alone, only when it labels them (arch 03 line 173; T-294's card), and Sam's text reads No; rows 4 to 21 need a handler step the bundle holds (K-02 default 4; T-294's card), and bundle v0 holds none. K-08 v1's `trace-skeleton.json` counts the same one choice. Turn 2's `build-frontier-s1-02.json` (v1) offers S11-21's door and the danger row, which bundle v0 does not hold (open gap 118).
130. **The turn's business.** The turn runs under the business head test owner 1's phone is seeded for (K-21 v1 `test-roster.json` tp-01: `biz-lees-barbershop`; K-04 `seed-business-head.json`), as v1's `compose-input-s1-02.json` and `finalize-pass.json` name the caller, so the door's target and the rows' business are Lee's Barbershop, while Sam holds no role yet (K-05 v1.2 `targets-first-text.json`). A real new owner's first text before any business head is open gap 119.
131. **Row ids** (T-1139's NEED item 4; CQ-157 (4)). A row a turn's walk writes takes `<kind>-<step>-<turn key>-<n>`: kind `thread`, `oq` (an open question) or `outbox`; the writing step in lower case; `n` from 1 in the walk's order among the rows of that kind and step; the turn key the turn's own id, in production the hash of its texts' event ids (arch 01 line 59; CQ-09). So each id is unique per turn, the same when a turn runs again (a repeated turn runs writesOf again before K-05 answers repeated: STATE note 1), and free of the person's words. A fixture writes the turn key as a readable stand-in, the handle's sample name: `sam` for `turn-s1-01-sam`, as v1's `compose-input-s1-02.json` already names the row (`outbox-s1-02-sam-1`); a proof run on a fixture takes the turn key from this table, so the walk's ids equal the fixture's. The commit's own rows keep T-305's rules: a question's clock row `clock-<question id>`, an event-log entry `log-<row id>-<move>` (`actions/src/commit/build.ts`, default 2). Code builds an id from its parts and never parses it (CQ-09). Turn 1's: `thread-s1-01-sam-1`, `oq-s1-02-sam-1`, `clock-oq-s1-02-sam-1`, `outbox-s1-02-sam-1`.
132. **The outbox row's channel** (T-1139's NEED item 5; CQ-157 (5)). The service the turn's last text came in on (K-06 v1 event `service`: imessage, rcs or sms), as K-03's channel of the same name: a reply goes the person's way, iMessage first for texts (arch 02 line 125; RULES 4.8), and a number with no phone row yet has no stored way (K-04 `phones.chat_way`, Q-33; K-05 v1.2 `targets-first-text.json`: phone null), so its own text's service is the one source. The walk reads it on the call as the gateAndCommit request's `turn.service`, which the runtime caller copies from that text's K-06 event (default 135; round 3). SMS and RCS get text only (CQ-11). Turn 1's is `imessage` (K-06 `text-imessage.json`'s form; `compose-input-s1-02.json`; K-08 `trace-skeleton.json`'s dispatch span).
133. **The outbox row's send class** (T-1139's NEED item 5). `quick_reply`: RULES 4.3's classes (K-07 v1 `sendClass`; K-04 core default 7) and Q-15's default ("A quick reply is one in the same turn"): S1-02's question answers Sam's own text in the turn it came in, so it goes at any hour (T-562's proof: "the reply in the same turn goes at 11pm (Q-15)"). K-08 `trace-skeleton.json`'s dispatch span carries `quick_reply`.
134. **S1-02's question and its clock row.** S1-02 is asked with no options, the kinds typed ("What kinds of work do you do? Just type them.": `compose-input-s1-02.json` options and `finalize-pass.json` optionIds empty), so the K-05 open question's `options` is empty; its card's clock is step 9's default (`clock: {"from": "step9Default"}`), so its `clock` is null; its clock row is the commit's (T-305: kind question, waiting, `fires` S1-02 with its card's safe default), armed at the row's first delivery receipt (arch 02 line 127; T-564), due 8pm the day after delivery with no visit (arch 02 line 127, DD). The thread is S1-01's write (S1-01 gives thread: README "Each step of journeys 1 and 2"): one K-04 `threads` row for the handle, kind chat, no booking, Linq's chat id from the text's event, which the walk reads as the request's `turn.chatId` (default 135), no reply address. `written` lists the versioned rows the commit inserted (K-05 v1 `commitResult`: turn, outbox and event-log rows left out); the core action's rows are named in its response, as v1's fixtures name them. `commit-s1-01.json` states the thread, the question and the outbox row field for field (default 136).
135. **The turn's service and chat id** (round 3; DL-333 ruling 1; the action-layer engineer's round 2 FAIL: the request carried neither, and no data-access call returns the event row). The gateAndCommit request's turn is `gateAndCommit.turn`: K-05 v1's seven fields, each by reference, and two more, `service` and `chatId`, the K-06 v1 event's `service` and `chatId` of the turn's last text (the last of `eventIds`). **Source:** the runtime caller, the handle actor that runs the turn (C-046), copies both from that text's K-06 event, which it holds (T-553 seals each text into its inbox), on every turn of texts (input `decided` or `coreOnly`). Both or neither (`dependentRequired`); a clock's turn has no text and carries neither (`gateAndCommit.request` refuses them on a clock's firing). `service` is never null, since a text always names its service; `chatId` is null when Linq did not report it, and for email, whose thread is its reply address. **Reader:** the commit's walk: `service` is the channel of a reply's outbox row (default 132), `chatId` S1-01's thread's `linq_chat_id` (default 134). A request without them (every v1 to v1.4 request) gives the walk no source, so a part whose rows need one fails closed, never guessed. **Not the turn row:** K-04 `turns` has no such column and K-05 v1's `turn` is closed, so the commit writes the turn row with K-05's seven fields alone; T-305's `buildCommitRequest` copies the request's turn into commitTurn as it is (`actions/src/commit/build.ts`), so the task that moves the commit to v1.5 drops the two fields there. **Never the model's or a log's:** buildFrontier's request keeps K-05 v1's closed turn, which refuses both, so neither reaches the frontier or K-02's context (K-02 v1.1 holds neither; decide's reads are unchanged); the response's `written` names rows by table, id and version alone (K-05 v1 `commitResult`); a span or log line names the handle by its K-04 id and holds no chat id (K-08 v1); the thread's `thread.created` event-log entry names the row by its id with new values `{"kind": "chat"}`, never the chat id. The chat id's one home is K-04 `threads.linq_chat_id`. Turn 1's: `imessage` and `linq-chat-demo-sam`, a stand-in in K-06 `text-imessage.json`'s form (`linq-chat-demo-alex`, the chat id K-04 `seed-thread-messages.json` gives Alex's thread); a run carries its event's own.
136. **The rows' content in the fixture** (round 3; DL-333 ruling 2). `commit-s1-01.json` states the rows its part writes in `given.stated.writes`, so T-1139's proof reads their values from the fixture, not from this README. The form K-09 allows is a `given` block: `gateAndCommit.given` is v1.4's `given` whose stated step may also carry `writes` (`statedWrites`), optional, so every earlier given still passes; a fixture's `given` is never sent, so the response stays as v1.4 has it. A rows-written detail beside `written`'s table, row id and version would change K-05's `commitResult` and put the rows' values, the chat id among them, in the response the runtime reads and in its span. `statedWrites` holds three lists, each present (empty when the part writes none of its kind), each item in K-05 v1's own write shape, as the walk hands it to the commit (`actions/src/commit/types.ts` `UnitWrites`: `state`, `asks[].question`, `messages`) and the commit sends K-05 unchanged: `state` (K-05 v1 `stateWrite`: an insert names the table and its K-04 columns and values, as K-04's seed fixtures write them), `openQuestions` (`openQuestionNew`) and `outboxRows` (`outboxRowNew`, which has no plan field, so a stated row has no plan; the commit refuses one with a plan: T-305's `outboxRowWithPlan`). Left out, as the commit's own (T-305): the question's clock row (`clock-<question id>`, kind question, waiting, firing the step with the safe default its card names), the event-log entries and the turn row; the core action's rows are named in its response. Turn 1's: `state` holds S1-01's thread, an insert into `threads` of `id` `thread-s1-01-sam-1`, `handle_id` `handle-sam`, `kind` chat, `booking_id` null, `linq_chat_id` `linq-chat-demo-sam` (the turn's `chatId`) and `reply_address` null, its version K-04's default; `openQuestions` holds S1-02's, `oq-s1-02-sam-1` (handle and person Sam's, business Lee's Barbershop, no booking, no options, no read-back, no versions read, `clock` null); `outboxRows` holds `outbox-s1-02-sam-1` (recipient `handle-sam` and `person-sam`, business `biz-lees-barbershop`, no booking, channel `imessage`, the turn's `service`, send class `quick_reply`, open question `oq-s1-02-sam-1`, no copy, no versions read, no plan).

### Fixtures, v1.5

Accepted (2): `build-frontier-s1-01.json` (the frontier a new number's first text gets: K-02 v1.1's `context-s1-01.json` unchanged) and `commit-s1-01.json` (`givesStepKeys`: S1-01 gives thread; the thread, S1-02's question with its clock row, and `outbox-s1-02-sam-1`; from round 3 its request's turn carries Sam's text's service and chat id, and its `given.stated.writes` states the thread, the question and the outbox row field for field: defaults 135 and 136). With v1's `compose-input-s1-02.json` and `finalize-pass.json` they chain one turn, each call's output feeding the next: the commit's outbox row is the row compose input reads (T-1141's unit proof; CQ-156 a settled). No refused fixture is added: K-02 v1 refuses the one new value itself, and v1's limit fixtures keep the gate's refusals.

### Who reads v1.5

The action-layer engineer (C-074 turn face, C-075 frontier builder, C-076 hubs and doors, C-087 the commit's walk; T-1139, T-1141, T-293), the runtime engineer (C-046, C-051; joins T-556 to T-559), the AI engineer (C-114, C-115, C-123) and QA (`tests/contract/tool-api.v1.test.ts`, which already takes v1.5's fixtures by their version; C-148). The Tool API mock (C-186) covers v1 to v1.4 until it is generated again (`npm run mock --workspace contracts`; open gap 123). From round 3 (default 135): the runtime engineer's handle actor (C-046; T-559's join) fills the request turn's `service` and `chatId` from the turn's last text; the action layer's commit (C-087; T-1139, T-1141) reads them in the walk and writes K-05's turn row without them; and the Tool API's validator (`actions/src/tool-api/validator.ts`, which holds K-09 v1 today) also holds K-06 v1 (`inbound-event/inbound-event.v1.schema.json`) when it holds v1.5, since `gateAndCommit.turn` reaches it. Each consumer adopts v1.5 in a task that needs it; tasks already building finish on their version (build prompt 6.4).

### Open gaps, v1.5

118. **Turn 2's frontier on bundle v0.** `build-frontier-s1-02.json` (v1) offers S11-21's door and the danger row, steps bundle v0 does not hold, against default 129's rule. Turn 2 has left the skeleton; journey 1's lane meets it with the full bundle (T-293, T-294).
119. **A new owner before any business head exists.** K-02's target names a business by id, and K-05 v1.2 reads such a turn with `businessId` null (`read-turn-state-first-text.json`). Proposed for QUESTIONS.md: S1-01's turn runs under the business head the sign-up link was made for, written before the turn; or a K-02 major version lets the door name none. Journey 1's lane.
120. **K-01 has no routing entry for the sign-up door** (K-02 README "v1.1"): a K-01 minor version adds `signUp: "S1-01"` to `routingTables.doors`; until then the frontier takes it as section 1's first step.
121. **Hop 10's fixture for turn 1.** v1's `finalize-pass.json` gives S1-02's first reply with the plan's own first words marked as the opener and no S13-02 opt-out line or privacy link. T-314's inserter (merged) puts the bundle's opener words ("This is Rosie, the automated booking assistant for <business>."), the opt-out line and the privacy link on Rosie's first text, and refuses a first text whole while the bundle has no `privacyLink` line (T-314's handoff item 3), which K-01 v1 and bundle v0 do not have; Q-30's privacy notice words and address wait on Jay. So finalize on turn 1 fails closed (lineNotInBundle) until the bundle carries the line, and `finalize-pass.json`'s outcome is not what finalize gives turn 1 under Q-30. A K-09 minor version adds turn 1's finalize fixture with Q-30's lines once the words exist. Proposed for QUESTIONS.md.
122. **Two forms of turn 1.** D-017's human test and `tests/e2e/skeleton` send JOIN (K-08 `trace-skeleton.json`'s ids: `evt-sam-0000`, `turn-s1-01-sam-join`); the fixture chain carries the master's words (`turn-s1-01-sam`), the text `compose-input-s1-02.json` answers. The turn's shape is one: the frontier's choices, the commit's rows and the reply's row are the same; decide's quote, the safety read's text and the composer's inbound differ, so the double records each form (K-02 README "v1.1"). K-08's sample trace names its rows by stand-ins of its own (`oq-s1-02`, `clock-oq-s1-02-sam`, `out-s1-02-sam`); a run's trace carries the run's ids (default 131), and the end-to-end test compares hops, never these ids.
123. **The Tool API mock** covers v1 to v1.4; generating it again for v1.5 is `contracts/mock/**`, outside T-1142's card.

## v1.6 (T-1173): compose input's term facts

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): the JOIN run's composer refuses its own expected plan for S1-02's row (T-376's round 1 NEED, `ops/cards/T-376.md`: literalValue twice, as K-03 v1.1's `refuse-plan-s1-02-on-v1-input.json` shows). v1's composeInput serves K-03 v1's input, which has no term facts, so S1-02's row reaches the composer with `facts: []`, while `plan-s1-02.json` states S1-02's card terms "About 15 minutes" and "$2 a booking", and the composer may state only `<facts>` (arch 07 line 403). K-03 v1.1 (T-1104) lets Rosie's own fixed terms that a step's card states reach the composer as term facts, each with its source line (CQ-82; DL-145), and its consumer table named this K-09 minor version (K-03 README "v1.1", "Consumers of v1.1"); CQ-86 and open gap 79 waited for it. The lead's ruling (DL-355): T-1173 takes v1.6, ahead of journey 7's freeze, which becomes v1.7 (T-054), since compose input is on the texting path (DL-308). **Kind:** additive, a minor version. `tool-api.v1.6.schema.json` holds every v1.5 definition under its own name, the unchanged ones by `$ref` to `tool-api.v1.5.schema.json`, which stays as it is. Three definitions change: `composeInput.response`, whose input is K-03 v1.1's `composeInput` (`../message-plan/message-plan.v1.1.schema.json`); `composeInput.errors`, which takes v1.5's errors and two more, each new under its own name (`fixedLineRowError`, `masterLineMissingError`); and `composeInput.exchange`, which takes both. Every K-03 v1 input is a K-03 v1.1 input (K-03 README "v1.1") and every earlier error is still one, so every v1 to v1.5 exchange is a v1.6 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks every earlier accepted fixture against v1.6). No call, key or other change. **Scope:** composeInput alone: its term facts for S1-02's row on bundle v0 (`contracts/bundle/fixtures/bundle-v0-skeleton.json`), CQ-86's default for every other step (default 137), and T-309's open question, its errors for a fixed-line row and a missing master line.

### What composeInput gives from v1.6

- **The input:** K-03 v1.1's `composeInput`, v1's fields with each fact a slot fact (v1's `fact`: a slot and its value) or a term fact (`termFact`: a value and its source line, no slot) (K-03 v1.1 `inputFact`; arch 07 lines 397, 403). Never the frontier, as v1.
- **Slot facts:** as v1, the row's committed values only as limit 7 lets its reader see them (arch 06 line 311; C-091; T-309).
- **Term facts:** each line of the row's step card that names no slot, word for word, with its source line, from the bundle the row was committed under, in the card's order after the slot facts (defaults 137, 138 and 141); none when the card states no term. They reach the composer with the row's other facts, as K-03 v1.1 says (C-091). For S1-02, the card's four lines (K-03 v1.1 default 3): `input-s1-02-v1.1.json` word for word.
- **An email copy:** as v1, the text row whose plan it takes (arch 07 line 397).
- **Errors:** v1's four (`notFound`, `alreadyPlanned`, `rowClosed`, `transient`), and `fixedLineRow` and `masterLineMissing` (defaults 139 and 140). Each is "refused, nothing composed and nothing written".

### Defaults (T-1173), numbered after T-1142's

137. **Which card lines are term facts (CQ-86's default, on every step).** A term fact is a line of the row's step card (K-01 `step.say[].elements.card.lines`, the card of the message the row carries) that names no slot, copied word for word from the bundle the row was committed under. A line with a slot is not a term fact: its values reach the composer as slot facts, through limit 7 (arch 12 line 592: the compiler slots the sample cast's values, Rosie's own fee terms excepted). For S1-02 all four card lines are term facts (K-03 v1.1 default 3); on every other step the default stands until the CTO rules on CQ-86 (open gap 125), and no list of terms exists (CQ-82): the named rule `termFactsFromCard` holds a term fact to its step's card line, never to a list.
138. **A term fact's source line** (CQ-82: each term with its source line; K-03 v1.1 default 2: the source as plain words). It names the card line it copies: the step, the line's place on the card and its K-01 path in the bundle (`S1-02's card, line 1`, `K-01 flows.A.steps.S1-02.say[0].elements.card.lines[0]`), with what the term is, the master's line and the rule that sets the term where the sources give them (RULES 3.15 for Rosie's fee), as K-03 v1.1's `input-s1-02-v1.1.json` writes S1-02's four, word for word. The bundle gives the line and its path, not the master's line or the rule (open gap 126).
139. **`fixedLineRow`** (T-309's open question; v1 open gap 16; T-309's default 7 throws it today). A row that carries a fixed line, whose words code writes into it word for word (arch 07 line 361), such as the guard's STOP, START and HELP replies, a safety line or an outage line, each an outbox row while PostgreSQL is up (default 6; NQ-RT-9), is never composed: composeInput answers `fixedLineRow` with the row and the fixed line's id (K-01 `fixedLine.id`), builds no composer input, calls no model and writes nothing. It is the row's answer, not a fault to retry. Which fixed line a row carries is the row read's (T-309's `fixedLineId`); how its plan is written stays open (open gap 128).
140. **`masterLineMissing`** (T-309's open question; T-309's default 7 throws bundleMissing, stepMissing and noMasterLine today). A row whose master line cannot be read has no `<master_text>` to compose from (arch 07 line 401): its bundle is in neither KV nor PostgreSQL's copy (arch 10 line 536), the bundle holds no such step (K-01 v1: a step a bundle does not hold is not reachable), or the step prints no line from Rosie to this reader at the row's place. composeInput answers `masterLineMissing` with the row, the bundle its turn pinned (arch 10 line 548), its step and what is missing (`bundle`, `step` or `line`); nothing is composed (fail closed) and nothing is written. It is not transient: the same request answers the same until the row's bundle or step changes. It names ids alone, never a line's words.
141. **The facts' order.** The slot facts as v1 gives them (T-309: the master line's slots, then the row's, each once), then the term facts in the card's order, line 1 first; for S1-02, whose row has no slot fact, the card's four terms in its order, as `input-s1-02-v1.1.json` holds them. One order, so one row always gives the same input: the recorded double keys a recording on it (T-1175).

### Named rules, v1.6

`tool-api.rules.mjs` adds two, under new names (rules are only ever added). Each reads only the contracts' own files beside it, synchronously, so `npm run contracts:check` loads the file as before.

- **`termFactsFromCard`:** on a composeInput exchange answered compose, each term fact's value is, word for word, a line of the card of the input's step (`masterText.step`) in bundle v0 (`contracts/bundle/fixtures/bundle-v0-skeleton.json`, the bundle the skeleton's fixtures run on: section "v1.5", "Scope"), and that line names no slot (default 137). The schema cannot see the bundle, so this rule alone refuses a term fact whose words differ from its card's line. An input with no term fact passes.
- **`inputIsK03Fixture`:** the same exchange's input is, word for word, K-03 v1.1's accepted `composeInput` fixture of the same row and turn (`contracts/message-plan/fixtures/index.json`; exactly one must exist): the same values, key for key in K-03's order. So `compose-input-s1-02-v1.6.json`'s input is compared with `input-s1-02-v1.1.json` itself, never trusted as a copy. It runs `termFactsFromCard` too.

### Fixtures, v1.6

Accepted (3): `compose-input-s1-02-v1.6.json` (rule `inputIsK03Fixture`): the same request as v1's `compose-input-s1-02.json`, after `commit-s1-01.json` wrote the row, answered with K-03 v1.1's `input-s1-02-v1.1.json` word for word, v1's input with S1-02's four card lines as term facts, each with its source line, so the composer may state "About 15 minutes" and "$2 a booking" (`plan-s1-02.json`); `compose-input-fixed-line-row.json` (Ben's stop reply row, which `stop-recorded.json` wrote: `fixedLineRow`, `stopReply`); `compose-input-master-line-missing.json` (turn 2's `out-s1-04-sam`, which `commit-s1-02.json` wrote under bundle v0, which holds no S1-04: `masterLineMissing`, missing step; section "v1.5", default 129 and open gap 118). Refused (3), in `fixtures/invalid/`, each `compose-input-s1-02-v1.6.json` with one fault: `refuse-term-fact-no-source.json` (Rosie's $2 fee with no source line: refused by the schema), `refuse-term-fact-names-slot.json` (the $2 fee naming the `fee` slot, which is the business's own late-cancel and no-show fee, RULES 3.3: refused by the schema) and `refuse-term-fact-not-card-line.json` ("About 10 minutes for your first kind of work", which S1-02's card does not state: the schema accepts it, and `termFactsFromCard` alone refuses it). Each is refused for its one fault: with the source line back, the slot dropped or the card's words back, its outcome is `compose-input-s1-02-v1.6.json`'s, which is accepted (checked 2026-11-10).

Each consumer adopts v1.6 in a task that needs it; tasks already building finish on their version (build prompt 6.4).

**Contract test, v1.6 (QA, its own task).** The mends of the three refused fixtures (the test's MENDS, as for v1 and v1.1: the source line back, the slot dropped, the card's words back, each giving `compose-input-s1-02-v1.6.json`'s outcome); `inputIsK03Fixture` and `termFactsFromCard` fired on damaged copies the schema still takes (a term fact's words changed, two keys swapped, a term fact dropped, another row); and each term fact of `compose-input-s1-02-v1.6.json` a line of S1-02's card in `bundle-v0-skeleton.json`, as K-03 v1.1's part checks `input-s1-02-v1.1.json`.
