## v1.8 (T-1219): an unmapped turn's end at the gate

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): T-1157 froze K-02 v1.2 (merged as 70d91ce; K-02 README "v1.2"), whose decide result can hold a read the AI layer's validator refused (`refusedRead`: the model that answered, its role and the indexes of its unmapped parts, with no output) and the unmapped turn's end (`decideResult.end`, `"oneQuestion"`), CQ-162 (a)'s default: "a turn still unmapped ends in one question with nothing changed". K-09 had a place for neither: gateAndCommit's request is v1.5's, whose decided input is K-02 v1.1's `contextAndDecide`, every read of which requires an output, so a v1.2 result fails the request schema before it reaches the gate (T-1157's handoff 2, `ops/cards/T-1157.md`; `actions/src/gate/framework/types.ts`), and no answer could say that the turn ended in one question. The lead carded this freeze from T-1157's card a0 (DL-457). **Kind:** additive, a minor version. **Its number:** v1.7 is T-054's journey 7 freeze (DL-355), not merged when this one was frozen, so this one is v1.8 (the lead's note on T-1219); it refers to v1.6, the newest K-09 on main, and holds none of v1.7's journey 7 calls (open gap 130). `tool-api.v1.8.schema.json` holds every v1.6 definition under its own name, the unchanged ones by `$ref` to `tool-api.v1.6.schema.json`, which stays as it is. Four definitions change, each taking every earlier value: `gateAndCommit.request`, whose decided input is K-02 v1.2's `contextAndDecide` (every K-02 v1 and v1.1 pair is one: K-02 README "v1.2"); `gateAndCommit.committed`, v1.4's fields with one more, `end`; `gateAndCommit.response`, which takes it; and `gateAndCommit.exchange`, which ties the answer's `end` to the request's. So every v1 to v1.6 exchange is a v1.8 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks every earlier accepted fixture against v1.8). No call, key or error is added. The changed definitions restate v1.5's and v1.4's other parts with the same keywords rather than point into them: `npm run contracts:types` (json-schema-to-typescript) failed on a `$ref` into v1.4's `gateAndCommit.committed/properties/written`, whose target refers on into K-05's `commitResult` ("Missing $ref pointer #/$defs/outboxRowWritten"), while a `$ref` to a whole `$defs` entry resolves, as in every earlier version.

**Not here:** any code (the gate, the commit and the runtime's wiring each move in their own task: "Who reads v1.8"); decide's code that gives the end (T-1158); the one question's words and its row (CQ-199; open gap 129).

### What v1.8 changes

- **The request.** `input.decided` is K-02 v1.2's pair: decide's result may hold refused reads, each in its place among the reads, and `end: "oneQuestion"`, set exactly when a read was refused and the one re-read has run (K-02 v1.2's `dependentSchemas`). The pair's named rules (`optionOffered`, `quoteInText`: `contracts/model/model.rules.mjs`) read a K-02 read's output and the result's kept parts, so both skip a refused read. On that end `input.planner` is null (default 144). Everything else is v1.5's.
- **The answer.** `gateAndCommit.committed.end`, `"asked"`: the turn's unmapped end, asked once for the turn. `gateAndCommit.exchange` requires it on a committed answer exactly when the request's decided result holds K-02's end, and refuses it on any other answer. An answer that holds it gated and wrote nothing for any part (`dependentSchemas`): each part decide kept is `asked`, every limit `notChecked` with no outcome (no limit ran), with no `stoppedBy`, no key and nothing it depends on; `planner` is null; and `written.written` is empty. The turn's core actions commit as in any turn (default 143).
- **Errors.** v1.5's. No limit runs on the end, so no limit's outcome, `valueFailsWords` or limit not built answers it; `decideRefused` still can, when a kept part or a K-02 read of the turn breaks a named rule, and a turn's own errors (`bundleMismatch`, `turnParked`, `transient`) answer it as they answer any turn.

### Defaults (T-1219), numbered after T-1173's

142. **The end runs no limit.** On a request whose decided result holds K-02 v1.2's end, the gate checks decide's output as it does now, skipping a refused read (it has no output), and runs none of limits 1 to 6: every part decide kept already ends in one question (K-02 v1.2's schema lets no part go on a turn with a refused read), and the gate answers a part decide ended in one question `asked`, its limits `notChecked` (`actions/src/gate/framework/gate.ts`, its default 2). A kept part of any verb is asked, redact among them, so nothing it asks for is done that turn (T-1157's security note a). With every read refused there is no part, and `parts` is empty (K-02 v1.2 default 14). Sources: CQ-162 (a)'s default; arch 06 line 289 ("Still unclear: one question; nothing changes for that part"); Q-41 (the re-read runs before the one question).
143. **"Nothing written" on the end.** The commit writes no row for any part: no state, no open question or its clock row, no outbox row, no effect and no key; and no walk runs, since no choice was taken (arch 03 line 191). It still commits the turn under its id, as every turn does: the turn row, so a repeat finds the first commit and answers `repeated` (K-05 v1 commitTurn; arch 10 line 508), and the turn's core actions, each text's safety outcome among them, since K-34 v1 writes one outcome row per text under the turn that joins it (default 5). A core action's rows are named in its response and left out of `written.written` (default 134), and its line, such as a safety line, stays in `written.outboxRows`, as in any turn (`actions/src/commit/commit.ts`, its default 9). So the schema holds `written.written` empty on the end and leaves `outboxRows`, `promoted`, `effects` and `openQuestions` to the core actions. With a text that reads No, as in `commit-s1-02-unmapped-end.json`, all of them are empty, and the answer is `committed`.
144. **No planner verb on the end.** The planner runs when nothing fits after meaning search (arch 03 line 189; arch 06 line 291), and its verbs write (an answer, a question, a hand-off, a gap). On the unmapped end nothing changes for any part (CQ-162 (a)), so a request whose decided result holds the end carries `planner` null (`DecidedInput`'s `if`), and its answer's `planner` is null.
145. **Asked once for the turn.** `end: "asked"` is the turn's one question: one for the turn, never one per part (K-02 v1.2 default 12). The turn ends there and is neither run again nor parked (T-1157's card b; T-1221). A repeat of the same turn id answers the first commit, `end` and all, with `repeated` true (K-05 v1 commitTurn). An answer to such a request without `end` would read as a turn that committed nothing, which a caller could take for a fault and run again, so `gateAndCommit.exchange` refuses it (`refuse-unmapped-end-not-asked.json`). What the question says, and which row carries it, are not this version's (open gap 129).

### Named rule, v1.8

`tool-api.rules.mjs` adds one, under a new name (rules are only ever added). It reads only the contracts' own files beside it, synchronously, as v1.6's two do.

- **`unmappedEndAsked`:** on a gateAndCommit exchange whose decided result holds K-02 v1.2's end, the decided pair is, word for word, K-02 v1.2's accepted `contextAndDecide` fixture of the same turn (`contracts/model/fixtures/index.json`; exactly one must exist): the same values, key for key in K-02's order. The answer is `committed` with `end` asked and one part result per part decide kept, in its order (`part` its index, `choiceId` the kept part's choice for choose and enter, else null), each `asked` with limits 1 to 6 `notChecked` and no outcome, no stop, no key and nothing it depends on. The schema ties `end` to the request and refuses a written row, a planner verb, a limit run or a part that goes on the end; it cannot count the answer's parts against decide's or read K-02's file, which this rule does.

### Fixtures, v1.8

Accepted (1): `commit-s1-02-unmapped-end.json` (rule `unmappedEndAsked`): K-02 v1.2's `pair-unmapped-all-refused.json` word for word, as the gate reads it: Sam's "Barbershop, plumbing, electrical" on S1-02's frontier (`build-frontier-s1-02.json`'s context, K-02's `context-s1-02.json`), where the AI layer's validator refused both Haiku reads and the one Sonnet re-read (part 0 unmapped each time), so decide ends the whole turn in one question with no part. The request's turn carries the service and the Linq chat id of Sam's text, the stand-ins `commit-s1-01.json` gives his chat (default 135), and its one core action is his text's safety outcome (No). The answer: `committed`, `end` asked, no part, no planner verb, and nothing in `written`, `effects` or `openQuestions`; the safety outcome's response names its row and sends no line. It is v1's `commit-s1-02.json` (the same turn id, text and frontier) as the gate answers it when every read is refused: no picked kinds, no walk through S1-11 and no S1-04 question. Refused (3), in `fixtures/invalid/`, each `commit-s1-02-unmapped-end.json` with one fault: `refuse-unmapped-end-written.json` (the commit writes S1-02's three picked kinds, as `commit-s1-02.json` does when both reads agree: refused by the schema, since an answer that holds the end writes no row of a part or a walk; with `written.written` empty it is the accepted fixture); `refuse-unmapped-end-not-asked.json` (the end answered as any turn, committed with no `end`: refused by `gateAndCommit.exchange`; with `end` asked it is the accepted fixture); and `refuse-unmapped-end-part-not-kept.json` (a part result, 'All on the list' asked with no limit run, for a part decide never kept: the schema takes it, and `unmappedEndAsked` alone refuses it; with `parts` empty it is the accepted fixture). Each differs from the accepted fixture in that one field and its note alone (checked 2026-11-11).

### Who reads v1.8

Each consumer adopts v1.8 in a task that needs it; tasks already building finish on their version (build prompt 6.4). What each must change, as its later card's lines (v1.8 changes no code):

- **The gate** (C-080; the action-layer engineer; T-1220, T-1157's card a). **Change:** read K-09 v1.8's gateAndCommit request, whose decided input is K-02 v1.2's pair (`actions/src/gate/framework/types.ts` reaches K-09 v1.5's generated types today); `checkDecideOutput` and `findDecideProblems` (`checks.ts`) skip a refused read, which has no `output`, and check every K-02 read and kept part as now; on `end: "oneQuestion"`, run no limit and answer each kept part `asked` with limits 1 to 6 `notChecked`, as `runGate` answers a oneQuestion part today, and no part when every read was refused (default 142). A result with no refused read is gated exactly as now. **Proves it with:** `pair-unmapped-all-refused.json` gated with no limit run and no part; `refuse-go-with-refused-read.json` with its part's outcome oneQuestion has that part asked; every earlier gate test unchanged.
- **The action layer's commit and the Tool API's check** (C-087, gateAndCommit's handler, `actions/src/commit/commit.ts`; C-074, `actions/src/tool-api/validator.ts` with `infra/build/tool-api-validators.mjs`; the action-layer engineer; a card proposed). **Change:** check gateAndCommit's request, response and errors by v1.8's definitions, as composeInput's are checked by v1.6's (`V1_6_CALLS`), so a v1.2 result passes the request check and the answer's `end` the response check; on the end, call no `writesOf` and write no row for any part (default 143), commit the turn and its core actions as any turn, and answer `committed` with `end: "asked"`, `planner` null and `written.written` empty; a repeat answers the first commit's `end`. **Proves it with:** `commit-s1-02-unmapped-end.json` answered field for field on PostgreSQL, nothing written but the turn row and the safety outcome's row; `refuse-unmapped-end-written.json`'s answer refused by the response check (`end` with a row written); and no answer to the end without `end` (`refuse-unmapped-end-not-asked.json`, which only the exchange, seeing the request, refuses).
- **The runtime's wiring** (C-046, the handle actor's decide and re-read ports, `runtime/src/wiring` and `runtime/src/turn`; the runtime engineer; T-1221, T-1157's card b, after T-1158 and T-1220). **Change:** pass decide's unmapped end on as `{result: "decided", decide}`, not `{error: "unmapped"}` (K-02 README "Who reads v1.2"); `runtime/src/turn/types.ts`'s `DecideResult` takes refused reads and `end`, and `turn.ts`'s `isResultOf` takes a result with no part when it holds the end; send the gateAndCommit request with K-02 v1.2's pair and `planner` null, and take an answer with `end: "asked"` as the turn's end: no second re-read, never run again and never parked; spans hold labels only. **Proves it with:** `refuse-option-not-offered.json`'s reads, through the root's decide port, give the gate `pair-unmapped-all-refused.json`'s result, answered as `commit-s1-02-unmapped-end.json`.
- **QA** (C-140): `tests/contract/tool-api.v1.test.ts` selects entries by `version` (DL-125), so v1.8's four fixtures join its generic checks now (each by v1.8's schema, the required-field walk, and `rereadBeforeQuestion` on every decided gate fixture); v1.8's own part is QA's task (below).
- **The Tool API mock** (C-186): covers v1 to v1.4 until it is generated again (open gap 123).

The AI engineer's side does not move: K-02 v1.2 is the result T-1158 gives, and K-09 v1.8 carries it unchanged. v1.8 changes no core action, so `runtime/src/actor/copies`' `CORE_CALLS_WITHOUT_TIMERS` stands (CQ-190).

**Contract test, v1.8 (QA, its own task).** `unmappedEndAsked` fired on damaged copies the schema still takes (a value of the pair changed, two of its keys swapped, a part result with no decide part as `refuse-unmapped-end-part-not-kept.json`); the mends of the three refused fixtures (`written.written` emptied; `end` asked put back; `parts` emptied), each giving `commit-s1-02-unmapped-end.json`'s outcome; and `end` refused on an answer to a request with no unmapped end (`commit-s1-02.json`'s answer with `end` asked).

### Open gaps, v1.8

129. **The one question's words and its row** (CQ-199; K-02 README "Open gaps, v1.2"). v1.8 answers the end asked and writes no row for it, so this commit sends nothing: no source words what Rosie asks about a text no read could map, and with every read refused there is no kept part to word it from. CQ-199's default: the composer's lane names the words, as a bundle fixed line, before T-1221 starts. Proposed: once those words exist, a K-09 minor version lets the end's answer name the one fixed-line outbox row the commit writes for it (send class quick_reply, a reply in the same turn: v1.5 default 133), and its fixture adds that row. Until then a turn that ends this way sends nothing, as today's turn, which errors, is run again and is parked, sends nothing.
130. **T-054's journey 7 freeze after v1.8.** `contracts/scripts/check-fixtures.mjs` and `tests/contract/tool-api.v1.test.ts` check every accepted fixture against every later minor version of K-09, and v1.8 holds no journey 7 definition, so a v1.7 frozen after v1.8 fails both: v1.8 refuses each journey 7 exchange. When T-054 rebases onto a main that holds v1.8, its freeze takes the next free number above v1.8 (v1.9) on top of v1.8, and its defaults and open gaps are numbered after this section's (142 to 145; 129 and 130), as journey 7's planned v1.5 became v1.7 (DL-355). The lead's call.

## v1.10 (T-1373): every row a safety record writes, named with its recipient

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): T-1358's finding (`ops/cards/T-1358.md`, round 1; DL-743; CQ-313). The handle actor's danger wake names only the handle the text came from (`runtime/src/safety/arrival.ts` sendDanger, about lines 1979-1981; `runtime/src/wiring/root.ts` dangerWake, about 548), because a dispatcher run claims by recipient, never by row: a safety run names its recipient (K-05 v1.14 default 134; `runtime/src/dispatch/dispatcher.ts` `isSafetyRun`, about 298-309), and the actor knows only its own handle (`runtime/src/safety/types.ts` `HandleIds`, about 127-132). `recordSafetyOutcome`'s answer named the line's row but not who it goes to (a threat's line goes to the owner: A3-48; `actions/src/commit/core/lines.ts` about 67-70; `actions/src/domain/safety/record.ts` about 447-454), and named no other row the record's commit writes: the line's email copy and a line the reach card sends to another handle (`actions/src/domain/messages-consent/messages-consent.ts` `contactsOf` and `outboxRows`, about 338-445), the hand-off's alert (v1 `handOff.response.alertOutboxRowId`) and a team page's rows (v1 `pageTeam.response.outboxRowIds`); `told` names only the hand-off's, the page's or the harm report's own id (v1.1; `types.ts` about 169-177). So a row to the owner or to Rosie's team waited for that person's next run, maybe never, against arch 06 line 329 (crisis words: Rosie's team and the owner at once; an emergency or a threat: the owner) and RULES 4.12 ("Danger now: the safety message first (911, or 988 for self-harm), then a person is told at once. Safety beats text hours, clocks and silence defaults"). The lead carded this freeze from T-1358's NEED (DL-743, DL-749). **Kind:** additive, a minor version. **Its number:** the next free one above v1.9 (open gap 142's default). `tool-api.v1.10.schema.json` holds every v1.9 definition under its own name, 423 of them by `$ref` to `tool-api.v1.9.schema.json`, which stays as it is. Two change, each taking every earlier value: `recordSafetyOutcome.response` (v1.1's fields restated with the same keywords, as v1.8 restated what it changed, with two new optional fields) and `recordSafetyOutcome.exchange` (which takes it). One definition is new, `safetyRowWritten`. `callName` is v1.9's: no call, key or error is added. So every v1 to v1.9 exchange is a v1.10 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks every earlier accepted fixture against v1.10).

**Not here:** the action layer filling the fields (T-1374); the actor's wake per recipient (T-1375); T-319's hand-offs and pages (the telling port is `NOT_TOLD_YET`, so a record that must tell someone answers `notBuilt` today: `actions/src/domain/safety/ports.ts`); a record carried in a turn's commit (open gap 146); the K-05 read a repeat's rows need (open gap 147: a K-05 minor version, then its `db/repo` read). Nothing is live yet: no deployed root lets the core answer (T-1358).

### What v1.10 changes

- **The line's recipient.** `safetyLine.recipientHandleId`: the handle `safetyLine.outboxRowId` goes to, null exactly when that row is null (the actor's own send; a page's or an agent's line, which the caller shows). For a threat it is the owner's handle, never the texter's (default 174).
- **Every row, with its recipient.** `safetyRows`: every outbox row the record's commit writes, in commit order, each `{outboxRowId, recipientHandleId, sendClass, writtenFor}` (`safetyRowWritten`), `writtenFor` saying what it carries: `line` (each row of the line: its text row, its email copy, or its one row by email), `handOff` (the hand-off's alert) or `teamPage` (one member's row of a team page opened at once). A repeat names the first record's rows, each as stored (default 175; open gap 147). A v1.10 call always returns both fields, together (the response's `dependentSchemas` and `allOf`); a v1 to v1.9 call leaves both out.
- **The line among the rows.** A line with an outbox row is among `safetyRows` as a `line` row; a line with none names no `line` row (the response's `allOf`).
- **The send class of the alert and the page rows** (CQ-313 question 1): safety, in a record's commit (default 176). The schema refuses an alert or a page row of any other class, and a `line` row of any class but safety or quick_reply (the outage line's, v1.1).
- **The recipient is a handle's id** (default 177), never a number or an address (K-08 v1 `handleRef`).
- **A call with no turn names no row** (default 178): `recordSafetyOutcome.exchange` holds `safetyRows` empty when `request.caller.turnId` is null.
- **What the schema leaves to tests** (QA's v1.10 part): that the `line` rows hold `safetyLine.outboxRowId` with `safetyLine.recipientHandleId` as its recipient; that row ids are unique; and that a repeat names the same rows as the record it repeats.

### Defaults (T-1373), numbered after T-054's

174. **The rows named, and the line's recipient.** `safetyRows` names every outbox row the record's commit writes, whatever writes it: today `record.ts`'s line rows (T-307's `outboxRows` for the line's recipient) and the telling port's rows (T-319's hand-offs and pages), in the order the commit writes them (K-04 `outbox_rows.commit_seq`: the line first, then who is told, as `record.ts` joins its writes). Each carries the row's `recipientHandleId` and `sendClass` as written (K-05 v1 `outboxRowNew`). The line's rows follow T-307 as built: the reach card on file, else the handle the text came from (open gap 144); a text row to the card's mobile with an email copy (`<row>-email`) to its email when it has one (RULES 4.1, 4.8; arch 02 line 125), or one row by email for the email way. A threat's line goes to the owner, to the handle he writes from (A3-48; S13-10; `lines.ts`; `record.ts` `lineRows`). `safetyLine.outboxRowId` is the first line row (`record.ts`), and `safetyLine.recipientHandleId` its recipient. Sources: T-1358's finding; RULES 4.12; arch 06 line 329; arch 02 lines 111 and 125.
175. **A repeat names the first record's rows, as stored** (kept in round 2: DL-751; section "Round 2" below). The event id is the key (K-34 v1 `safety_outcomes_event_key`): a repeat writes nothing and answers the first record (K-09 v1: "one outcome row per text, and a repeat returns the first"; `record.ts` default 1), so its `safetyRows` are the rows the first record wrote, the same list in the same order, each with the recipient and send class its stored row holds (K-04 `outbox_rows.recipient_handle_id` and `send_class`), and `safetyLine.recipientHandleId` the stored line row's recipient. The ids are the first record's, never made anew: the line's from the event id (`ports.ids.rowId("line", eventId)`, as `record.ts` names its row, then that row's email copy, T-307's `emailCopyId`, in T-307's order), then the alert's and the page rows' as the telling port's repeat names them, in the order it wrote them (T-319). Each is read from its stored row through K-05's next minor version, which no frozen version gives yet (open gap 147), so T-1374 depends on it. A row the read shows not written is not named, and a line with no written row names none (`outboxRowId` and `recipientHandleId` null, no `line` row). A repeat's rows are never rebuilt from the record and the reach card as they are at the repeat (section "Round 2"). So a repeated answer wakes every recipient again (T-1352's ruling: "There is no retry, except a later repeated answer"), and nothing goes twice: one claim per row, each part under its K-07 key. Sources: the card ("on first and repeated answers alike"); T-1352's case 9; K-05 v1.14 item 67 (a safety run claims only its named recipient's safety rows).
176. **The send class of the hand-off's alert and of a team page's rows (CQ-313 question 1).** The hand-off's alert (v1 `handOff.response.alertOutboxRowId`, which stated no class) is send class safety for a hand-off of kind `crisis_words`, `emergency` or `threat`, and routine for `other`; a team page's rows are safety for crisis words, an emergency or a threat and routine otherwise, as v1 `pageTeam.response.outboxRowIds` already states. A record tells only for crisis words, an emergency or a threat (`record.ts` `tellsOf`; arch 06 line 329), so every alert and page row in its answer is safety, and the schema holds it (`safetyRowWritten`). Sources: arch 06 line 329 ("crisis words, Rosie's team and the owner at once"; "Any other hand-off: the owner in text hours"); RULES 4.12 ("then a person is told at once. Safety beats text hours, clocks and silence defaults"); RULES 4.3 ("Any time: a quick reply, a visit happening now, safety"); K-07 v1 `sendClass` ("Only routine waits for text hours"). So these rows go at any hour, are claimed by K-05 v1.14's safety-only claim (item 67) under a wake that is never gated, are never asked by T-1337's or T-1342's hold (neither asks a safety row), and go first in any run (T-1352's rulings, rule 3); limit 8 treats them as every safety row (open gap 145). The other half of CQ-313 question 1, T-1342's condition 2 reading a writer's own crisis line in `core.test.ts` as the owner's notice, is the lead's record to correct: the owner's notice is a hand-off's alert or a threat's line, as `j3-safety-arrival-crisis-rows.json` and `j3-safety-arrival-threat-repeat.json` show.
177. **The recipient is a handle's id.** `recipientHandleId` is K-08 v1's `handleRef`: a K-04 `handles.id`, never the raw number or address (arch 02 diagram line 105; arch 10 line 516), as a phone row in the event log is already named by its handle id (K-08 v1 `rowRef`). An email row's recipient is its email handle's id. So the answer the actor reads, and any span made from it, carries no number or address (`refuse-safety-row-recipient-not-handle.json`).
178. **A call with no turn names no row.** K-04 `outbox_rows.turn_id` is not null, so only a commit with a turn writes outbox rows (`actions/src/domain/flags/writes.ts`: "Outbox rows: only a turn's commit writes them"). A page's or an agent's record shows its line (v1) and names no row, and so does any caller whose `turnId` is null; `recordSafetyOutcome.exchange` holds it. Such a record tells by a clock row due at once, as a page's or a job's message goes (v1.4 default 112).

### Fixtures, v1.10

Accepted (2), each naming `arrivalTurn`, which part 2 of the contract test asks of every record made on arrival and which holds for them as for v1.1's. `j3-safety-arrival-crisis-rows.json`: A3-25 on arrival, Alex's crisis words (K-02's `safety-crisis-words.json` word for word), first recorded: the crisis line by Alex's reach card (`customers/seed-customer-mobile-email.json`: the way texts, a mobile and an email), a text row to `handle-alex` and its email copy to `handle-alex-email`; Sam told at once through the hand-off's alert to `handle-sam`, the owner's notice; Rosie's team paged at once, one row to each of its two members (`handle-team-1`, `handle-team-2`, which name no member, as `page-team.json`'s rows do); all five rows send class safety. `j3-safety-arrival-threat-repeat.json`: A3-48's record made again after `j3-safety-arrival-threat.json` (T-1352's case 9: a re-read applies it again), answered repeated with the first record's rows: the threat's line to Sam (`safetyLine.recipientHandleId` `handle-sam`, not the texter's `handle-alex`: T-1358's finding a) and the hand-off's alert to Sam. Refused (3), in `fixtures/invalid/`, each `j3-safety-arrival-crisis-rows.json` with one fault in its alert row: `refuse-safety-row-without-recipient.json` (no `recipientHandleId`); `refuse-safety-row-recipient-not-handle.json` (Sam's number, the seed's fictional one, in place of his handle's id: no K-08 `handleRef`); `refuse-safety-row-alert-routine.json` (send class routine: default 176). Put right (`handle-sam`; `handle-sam`; safety), each is the accepted fixture. Each differs from it in that one field and its note alone (checked 2026-11-14).

### Who reads v1.10

Each consumer adopts v1.10 in a task that needs it; tasks already building finish on their version (build prompt 6.4). What each must change, as its card's lines (v1.10 changes no code):

- **The action layer's safety record** (C-104 safety and hand-offs, `actions/src/domain/safety/record.ts`; C-102 messages and consent; C-087; C-074's check; the action-layer engineer; T-1374). **Change:** answer v1.10's two fields on every `recordSafetyOutcome` answer the core makes: `safetyLine.recipientHandleId` from the first line row, and `safetyRows` from the commit's `writes.messages`, each row's id, `recipientHandleId` and `sendClass` and whether the line or the telling port wrote it; on a repeat (`repeatAnswer`, which today reads only whether the line's row was written: `outboxRowWritten`), the first record's rows with their stored recipients and classes (default 175): the line's ids from the event id, then the ids the telling port's repeat names, each read through open gap 147's K-05 read, a row not written left out; check the answer by v1.10's `recordSafetyOutcome.response` (C-074's check, as composeInput's is by v1.6). **Proves it with:** both accepted fixtures answered field for field (the crisis record's five rows; the threat's repeat naming Sam's two rows), and none of the refused answers given. **Depends on:** open gap 147's K-05 minor version and its `db/repo` read, both merged.
- **The telling port** (C-104's hand-offs and pages; T-319): the rows it writes for a record (`TellPlan.writes.messages`) carry each recipient's handle and send class safety (default 176), and its repeat names them.
- **The handle actor** (C-046's safety read on arrival, `runtime/src/safety/arrival.ts` sendDanger and `types.ts` `RecordSafetyOutcomeResponse`; the runtime engineer; T-1375). **Change:** read the two fields; start one safety-only wake (`wakeSafety`) per distinct `recipientHandleId` among the `safetyRows` of send class safety, each with that recipient's row ids, never gated, never awaited, caught and kept in `pending`, as T-1352's wake is; a quick_reply row (the outage line's) is not the danger wake's. An answer without `safetyRows` (v1 to v1.9) keeps T-1352's one wake for the line's row. The run is K-05 v1.14's safety-only claim, which takes any recipient handle, an email handle among them.
- **QA** (C-140): `tests/contract/tool-api.v1.test.ts` selects entries by `version` (DL-125), so v1.10's fixtures join its generic checks now; v1.10's own part is QA's task (below).
- **The Tool API mock** (C-186): covers v1 to v1.4 until it is generated again (open gap 123).

**Contract test, v1.10 (QA, its own task).** The mends of the three refused fixtures, each giving `j3-safety-arrival-crisis-rows.json`; what the schema leaves to tests (the line's row and recipient among the `line` rows, unique row ids, a repeat's rows equal to its record's: `j3-safety-arrival-threat-repeat.json` against `j3-safety-arrival-threat.json`'s line row); and a call with no turn naming a row, refused (`j3-safety-page-request.json`'s answer with a row added).

### Open gaps, v1.10

144. **Where a writer's danger line goes (CQ-313 question 2).** T-307 builds the line's rows by the reach card on file (`messages-consent.ts` `contactsOf`, about lines 338-378: the card's way, mobile and email; else `to.via`, the handle the text came from) and adds an email copy for a card with an email (`outboxRows`, about 433-443), while `actions/src/domain/safety/ports.ts` (about 141-145, `SafetyLineChoice.to`) and `commit/core/lines.ts`'s default 2 say the line goes "back on the handle the text came from". So a writer's line can go to another of their handles, or by email only for the email way. The sources: RULES 4.8 ("Every message goes their way, fallbacks too"; "an email gets every message too"); arch 02 line 125 ("Each row goes the person's way, iMessage first for texts, email only for the email way"; "A failed safety line retries every 10 s for 15 minutes and goes at once every other way the person allows"); arch 02 line 111 and RULES 4.12 (the outcome step's checked message first, at any hour, past any opt-out). None of them says the line goes back on the handle the text came from when the card names another, or that the email way leaves that handle out. **Answered for the contract:** v1.10 names every row with its recipient (default 174), so the danger wake reaches the card's other mobile and the email handle as well as the texter's, and no further K-09 change is needed whichever way the routing is ruled. **Default for the routing, as built:** the reach card's way, with its email copy; with no card, the handle the text came from. **Proposed:** the architect with the security reviewer and the action-layer engineer rule, with Jay if the sources stay silent, whether a writer's danger line also goes on the handle the text came from when the card picks another handle or the email way (the person in danger is at that handle now: RULES 4.12, "the safety message first"); if so, T-307's builder adds that row in the record's commit, and `safetyRows` names it like any other. T-1374 and T-1375 build on the default meanwhile.
145. **A safety row to a person told whose phone is stopped.** Default 176 makes a record's alert and page rows send class safety, and limit 8 sends every safety row to a stopped phone with the opt-out override (T-1352's rulings: limit 8 sends a safety row to a stopped phone), as `messages-consent.ts` builds one for a stopped phone that has texted Rosie (its default 4). The sources say the outcome step's checked message goes "past any opt-out" (arch 02 line 111) and that a person is told at once (RULES 4.12), but not whether the owner's or a team member's own STOP (RULES 7.2; a work phone's, RULES 7.7) holds their alert back. Default: as limit 8 does for every safety row, it goes. Proposed: once it is ruled, K-07's next version states limit 8's override, or its limit, for the person told. Round 2: the security reviewer could not confirm the default from the sources (its round 1 note a: arch 02 line 111's "past any opt-out" covers the outcome step's checked message to the writer, and RULES 4.1 and 1.8 do not say whether an owner's or a team member's own STOP holds back a safety alert), so the gap stays open and the lead routes it to Jay (CQ-316; DL-751).
146. **Rows to others written by a record carried in a turn.** v1.10 adds the rows to `recordSafetyOutcome`'s own answer, the arrival record's, which the danger wake reads. A record carried as a core action of a turn answers inside `gateAndCommit`'s core result, which stays v1.9's (v1.1's shape, no `safetyRows`), and the turn's commit wake names only the committing handle (T-1358's finding). A danger text's arrival record comes first, so the batch turn's record of it answers repeated and writes nothing (defaults 5 and 36); but a record whose first write is in a turn (for example a text whose arrival record was not written) writes its hand-off's alert and page rows there, and they wait for their recipients' next runs. Default: as today (nothing is live: the telling port answers `notBuilt`). Proposed: a K-09 minor version whose core result for `recordSafetyOutcome` carries v1.10's answer (`gateAndCommit`'s committed answer restated, as v1.8 restated it), and a runtime card that wakes those recipients from the turn's commit.
147. **The K-05 read a repeat's rows need** (round 2; the action-layer engineer's round 1 FAIL; DL-751). Default 175 names a repeat's rows with the recipient and send class their stored rows hold, and no frozen K-05 call returns those for a safety row: `readOutboxRowWritten` (K-05 v1.4, `data-access.v1.4.schema.json` about lines 1386-1411; `SafetyReader.outboxRowWritten`, `ports.ts` line 95), the read `repeatAnswer` makes today, answers only `written`; `readOutboxRow` (v1.3) returns a row's `recipient.handleId` (`rowRecipient`, about 897-903) but answers a row of send class safety with `fixedLineRow` alone (about 1151-1167, 1230, 1372); and v1.14 adds only the claim's `sendClass`. **Needs:** K-05's next free minor version (v1.15 while v1.14 is the newest frozen: `contracts/data-access/frozen.json`), only adding: `readOutboxRowWritten.response` with two optional fields a v1.15 call always returns, `recipientHandleId` (`outbox_rows.recipient_handle_id`, a handle's id, never its number or address) and `sendClass` (`outbox_rows.send_class`), each null exactly when `written` is false. The read still selects nothing of the row's words (`refuse-outbox-row-written-returns-words` stays refused) and keeps v1.4's role and scope (K-05 README item 41), so that card checks that every row a record names is seen under the call's business, a team page's rows as T-319 writes them among them. Its fixtures: a written line row, its email copy and an alert, each with its recipient and class, and a row not written, with both null. Then the database engineer's `db/repo` read returns the two columns, and the action layer's `SafetyReader` takes them (T-1374). One read per row by its id, as today's, for the handful of rows a record writes. **Who:** the lead cards the K-05 version for the architect and its `db/repo` read for the database engineer; T-1374 depends on both. **Meanwhile:** no default: until both merge, T-1374 cannot answer a repeat's v1.10 fields, and a repeat's rows are never rebuilt from the reach card in their place (default 175; section "Round 2").

### Round 2 (DL-751): a repeat's rows, read from the stored rows

The first verification judged 8a7b0e1: QA, the runtime engineer, the QA and eval engineer and the security reviewer PASS; the action-layer engineer FAIL on repeated answers, since default 175 read a repeat's rows "from the stored rows" and no frozen K-05 call returns a stored safety row's recipient and send class (open gap 147). The lead's round 2 rulings (`ops/cards/T-1373.md`) asked for one of two ways, with sources: (a) keep default 175 and name the K-05 read it needs, with the minor version it takes; or (b) rebuild a repeat's rows from what the record and the reach card give, with no stored-row read, if the sources allow it and the ids stay the first record's.

**Chosen: (a).** The sources do not allow (b):

- **A repeat returns the first record.** K-09 v1 says of `recordSafetyOutcome`: "one outcome row per text, and a repeat returns the first" (`tool-api.v1.schema.json`; `record.ts` default 1), and a v1.10 first answer names each row with the recipient and class written, so a repeat's answer is those. After the commit only the stored rows hold them: K-34 v1 `safety_outcomes` keeps the text's handle, person, business, booking and turn, never a row's recipient (`ports.ts` `StoredOutcome`).
- **What T-307 builds the line from can change before the repeat.** A repeat comes later: the batch turn's record after the arrival record (defaults 5 and 36), or a re-read applied again (T-1352's case 9). In between, the reach card T-307 routes by can change where the person's messages go (RULES 4.8: "Every message goes their way"; `moveUpdates`, A7-98, moves them to a new contact), and a threat's line follows the owner's handle as read at the time (`record.ts` `lineRows`, about lines 447-454; K-05 v1.4 `readBusinessOwner`, which a hand-over, S11-100, changes). A rebuilt row keeps its id (`ports.ids.rowId("line", eventId)`, `emailCopyId`), but its recipient may not be the stored row's, and a rebuilt list may leave out a row that was written (an email copy to an email the card no longer holds); `readOutboxRowWritten` can tell whether an id was written, never to whom it goes.
- **A wrong recipient wakes nothing.** A safety run claims only its named recipient's safety rows (K-05 v1.14 item 67, default 134; `dispatcher.ts` `isSafetyRun`, about lines 298-309), and a repeated answer is the danger wake's only retry (T-1352's ruling), so a rebuilt recipient that is not the stored row's would wake a run that claims nothing of that row, and the person told would wait for their own next run, against RULES 4.12 (T-1358's finding).

So v1.10's schema and fixtures stand unchanged: `j3-safety-arrival-threat-repeat.json` names the first record's rows (the threat's line and the alert, both to `handle-sam`), the rows a read of the stored rows gives. Default 175 now names the ids a repeat reads and the read, and open gap 147 the K-05 minor version it takes; T-1374 depends on it. The telling port naming its rows on a first answer and on a repeat stays T-1374's handoff (T-319; "Who reads v1.10"). Open gap 145 stays open, and the lead routes it to Jay (CQ-316).

## v1.11 (T-1471): S1-04's buttons question and the turn that answers it

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): the S1-04 design ruling (`ops/reviews/s1-04-design/check-merge.json`, its card 1; DL-999), which answers CQ-122 (2). S1-04 ("Which of your kinds of work share one company, with one tax number?": MASTER-FLOW-A lines 88 to 100) asks with three buttons, "Reply 1 Some share one, 2 All share one, 3 Each is its own" (line 97), and is no split: master-A.json gives it no branches, so the bundle holds no branch, Means line or card for it, the frontier (C-075) offers no choice, decide (C-115) has nothing to pick (K-02's `optionOffered`), and the S1-04 question a test phone holds on staging cannot be answered. v1.2 default 86 already rules this case for four other steps (a question asked with options at a step with no split gets its option choices from a table in K-09), and open gap 88 names the K-01 minor version that later retires such a table. Default 86's stakes, high for the reason unknown, cannot be taken over: limit 1's sign-up rule passes only a choice with no stake (`actions/src/gate/limit-1/allowed.ts`, default 14 (e)), so every S1-04 answer would be refused. And nothing yet said what the answering turn writes: S1-04's company rows, S1-08's plan row, S1-09's question. **Kind:** additive, a minor version, and it changes no shape. `tool-api.v1.11.schema.json` holds every v1.10 definition under its own name, each by `$ref` to `tool-api.v1.10.schema.json`, which stays as it is, so every v1 to v1.10 exchange is a v1.11 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks every earlier accepted fixture against v1.11) and a consumer on v1.10 keeps working: nothing is renamed, removed or changed in meaning. No call, key, error or definition is added. What v1.11 adds is the numbered defaults below, the fixtures that hold them and two named rules. **Scope:** S1-04, S1-08 and S1-09's question in flow A, in a turn; the choices are built for bundles v1 and v2 (S1-04 is byte-identical in both: the ruling's decision (g)). **Not here:** any code (each consumer moves in its own card: "Who reads v1.11"); K-01, K-02 and K-03, which do not change; K-05's versions for a row's own step and line (the ruling's cards 3 to 7) and for the party of a second company (card 17); S1-09's answer (T-635); stored options and "Reply 1 ..., 2 ..." on the question (cards 20 and 21).

### Defaults (T-1471), numbered after T-1373's

179. **S1-04's three option choices: default 86's shape, with two stated differences** (card 1 item 1; the ruling's decision (a)). For a live open question sent to the turn's handle whose step, in the bundle the question was asked under (K-04 `open_questions.bundle_hash` and `bundle_build`), is no split, has no card and carries buttons, and for which default 180's table has an entry, the frontier builder (C-075) offers one K-02 option choice per button, in the buttons' order: `question` the open question's id, `step` its step, `label` and `branch` the button's words, word for word, standing in for the branch label the bundle lacks, `definition` and `fields` as the table gives them, `target` the question's, and `choiceId` an id the frontier gives as it gives any option choice its id (`c-01`), never a stored option's id, since the question stores none today (default 187). The question is among the context's open questions; the context carries no step card for it (a K-01 v1 card is a split's), so decide reads it by its labels and definitions and answers it only by choosing one of the three, naming the question's id (default 86's rule; arch 03 lines 181, 183, 187). **Difference 1, stakes:** each choice's stakes are those of its own walk, built as a split branch's are (K-02 README "Stakes"): the step's gives, then the walk from the step's next to the first step that asks, waits or ends (arch 03 line 191), never default 86's "high, unknown". For S1-04 the walk gives `companies`, passes S1-08, which only tells, and stops at S1-09's question on bundle v2, or ends at S1-08 on bundle v1, which does not hold it: low with no reason both times, so limit 1's sign-up rule can pass the answer. A walk that meets a high-stakes effect gives high with that reason, as for any branch. **The bundle the stakes walk runs on** is the bundle the question was asked under, the one the buttons are read from, so one question's labels, order and stakes come from one bundle, as the frontier walks a split's options today (`actions/src/frontier/sources/open-questions/open-questions.ts`, `ownStakes` on the question's bundle); never the turn's pinned bundle, which the commit's walk reads (default 181). The two walks can differ only in the steps after the question's step (default 188 keeps the step itself), and a later cut whose walk on meets a stake the question's bundle did not is open gap 162. **Difference 2, the option list:** the buttons of the question's step in the bundle the question was asked under, not K-04 `open_questions.options`, since the walk asks a plain step with its options empty today (the question a phone already holds stores none); the turn that answers may pin a later bundle, and the frontier's labels still come from the question's own, which default 188 holds equal to the turn's. A buttons step with no table entry is not offered and is logged without its labels (fail closed). Default 86 stands unchanged for its own four steps. As the question goes out today it carries no numbered options (cards 20 and 21 add them), so a tester answers in words; a bare number counts only when it clearly answers the one open question (arch 03 line 187).
180. **The table** (card 1 item 2; `BUTTONS_OPTIONS` in `tool-api.rules.mjs` holds the same values). A field is listed on the choice as a K-01 `answerField` (name and type) and is given by decide only when the owner's words hold it, with those words (K-02 `fieldValue`; arch 03 line 183; limit 6 re-parses it): `shared_kinds` is the kinds that share one company, in the owner's own words ("Plumbing and electrical"), which code, never the model, matches to the owner's picks (default 182); `company_name` is the name the owner gives that company ("Lee Home Services"). An answer with no `company_name` takes the name default (default 183); "Some share one" with no `shared_kinds` is refused (default 184). The master prints a Means line only for "Each is its own" (MASTER-FLOW-A line 95); the other two definitions are this contract's declared words, taken from the question itself, as default 107 declared one, and stand until Jay answers (open gap 148).

| Step  | Option as sent (`label` and `branch`) | `definition`                                                                                        | Its words come from                             | `fields`                                     |
| ----- | ------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------- |
| S1-04 | 'Some share one'                      | 'some of the kinds of work share one company, with one tax number, and each other kind is its own.' | declared here, a default (open gap 148)         | `shared_kinds` (text), `company_name` (text) |
| S1-04 | 'All share one'                       | 'every kind of work shares one company, with one tax number.'                                       | declared here, a default (open gap 148)         | `company_name` (text)                        |
| S1-04 | 'Each is its own'                     | 'no two kinds of work share a tax number.'                                                          | the master's Means line (MASTER-FLOW-A line 95) | none                                         |

181. **The walk of the turn that answers** (card 1 item 3; `actions/src/commit/walk`). The walk reads the turn's pinned flow graph alone (`writes.ts`: "The walk reads the pinned bundle's flow graphs alone"), never the bundle the question was asked under: the question row it reads to close (`LiveQuestion`) carries no bundle id. An option choice whose step, in the turn's pinned graph, is not a split, and whose `branch` is one of that step's button labels in that graph, word for word, is committed in three moves, in this order, in the turn's one commit. (1) **The close:** the open question is closed answered at the version its row is read at on the call's transaction, never a version the request carries (the walk's contract, `writes.ts` default 11: the live question the option names, sent to the turn's handle, at the chosen step, of the call's business; K-05 v1 `openQuestionClose`: `expectVersion` that version, `status` answered, `answeredOptionId` null, since none was stored: default 187), and the walk names that row at that version in its versions read. No field of the request carries a version the frontier read, so there is none to compare: a question that is no longer live when the walk reads it is refused with nothing written (the walk's `questionNotLive`; limit 4 answers the same of a question no longer live); a live question that moved since the frontier read it closes at the version it has now; and a question that moves between the walk's read and the commit's write is refused by the commit's own compare, with nothing written (K-05 `versionChanged`). A branch that is no button of the step in the turn's pinned graph is refused with nothing written; default 188 says why the frontier's labels, read from the question's bundle (default 179), are the turn's. (2) **The step's write:** the step is written as answered, its gives through its bound writer (S1-04's: defaults 182 to 184) and its own message not again; a step that gives a key with no writer bound is refused before anything is read. (3) **The walk on,** from the step's next (arch 03 line 191). S1-08 lays out the plan and asks nothing ("needs companies pf.rules, gives" nothing: line 111; no write of its own: "Each step of journeys 1 and 2"), so its writer returns one outbox row and no state: to the turn's handle and person, under the choice's business, on the turn's service (default 132), send class `quick_reply` (default 133), no booking and no question (`openQuestionId` null), written only when the stop home lets Rosie text this owner (default 189). The walk stops at S1-09, the first step that asks: its open question (the turn's handle and person, the choice's business, no options, no read-back, `clock` null, since S1-09 prints no Clock line and step 9's default holds), its clock row, the commit's own (default 134), and its outbox row, which names the question. S1-10 is never read. Ids follow default 131: `outbox-s1-08-<turn key>-1`, `oq-s1-09-<turn key>-1`, `clock-oq-s1-09-<turn key>-1`, `outbox-s1-09-<turn key>-1`. The part gives `companies` (`STEP_GIVES`), and the answer names the closed and the opened question (`openQuestions.closed`, `opened`). **`written`** names every versioned row the commit writes, each once at the version it has after the write, the closed question among them, and a row keyed by two columns by K-05 v1.18 item 77's name, which the database builds (`<company id>/<kind id>`, `<pick id>/<pf key>`, `<draft id>/<block>`: `db/repo/setup/row-names.ts`); the fixtures list them in the order `db/repo` answers them (the state rows as stated, the question opened, the question closed, the clock row: `db/repo/commit-turn.ts` default 2), an order K-05 does not freeze, so a consumer compares the list as a set (open gap 150). On a bundle that does not hold S1-08 (bundle v1) the walk ends at the missing step as it does today; S1-08's row and S1-09's question need bundle v2 (card 8). S1-08's row asks no question, so K-05's row read gives it no master line until K-05 holds a row's own step and line (cards 3 to 7; K-05 open gap 29): that is K-05's, not this version's.
182. **What S1-04's writer writes, and in what order** (card 1 item 4; the ruling's decision (c)). The target is v1's `SetupS1_04`: companies, each with a name and its picks. The writer is read-only and returns rows; the commit writes them. **Kept picks:** the caller's person's own K-24 picks (`picked_kinds.owner_person_id`) whose outcome is listed or new_work, this turn's and those on file; a not_booked pick is never placed (K-24 `picked_kinds_not_booked_unplaced`). A kept pick that is already placed is default 190's. **Groups:** "Each is its own" puts each kept pick alone; "All share one" puts all together; "Some share one" puts the picks `shared_kinds` names together, at least two, each word matching exactly one kept pick (default 190 says what a word is matched against), and each other pick alone. **The company under way:** the group that holds the first kind named (the kept pick of the lowest `position`; S1-02 sets the first kind named up first) takes the company the turn already runs under, the caller's person's own company whose business is the call's business; every other group is a new company (open gap 151). **Rows, in the order the foreign keys need:** (1) K-23 `companies`: each new company inserted with its id (`company-s1-04-<turn key>-<n>`, n from 1 in the groups' order, in default 131's form), `owner_person_id` the caller's person, its name (default 183) and `business_id` null, since a company gets its business at its own S1-09 (line 93: "A separate company is a second business on the same login"); the company the turn runs under is updated, at the version read, only when the owner named it. (2) K-23 `company_kinds`: one per placed pick whose company and kind are not on file together, `business_id` copied from its company, `trade_name` null (line 92's trade names are a later card), `pf_facts` and the two switch lists the kind's own from K-24's catalog by its id (`kinds.facts`, `kind_flows.switches_on`; K-05 README's table for S1-04); a drafted (new work) kind's from its draft as it stands (K-05 v1.19 `readPickedKinds`: `draft.blocks`' `factsOn`, `switchesOn` and `outcome`), never empty lists: `pf_facts` is the protective facts, which start on (MASTER-FLOW-A lines 45 and 46: "Protective facts start on: contracts, crews and forms", "Only the business's clear no turns a protective fact off. Silence or a skipped block keeps it on"; K-24 README "Drafts"), under their live names in K-26's registry, `documents` (the master merged `contracts` into it: FRONTEND-SPEC line 563) and `forms`, in that order, then each answered block's `factsOn` in block order, each name once, and `pf_switches_a` and `pf_switches_b` are both the answered blocks' `switchesOn` in block order, each once (K-24 `new_work_draft_blocks.switches_on`: "in both flows"). With no block answered, which is every draft the walk makes today (S1-03's writer drafts it with no block), that is `["documents", "forms"]` and two empty switch lists. With some answered, the answered blocks add their facts and switches, a block with a clear no adds nothing, and both protective facts stay on: no frozen source says which block's no turns which protective fact off (K-24 README: "the protective-fact rule is code's"), so until the checklist's card names it the writer keeps them on, the closed side, since a fact kept on never hides its question. `crews` has no live name in K-26's registry, which refuses a merged name like any unknown (K-26 README rule 3), so it is written under no name (open gap 163). A drafted kind stays offline until Rosie's team approves its draft, so no live booking reads these lists before a person has checked them. A pair already on file is kept as it is, never written again (staging's roster business is seeded with a barber kind, which stays). (3) K-24 `picked_kinds`: each kept pick updated at the version read with its `company_id`, and its `business_id` where its company has one. (4) Then the rows of a pick that gained a business, `picked_kind_texts`, `new_work_drafts` and `new_work_draft_blocks`: `business_id` set, each at the version read. **No event-log entry for these rows:** the writer returns none (`stateEvents` empty), as S1-02's and S1-11's writers return none, since a state row's entry is its writer's (only the domain knows its kind: `actions/src/commit/types.ts` `UnitWrites`) and K-08 v1.1 has no kind for a `companies`, `company_kinds` or `picked_kinds` row or for a pick's text and draft rows (QUESTIONS.md CQ-56: K-08 adds each kind with the version that first writes it; open gap 164). The commit writes its own entries for the rows it builds, the questions, the clock row and the outbox rows, as for any turn (`actions/src/commit/build.ts`; T-305). **Whose rows a signer's answer may write:** only the caller's person's own picks, their text and draft rows, the company that person owns under the call's business, and new companies owned by that person with no business; never another person's row, and never a new company under a business. `commit-s1-04.json` and `commit-s1-04-some-share.json` state these rows field for field, and `buttonsAnswerCommit` holds them.
183. **A company's name** (card 1 item 4; K-23 `companies.name` is not null). The name the owner gives (`company_name`) is the shared group's company's name: a new company is inserted with it, and when that group takes the company the turn runs under, that company's name is updated to it. With no name given, the company the turn runs under keeps its stored name, and a new company takes the owner's own S1-02 words for its group's first kind (`picked_kinds.words`, such as "plumbing"), until its S1-09 reads the listing. "Each is its own" carries no field, so each of its new companies is named this way. A default until Jay answers (open gap 152).
184. **The refusals, and no second company until the party version is live** (card 1 item 4; the ruling's "a second company makes the phone undecided"). The writer refuses the part with nothing returned, so nothing is written and the question stays open, when: a `shared_kinds` word fits no kept pick, or two (default 190); the shared group holds fewer than two picks; the words name a second sharing group; two picks of one kind would land in one company (K-23 `company_kinds`' key); the owner has no kept pick; the caller's person owns no company whose business is the call's business (an admin, say, who answers under another person's company: default 182 lets an answer write only the caller's own person's rows, so with no such company there is none for the first group to take, and the writer never puts that group in another person's company or in a new one); a kept pick is already placed (default 190); or a read it needs fails (it fails closed, never read as "none"). And it refuses **any answer that would write a second company while the binding's flag says the party rule is not live.** Why: K-05 v1.11 item 52 (default 98) answers a handle whose person holds a business beside a company with no business yet as several businesses, so no later turn would run for that phone and its STOP would not be recorded (K-05 open gap 57). The flag is handed in by the binding and is off unless the caller sets it; staging sets it only after K-05's party version and its `db/repo` read are live (cards 17 to 19; open gap 149). Until then only an answer whose groups come to one company is saved, "All share one" in practice, and `commit-s1-04-some-share.json` shows the rows the writer returns with the flag on. Asking "which?" about an unclear word is a later card (open gap 153). What `gateAndCommit` answers for a refused part is open gap 157.
185. **The one-kind rule and its count** (card 1 item 5; CQ-359's default, now stated; the ruling's decision (d)). S1-04 is "Asked only when Sam names more than one kind of work" (line 90). The count is every kept pick the owner has (default 182): this answer's and those on file; a not_booked pick does not count. With exactly one, S1-04 is not asked: the walk settles it through bound code (a buttons step that bound code settles is walked, not asked), the one kind's company is the company the turn runs under, the pick takes its `company_id` and `business_id` on its own insert with its `company_kinds` row inserted before it (a commit locks and compares before it writes, so it cannot update a pick it inserts: the ruling's reading of `db/repo/commit-turn.ts`, proven in card 23), the step gives `companies`, and the same walk writes S1-08's row and asks S1-09. With more than one, S1-04 is asked. Until cards 22 to 25 ship, S1-04 is asked whatever the count. A default until Jay answers (open gap 154).
186. **S1-08's four term lines** (card 1 item 6; the ruling's decision (h); narrows default 137 for this one step). S1-08's plan card (MASTER-FLOW-A line 109; master-A.json S1-08) mixes Rosie's own fixed lines with the sample story's: "No extra agreement for your work" and "Then the barbershop, live in about 15 minutes" are Sam's story, untrue for another trade (Master Corrections C6), and "Then Lee Home Services: plumbing, then electrical, about 5 minutes each" carries the sample company's name, a slot. Default 137 would make the first two term facts, since they name no slot. So on S1-08 a card line is a term fact only when it is one of these four, word for word, at its place on the card (K-01 `flows.A.steps.S1-08.say[0].elements.card.lines[n]`; `S1_08_TERM_LINES` in `tool-api.rules.mjs`): line 1 (`lines[0]`) "Your business and your ID: about 3 minutes"; line 2 (`lines[1]`) "Any license or document you want shown (optional)"; line 6 (`lines[5]`) "Then your people"; line 7 (`lines[6]`) "Nothing you tell me is asked twice". Every other S1-08 line gives none, and one of the four that the row's bundle words otherwise gives none either. Each fact's source line follows default 138 (`S1-08's card, line 1`, with its K-01 path). A withheld line is never replaced by made-up words: the plan the owner gets is shorter and never untrue. Finalize reads the same rule for its terms (card 15). The owner's own plan lines, built from their kinds and companies, wait for the CTO's answer to CQ-86, which stays open (open gaps 125 and 156). No compose-input fixture for S1-08 is frozen here: `termFactsFromCard` reads bundle v0, which does not hold S1-08, and no bundle holds S1-08's compiled card until bundle v2 (card 8), on which card 14 proves this default.
187. **Option ids, for the later options card** (card 1 item 7; card 21). When a question asked with buttons stores its options as sent, each option's id is `<step>/<n>`, n from 1 in the order sent (`S1-04/1` to `S1-04/3`), as K-04's own seed writes them (`core/fixtures/seed-open-question.json`: `A7-03/1`); never `<question id>-o<n>`, which has no source. An answer to a question that holds options then closes it with the option whose label is the chosen branch (`answeredOptionId`); a question stored with none closes with null, as default 181 has it. The choices' `choiceId`s stay the frontier's own until that card rules otherwise.
188. **One step's buttons across bundle cuts** (round 2; DL-961 ruling 1; the ruling's decision (g)). The frontier reads a buttons question's labels from the bundle the question was asked under (default 179), and the walk reads them from the turn's pinned graph (default 181): the request carries no bundle id for the question, and the walk's question read gives none. What holds the two readings together is a rule on bundle cuts: while a question asked at a step under an earlier bundle may still be open, a later cut keeps that step's buttons byte for byte, their labels and their order, as bundle v1 kept S1-01 and S1-02 byte for byte "so a question asked under v0 keeps its options" (DL-961 ruling 1) and as bundle v2 keeps v1's six steps, S1-04 among them (decision (g)). So the label a choice carries is a button of its step in the turn's graph, and the walk's check passes. The cut's own check holds the rule, as bundle v1's states that S1-01 and S1-02 equal v0's byte for byte (DL-965). Where a cut breaks it (a button reworded, dropped or put in another place while an earlier question is open), the choice's `branch` is no button of the step in the turn's graph, and the walk refuses the part with nothing written: the question stays open and is offered again with the labels it was asked with, never a guess at which new button an old one became (fail closed; open gap 161).
189. **S1-08's row and the stop home** (round 2; T-633's `actions/src/tool-api/journey-1/kinds/writers.ts` default 12; DL-878's handoff 1). S1-08's writer writes its row only when the stop home lets Rosie text the owner, as S1-03's and S1-74's writers write theirs: the owner is read with `readStop` on the stop reads the binding hands in (the runtime side's, since `rosie_app` reads no handles, person_handles or phones: DL-982), and `mayText` rules for the call's business, the turn's own handle and a quick reply, the turn counting as texting in when its decided texts hold a text. "texts": the row of default 181. "noTexts": no S1-08 row, and the walk goes on. With no stop reads bound the writer answers notBuilt and the part is refused with nothing written (fail closed). So S1-08's writer reads the stop home's rows, and nothing else: the ruling's card 13 said "It reads no table" before T-633 merged, and T-1482 takes this default instead. The opt-out line is finalize's, from the same phones row, never the writer's (T-633 default 12). The fixtures hold the "texts" case: each owner texts in from their own number, with no stop and no withdrawn yes. S1-09's question and its row are the walk's own (default 181), written as the walk writes S1-02's and S1-04's questions today, with no stop read of the walk's; whether the walk must make that read is open gap 165.
190. **What a `shared_kinds` word is matched against, and a kept pick already placed** (round 2; the ruling's decision (c): "each word matching exactly one kept pick"). No source says what a word is compared with or how, so the rule is this contract's own, and it fails closed. The words are the field's source words (K-02 `fieldValue.words`, the exact words of the turn's text, never the model's `value`), as S1-02's writer reads `kinds`, and they are read as the kind matcher reads words (T-632's `words`: lower case, function words such as "and" and bare numbers dropped, plurals folded: `actions/src/domain/kind-matcher/words.ts`). Each word left is compared with both names of every kept pick, read the same way: the pick's own words (`picked_kinds.words`, what the owner typed at S1-02) and its kind's name (`kinds.name`; a drafted kind's name is the owner's words for it). A word names a pick when it meets a word of either name by the matcher's own rule (`meets`: the same word, one starting the other, or the same first five letters), so "plumbing" names a pick typed "plumbing" and a pick of the kind named Plumber. Every word must name exactly one kept pick: a word that names none, or names two picks, refuses the part (default 184); several words may name one pick; and the shared group is the picks named, at least two. The catalog is not read again: S1-04 groups picks the owner already made and never picks a kind. A not_booked pick is no kept pick and is never matched. **A kept pick already placed** (its `company_id` not null when the writer reads it: S1-04 answered again, say, or a pick the one-kind path of default 185 placed on its insert) has no source. Moving it could carry its rows from one business to another, which K-05's commit refuses (`db/repo/commit-turn.ts` default 16), and leaving it where it is would let the rows differ from the groups the owner just named. So the writer refuses the whole part with nothing written when any kept pick is already placed, and the question stays open (default 184; open gap 166).

Also settled: open gap 159 is closed by the AI engineer's review (below), and the fixtures' notes and index entries say what the fixtures now hold.

### Named rules, v1.11

`tool-api.rules.mjs` adds two, under new names (rules are only ever added), and two tables they read, `BUTTONS_OPTIONS` (default 180) and `S1_08_TERM_LINES` (default 186). Each rule reads only the exchange it is handed.

- **`buttonsOptionChoices`:** on a buildFrontier exchange answered with a context, each open question at a step of `BUTTONS_OPTIONS` is offered exactly the table's option choices, in the buttons' order: `label` and `branch` the button's words, `definition` and `fields` the table's, `step` the question's, and stakes low with no reason, which is what the choice's own walk gives on bundles v1 and v2 (default 179); and the context carries no step card for that step. The schema cannot see the table, so this rule alone refuses a reworded label, another definition, a field the table does not give and default 86's stakes.
- **`buttonsAnswerCommit`:** on a gateAndCommit exchange whose stated step is a step of `BUTTONS_OPTIONS`, exactly one part answers that step with one of its buttons, committed and giving the step's keys (`STEP_GIVES`); its fields are the table's for that option; the answer closes the question it answers; and `given.stated.writes` states the walk's rows: S1-08's row, which names no question, then S1-09's question, opened with no options, and its row (default 181). For S1-04 the stated state rows are held to defaults 182 to 184: foreign-key order; every kept pick placed by an update that sets its company; exactly one group in the company the turn runs under, whose picks and rows take the caller's business; every other group in a new company owned by the caller's person with no business; a company kind only for a company a pick is placed in, with no trade name; the groups the label means; and a name written only where the owner gave one. It holds the case where Rosie may text the owner, the only one frozen: an exchange in which the stop home rules "noTexts", so S1-08's row is not written (default 189), is not a v1.11 fixture, and one frozen later brings its own rule under a new name. The rule does not read `written` or the limits' results.

### Fixtures, v1.11

Accepted (3). `build-frontier-s1-04.json` (rule `buttonsOptionChoices`): Sam answers S1-04's question after `commit-s1-02.json`, and the frontier is the three option choices alone, each low with no reason, with no card and no fact. `commit-s1-04.json` (rule `givesStepKeys`): the roster world, staging's (`db/seeds/roster-owner.ts`), an owner who named plumbing and electrical and answers in words that mean "All share one": the question closed, both picks placed in the company the turn runs under with its business, a company kind for each, the seeded barber kind kept, the first pick's 11 text rows given the business, no companies row, S1-08's row and S1-09's question; this is the answer the shortest path saves. `commit-s1-04-some-share.json` (rule `givesStepKeys`): Sam's own answer, the master's (lines 90 and 98), "Some share one" with `shared_kinds` and `company_name`: one new company, Lee Home Services, with no business, plumbing and electrical placed in it, and the barbershop, the first kind named, in the company the turn runs under; the rows the writer returns once the flag of default 184 is on. Each states its part's rows in `given.stated.writes` (default 136), and its `written` names every versioned row the commit writes, a two-column row by K-05 item 77's name (default 181; open gap 150). Each part's limits are what the gate answers: limits 1 to 4 pass and limit 5 is not checked in both; limit 6 is not checked in `commit-s1-04.json`, whose choose carries no field (`actions/src/gate/limit-6/re-parse.ts`: a choose with no field gives it no value, and only the sign-up door passes with none), and passes in `commit-s1-04-some-share.json`, whose two text fields it re-parses. Both hold the case where the stop home lets Rosie text the owner (default 189). The index names `givesStepKeys` on the two accepted commit fixtures, as it does on every accepted fixture that states a step of journey 1 or 2 (the contract test's standing check, `tests/contract/tool-api.v1.test.ts`); both also pass `buttonsAnswerCommit` (run on them 2026-11-20), which QA's part runs on them from then on (below), so each refused twin is refused for its one fault alone. Sam's text is the master's line 98 without its bare "1", since the question as asked today carries no numbered options. The fixtures carry the stand-in bundle id every K-09 fixture carries, and their labels are bundle v1's S1-04 buttons, which QA's contract test compares word for word. Refused (7), in `fixtures/invalid/`, each an accepted fixture with one fault that the schema takes and its rule alone refuses. Of `build-frontier-s1-04.json`: `refuse-s1-04-option-stakes-high.json` (default 86's stakes, high for the reason unknown), `refuse-s1-04-option-label-reworded.json` (the second label reworded) and `refuse-s1-04-option-field-not-in-table.json` ("Each is its own" carrying `company_name`). Of `commit-s1-04.json`: `refuse-s1-04-commit-question-left-open.json` (the closed list empty), `refuse-s1-04-commit-plan-row-asks.json` (S1-08's row naming S1-09's question) and `refuse-s1-04-commit-all-share-second-company.json` (a pick placed in a new company though the owner chose "All share one"). Of `commit-s1-04-some-share.json`: `refuse-s1-04-commit-new-company-with-business.json` (the new company written under the turn's own business). Each index entry names the value that makes it its accepted twin (checked 2026-11-20).

### Who reads v1.11

Each consumer adopts v1.11 in a task that needs it; tasks already building finish on their version (build prompt 6.4). v1.11 changes no code and no validator: a call checked by v1.10's definitions is checked the same by v1.11's.

- **The frontier builder** (C-075; the action-layer engineer; card 9, T-1478): defaults 179 and 180 and `build-frontier-s1-04.json`. It reads a step's button labels from the bundle the question was asked under, gives a plain step's answer its own stakes, walked on that same bundle, and offers the table's choices; a buttons step outside the table offers nothing.
- **The commit's walk** (C-087; the action-layer engineer; cards 10, 11 and later 21 and 22; T-1479, T-1480, T-1490, T-1491): default 181 and `commit-s1-04.json` up to row ids (the close at the version the row is read at on the call's transaction, the branch checked against the step's buttons in the turn's pinned graph, the step's write, S1-08's row with no question, S1-09's question and its row), default 188 for what a refused label means, default 187 for stored options, default 185 for the one-kind path.
- **S1-04's writer** (the action-layer engineer; card 12, T-1481, and card 23, T-1492): defaults 182 to 185 and 190; `commit-s1-04.json`'s state rows for an "All share one" answer and `commit-s1-04-some-share.json`'s with the flag on; every refusal of default 184; no event-log entry; a drafted kind's protective facts.
- **S1-08's writer** (the action-layer engineer; card 13, T-1482): default 181's S1-08 row, written only when the stop home lets Rosie text (default 189), so it takes the stop reads from its binding, as T-633's writers do.
- **The bundle cuts** (the playbook engineer; card 8 and every later cut): default 188, a step's buttons kept byte for byte while an earlier question at it may be open.
- **Compose input's term facts** (C-090, C-091; the action-layer engineer; card 14, T-1483) and finalize's terms (card 15, T-1484): default 186 and `S1_08_TERM_LINES`.
- **The bindings** (cards 15, 16 and 19; T-1484, T-1485, T-1488): default 184's flag, off unless the caller sets it.
- **Decide and the composer** (C-115, C-118; the AI engineer): the three choices with their definitions and fields and no step card (defaults 179 and 180); S1-08's row with only the four term facts (default 186).
- **QA** (C-140): `tests/contract/tool-api.v1.test.ts` selects entries by `version` (DL-125), so v1.11's ten fixtures join its generic checks now; v1.11's own part is QA's task (below).
- **The Tool API mock** (C-186): covers v1 to v1.4 until it is generated again (open gap 123).

**Contract test, v1.11 (QA, its own task: T-1496, the ruling's card 27).** Each new fixture against its definition and named rule; `buttonsAnswerCommit` passing on `commit-s1-04.json` and `commit-s1-04-some-share.json`, which the index names for `givesStepKeys`; every earlier accepted fixture accepted by v1.11; the mends of the seven refused fixtures, each giving its accepted twin (stakes low with no reason; the label "All share one"; no field; the question in the closed list; `openQuestionId` null on S1-08's row; both picks in the turn's company with no companies insert; `business_id` null on the new company); the two rules fired on damaged copies the schema still takes (a definition changed, the choices out of order, the state rows out of foreign-key order, a new company owned by another person, a pick left unplaced in its group's business); `build-frontier-s1-04.json`'s labels equal to S1-04's buttons in bundle v1 (`bundles/v1/bundle/flow/A.json`), word for word, failing on a copy with one label changed; `S1_08_TERM_LINES` equal to those lines of S1-08's card in the master's record; and each accepted commit fixture's `written` naming one row per versioned row of its stated writes, the closed question and the clock row, a two-column row by item 77's name. The damaged copies the security review named (a pick's or a text row's `set` carrying a column other than `company_id` and `business_id`) pass `buttonsAnswerCommit` as it stands, which holds what is set and not what else is: default 182 is exact in words, T-1496 adds the copies and reports what the rule answers, and a rule that refuses them, if the lead cards one, is added under a new name (rules are only ever added).

Each with its default where one is in use. For QUESTIONS.md, in these words:

K-02's `optionOffered` refuses only a field the choice does not carry, never a missing one (`contracts/model/model.rules.mjs`; `ai/src/decide/check.ts`), decide's prompt says to leave out a field the turn gives no words for (`ai/src/decide/prompt.ts`), and the gate takes a choose that leaves one out (`actions/src/gate/framework/checks.ts`), where limit 6 then has nothing to check (`commit-s1-04.json`). For the CTO, with the playbook engineer: a later bundle cut rewords, drops or moves a button of a step while a question asked at that step under an earlier bundle is still open. The frontier offers the old labels, the walk reads the new ones, and the answer is refused with nothing written, so that question can never be answered. What should happen to it: is it closed as withdrawn and asked again under the new bundle, does the walk read the question's own bundle (K-04 `open_questions.bundle_hash` and `bundle_build`, which the walk's question read would then carry), or may no cut ever change a button of a step that can hold an open question? For the action-layer engineer, with the architect: the frontier walks a buttons option's stakes on the question's bundle, and the commit walks the turn's pinned bundle. A later cut could add, after the question's step, a step with a high-stakes effect the question's bundle did not hold; the choice would be offered low and pass limit 1's sign-up rule. Should the stakes walk run on the turn's pinned bundle instead, for split questions as well (`open-questions.ts` reads the question's for both)? Default in use: the question's bundle, as today. It fails closed today: the walk writes only a step's message and its record writes through bound writers and answers notBuilt for any other effect (`writes.ts`), so such a turn writes nothing. On bundles v1 and v2 both walks give low.
