For Jay: the master's protective facts are contracts, crews and forms. `contracts` is `documents` today and `forms` is `forms`; which live fact, if any, did `crews` become? For the playbook engineer, with the architect: which block of S1-03's checklist turns which protective fact off with a clear no? No frozen contract says (K-24: "code's and the bundle's"). Defaults in use: a drafted kind's company kind is written with `documents` and `forms` on and nothing for `crews`; a clear no on any block turns neither off until the checklist's card names its block, so a protective question is never hidden; the kind stays offline until Rosie's team approves it.

Default in use: none is written (CQ-56's: K-08 adds each kind with the version that first writes it). The K-08 minor version that adds journey 1's setup kinds is not carded.
165. **S1-09's question when Rosie may not text** (default 189). For the action-layer engineer, with the security reviewer: S1-08's writer rules `mayText` and writes no row on "noTexts", but the question the walk stops at (S1-09's here, S1-02's and S1-04's before it) and its outbox row are the walk's own, written with no stop read. A stopped number's text gets only the guard's one fixed reply (`actions/src/homes/stop/stop.ts` default 4; RULES 7.3), so the case left is an owner who texts in after withdrawing their yes at that business. Must the walk rule `mayText` before it opens a question and writes its row, and what does it do on "noTexts": open the question with no row, or write nothing? Default in use: the walk as built, with no stop read of its own. Whether a later hop checks the stop again before the row is sent was not checked for this freeze.
166. **How a `shared_kinds` word is matched, and a kept pick already placed** (default 190). For Jay, through the action-layer engineer's card 12: no source says what the owner's words for the sharing kinds are compared with. Default in use: each word, read as the kind matcher reads words, must meet the owner's own S1-02 words or the kind's name of exactly one kept pick, else the answer is refused with nothing written (open gap 153 asks what the owner is then told). And when the owner answers S1-04 with a kind that already has a company (S1-04 asked again, or a kind added after the first was set up), may its company change? Default in use: no; the answer is refused with nothing written and the question stays open.

## After v1.11, with no new version (T-1499): code settles S1-02's branch

**Why** (DL-1014 and DL-1016; the ruling of DL-1018, `ops/reviews/s102-answer-ruling/`, its card 1; answers CQ-157 (2) and CQ-348 (3)): on staging an owner's typed answer to S1-02, "Barbershop, plumbing, electrical", got no reply. S1-02's three options differ only by whether each kind of work is on Rosie's list, and the model never sees that list. No contract said who turns the owner's words into one of the three: v1's `SetupS1_02` says each kind carries its outcome "after matchKinds", K-02's `decide-s1-02-choose.json` has both reads choose "All on the list" with the words, and S1-02's writer only refused a branch that did not fit the words (`branchNotWords`). The ruling gives the match to code. **Kind:** this README's text alone. No version, schema, named rule, fixture, index entry or `frozen.json` changes, and no frozen file is edited: the frozen rules hold one line for S1-02, its gives (`STEP_GIVES` in `tool-api.rules.mjs`), and the one frozen exchange that answers S1-02 already has this default's shape (`commit-s1-02.json`: c-01 named, every part listed, "All on the list" walked). **Why it sits here:** every earlier default is in its version's own section, read and passed by that version's judges and frozen with it. Default 191 comes after v1.11's freeze and belongs to no version, so it has its own section after the newest version's, and the next K-09 version that touches S1-02's answer restates it with fixtures (open gap 167). **Not here:** any code (each reader moves in its own card: "Who reads default 191"); the K-09 version for an asked part's reply (T-1504); what decide answers, which is K-02's (K-02 README default 16).

### Default (T-1499), numbered after T-1471's

191. **S1-02's answer: decide carries the owner's words, and code settles the branch.** Decide answers S1-02's question with one part: `choose`, the question's id, the option of that question with the lowest `choiceId`, and the owner's typed words in the `kinds` field (K-02 README default 16; `decide-s1-02-choose.json`). The option it names says nothing about Rosie's list. **The settler port.** The commit's walk (C-087) takes settlers from its binding, by step id, beside the question read and the step writers (`WalkDeps.settlers`, handed in as `questions` and `writers` are). A settler, as default 191 asks one, is read-only code for one split step: given the call's transaction and business scope, the step as the turn's pinned graph holds it, the passed part, its choice and the turn, it answers the label of the branch the turn takes at that step, or it refuses. It writes nothing. **Only at a split step.** Default 191 asks a settler only at a step whose branches the turn's pinned graph holds. A settler bound at a step with no branches is never asked under default 191. S1-04 is such a step: it is no split and asks with buttons (default 179), an S1-04 button answer is walked on its chosen label as default 181 says, and with one kind S1-04 is walked, not asked (default 185). T-1491 may bind S1-04's one-kind settler in the same place, `WalkDeps.settlers` by step id; its own version states when that settler is asked and what it answers, and default 191 says neither. **When the walk asks it.** The walk asks a settler only for a part the gate passed that chooses an option of the open question asked at a split step with a settler bound, and only when no hold keeps that part back. A part that an open safety hold or an unread text keeps back is answered `held` and never walked (`commit.ts` step 3, `hold.ts`), so nothing is read for it and the turn commits with that part held, as today. Its settler is not asked: a settler's refusal or failed read, like a writer's, is not caught where the walk is called (`commit.ts` line 367, `ports.writesOf`), so it would fail the whole call. For a part the walk does reach, it asks that settler once, before it walks: before it reads a branch from the graph and before it calls any writer. Of the choice decide named, the walk reads only what every option of that question shares, which is the question's id, the step and the target; it never reads that choice's `branch`, `label` or `choiceId` to find the branch. So the same words give the same branch whichever of the question's options a read named. **What the walk does with the answer.** The label the settler answers must be a branch of that step in the turn's pinned graph, word for word; one that is not is refused with nothing written (fail closed, as defaults 181 and 188 treat a label the graph does not hold; bundle v1 kept S1-02 byte for byte from v0 "so a question asked under v0 keeps its options", DL-961 ruling 1, so a question asked under v0 is settled by the turn's graph, and a later cut that rewords one of S1-02's branches fails closed here). From there the walk is the walk of a chosen branch as it is today, with the settled branch in the chosen one's place: the question is closed at the version its row is read at (default 181 (1)), the step's own writer writes its gives, each step of the settled branch is written by its writer, and the walk goes on to the first step that asks, waits or ends. Each writer is handed the settled branch as the branch chosen. The unit's why names the step and the settled branch, never the option decide named and never the person's words. A settler that refuses, or whose read fails, fails the call closed with nothing written, as a writer's refusal does today (open gap 157), and the question stays open; a failed read is never taken as a branch. **S1-02's settler** reads the owner's words as S1-02's writer reads them today: the `kinds` field's source words (K-02 `fieldValue.words`, the exact words of the turn's text, never the model's `value`), else the part's quote. By K-02 README default 16 those words, like the field's value, are the part's whole quote exactly as typed, never a list cut out of it (for "We do haircuts, beard trims and shaves", the whole text), so the settler splits that one span: into parts at commas, semicolons and line breaks, a part with no word left being no part, and words before the list staying in the first part (open gap 172), with one exception (T-1518; DL-1042, DL-1054; `actions/src/domain/kind-matcher/matcher.ts`, `partsIn`): a run of pieces next to each other, split at commas alone, of no more pieces than the catalog's most-cut name has (`nameParts`, the most parts `splitParts` cuts one catalog kind's name into, four today: `matcher.ts` lines 200 and 313), that together are exactly one catalog kind's own name, as typed or in lower case (compared by `words.ts` `wordKey`, so case, plurals, function words and punctuation never matter), stays one part, as typed ("Glass, window and door installer" is one part, read as that one kind, `glass-doors`); a longer run is read piece by piece even when together it is a kind's own name ("Medical imaging center, X-ray, CT, MRI, ultrasound", five comma pieces, is five parts), pieces a semicolon or line break separates are never joined, and every other split is as before; each part read by the kind matcher over K-24's catalog as read on the call (T-632; K-05 item 78's read). It answers by the table below, checked from its first row down.

| What the matcher reads of the parts                                              | What the settler answers                                                        |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| no part: the words name no work                                                  | refused (`noWords`), nothing written, as today                                  |
| any part is `which` (two kinds fit it)                                           | notBuilt, nothing written: the owner's "which?" (arch 05 line 237) is not built |
| a `not_booked` part beside a `listed` or `new_work` part                         | refused (`notBookedBesideKept`), nothing written (open gap 169)                 |
| every part is `listed`                                                           | "All on the list"                                                               |
| at least one part is `new_work`, every other part is `listed`, none `not_booked` | "One isn't on the list yet"                                                     |
| every part is `not_booked`                                                       | "Not a service we book"                                                         |

**What the table changes from today (default 191).** Today's writer reads the words under the option decide named (`answer.ts`), and only under c-01 and c-02 does it read the catalog and refuse what does not fit; under c-03, "Not a service we book", it reads no catalog and keeps every part as a not-booked pick with no kind, whatever the matcher would read. With a settler bound the option named is never read, so three things change. **A mixed answer is refused whichever option decide named** (the table's third row): not booked beside kept work has no branch, since S1-02's card holds none for a mix (open gap 169). Today c-01 and c-02 refuse it (`branchNotWords`) and c-03 keeps it, kept work and all, as not-booked picks; with a settler bound c-03 no longer saves it. The settler refuses it with `notBookedBesideKept`, a refusal T-1502 adds to `KindsFault` (`actions/src/tool-api/journey-1/kinds/errors.ts`) and throws as `KindsRefusedError("notBookedBesideKept", [the step's id])`. It is not `branchNotWords`, which stays the writer's second lock (below) and fires only when the settler's reading and the writer's differ, a fault, so a log tells the owner's mix from a fault. What `gateAndCommit` answers for it, and what the owner is then told, stay open gap 157. **A part two kinds fit is notBuilt whichever option decide named** (the second row), where today c-03 keeps it as a not-booked pick. **The catalog is read for every answer,** since the matcher reads every part over it: an answer whose every part is not booked now fails closed with `noCatalog` (`KindsReadError` on `readKindCatalog`, nothing written) when the catalog is empty, where today c-03 read none. This is intended (fail closed): the catalog is seeded wherever the settler is bound, and an empty one means its seed has not run (K-05 v1.19 default 170).

**Where `not_booked` comes from (default 191; CQ-348 (3)).** From code alone: the matcher's own reading of the part's words against RULES 1.4 (`actions/src/domain/kind-matcher/not-booked.ts`: gift cards, no visit, goods, delivery or a space "only", renting or a thing "alone"). Never from decide, whose named option carries no outcome, and never from an option the owner or the model picked. `matchKinds` keeps answering `not_booked` for such a part and `new_work` for a part no kind fits. "Work the law bars" has no words code can read: such a part is read as listed or new work like any other, and a drafted kind stays offline until Rosie's team reviews it (S1-02's safe default; S1-74).

**The writer after the settler (default 191).** S1-02's writer keeps the picks under the settled branch by its own table, which does not change: a listed part is a listed pick of its kind; a new part is a new-work pick of a kind drafted for it; under "Not a service we book" every part is a not-booked pick with no kind, and the part gives no `kinds`. The settler's reading and the writer's are one reading: the catalog is read once for the call and both use it, so the two can never read different catalogs. The writer's own check that the branch fits the words stays as a second lock. With a settler bound it can fire only if the two readings differ, which is a fault and fails closed.

**With no settler bound (default 191).** The walk behaves as it does today: it walks the branch of the option decide named, and S1-02's writer refuses a branch that does not fit the words (`branchNotWords`). So before the binding carries the settler (T-1503), an answer whose parts are all listed commits under c-01 with `commit-s1-02.json`'s rows, up to row ids, and any other answer is refused with nothing written. A binding hands in `settlers` or leaves it out; nothing else turns the settler on.

**What the gate and the answer hold (default 191).** Limits 1 to 6 run on the option decide named, before the walk, as today, and limit 6 re-parses `kinds` from its words as a text field. That stands for the settled branch because the options of one question share its question, step and target, carry the same stakes (K-02 README "Stakes": every option of an open question is high when any one is) and, at S1-02, the same one field; default 191 asks a settler only at a split step whose options are alike in all of these (open gap 170). The answer's `partResult.choiceId` stays the option decide named, since it names the part's choice as the gate passed it (`commit-s1-02.json`), and its `gives` are the keys the settled branch's rows give. No field of the answer names the settled branch (open gap 168). On staging, S1-03's and S1-74's writers answer notBuilt until the stop reads are bound (CQ-360), so there "One isn't on the list yet" and "Not a service we book" are settled and then answered notBuilt, with nothing written.

**Examples.** "Barbershop, plumbing, electrical" settles "All on the list" and writes `commit-s1-02.json`'s rows whether a read named c-01, c-02 or c-03. An answer with one listed kind and one kind no catalog kind fits settles "One isn't on the list yet" with c-01 named: one listed pick, one drafted kind with its new-work pick, and S1-03's message (the ruling's proof for T-1502: "c-01 named, 'Barbershop, dog yoga' drafts one kind").

### Who reads default 191

Each reader moves in its own card; nothing built changes until then.

- **The commit's walk, S1-02's settler and S1-02's writer** (C-087 and journey 1's kinds; the action-layer engineer; T-1502): the port, when the walk asks it (a split step, a part the gate passed and no hold keeps back), what it reads of the named choice, the table with its refusal `notBookedBesideKept`, the one catalog read shared with the writer, the unit's why, and `settlers` on the turn face's binding. It reports what open gap 170 asks before the settler is bound, and tests what open gap 172 asks.
- **The binding on staging** (the runtime engineer; T-1503): hands the settler in beside the question read and the writers. Until it does, the walk is today's.
- **Decide** (C-115; the AI engineer; T-1500, decide-2): K-02 README default 16. The option a read names is never read as the branch, so a read gains nothing by guessing among the three.
- **S1-04's one-kind settler** (the action-layer engineer; T-1491): may bind its settler in the same place, `WalkDeps.settlers` by step id. Default 191 never asks it, since S1-04 has no branches: an S1-04 button answer is walked on its label (default 181), and S1-04 with one kind is walked, not asked (default 185). T-1491's own version states when that settler is asked and what it answers.
- **QA** (C-140): no contract test changes, since no schema, rule, fixture or index entry does. T-1502's own proofs hold the table.
- **The architect** (T-1504 and later K-09 versions): open gap 167.

### Open gaps, default 191

Numbered after v1.11's. Each with its default where one is in use. For QUESTIONS.md, in these words:

167. **No frozen exchange shows a settled branch that differs from the option named** (default 191). The one frozen answer to S1-02, `commit-s1-02.json`, names c-01 and walks "All on the list", so the fixtures neither show nor refuse the new cases, and no named rule holds the table. For the architect, with the lead: a K-09 minor version adds a commit fixture for c-01 named with a new-work part (S1-03's rows), one for c-01 named with every part not booked (S1-74's row), a refused twin whose rows follow the named option against the words, and a named rule for the table. S1-03's and S1-74's rows ask no question, so such a fixture also waits on what carries a row's own step and line (CQ-360). A fixture for "Not a service we book" must also pass `givesStepKeys` (`tool-api.rules.mjs` lines 215 to 235), which the contract test holds every accepted fixture that states a step of journeys 1 and 2 to ("each setup fixture writes its step's keys": `tests/contract/tool-api.v1.test.ts`). That rule holds a stated S1-02 to S1-02's gives (`STEP_GIVES`: `kinds`), and under that branch the part gives no `kinds`, so it refuses such an S1-02 row. Rules are only ever added and `givesStepKeys` does not change, so that version must state how such a fixture is checked: for example, a rule under a new name that holds S1-02's gives by the settled branch, named in the index for that fixture in its place. Default in use: default 191's words, and T-1502's unit and integration proofs.
168. **The answer names no settled branch** (default 191). `partResult.choiceId` is the option decide named, and no field says which branch code took. For the runtime engineer and the AI engineer: does the trace, the composer or a later turn need the settled branch from the answer itself? Default in use: no. The rows written carry their own steps, and the unit's why names the settled branch. If a caller needs it, a K-09 minor version adds an optional field to the part's result.
169. **Work Rosie never books beside work it keeps, and a part two kinds fit** (default 191; open gap 157; CQ-199). For Jay: an owner names a kind Rosie books and something it never books in one answer ("Barbershop, gift cards"). S1-02's card holds three branches and none for a mix. Should the kinds Rosie books be saved and S1-74's line said for the rest, or should Rosie ask? And when two kinds fit one part, what does Rosie's "which?" say? Default in use: the mix is refused and the "which?" is not built, each with nothing written; the question stays open and the owner is told nothing (open gap 157: a refused part still ends silent, and T-1504's reply is for an asked part, not a refused one).
170. **The limits run on the option named, and the walk takes the settled one** (default 191). For the action-layer engineer, to report from T-1502 before the settler is bound: does any limit, or the gate's own checks, read an option's `branch` or `label` in a way that could pass one option of a question and refuse another? Default in use: none does at S1-02, whose three options share their question, step, target, stakes and field, so the limits' results hold for the settled branch. This was not checked against the gate's code for this default. If one does, T-1502 reports NEED and the settler is not bound.
171. **`match-kinds.json`'s note, and the kind ids of CQ-157 (2)** (default 191). The frozen v1 fixture's note says "the frontier builder asks for each part's closest kinds", `commit-s1-02.json` names it in `given.after`, and its kind ids "illustrate K-24's id form" and are not the catalog's. Under default 191 no caller makes a `matchKinds` call before the commit: the frontier offers S1-02's three options from the bundle alone, and the commit's settler makes the reading. Default in use: the fixture stands as the call's sample, for a caller that wants the reading alone; the commit takes every kind id from K-24's catalog rows as read on the call, never from the request or a fixture. For the architect: whether `matchKinds` keeps any caller in a turn is said with the version of open gap 167.
172. **Words before the list** (default 191, round 2; K-02 README default 16). The `kinds` field's words are the part's whole quote exactly as typed, never a list cut out of it, so a text with words before its list ("We do haircuts, beard trims and shaves") gives the settler and the writer a first part that holds those words ("We do haircuts"): the split is at commas, semicolons and line breaks (`actions/src/domain/kind-matcher/words.ts`, `splitParts` and `partSpans`), with default 191's one exception alone (T-1518; DL-1042, DL-1054; `actions/src/domain/kind-matcher/matcher.ts`, `partsIn`): a run of pieces next to each other, split at commas alone, of no more pieces than the catalog's most-cut name has (`nameParts`, four today: `matcher.ts` lines 200 and 313), that together are exactly one catalog kind's own name, as typed or in lower case, stays one part ("Glass, window and door installer"); a longer run is read piece by piece ("Medical imaging center, X-ray, CT, MRI, ultrasound", five comma pieces), and every other split is as before, so this text still splits after "We do haircuts". S1-02's writer under "Not a service we book" reads no matcher and splits as before, with no exception (`actions/src/tool-api/journey-1/kinds/answer.ts`, `partsOf`). The matcher drops function words, "we" and "do" among them, but not every word a person puts before a list ("We offer haircuts" keeps "offer": `words.ts`). For the action-layer engineer, to test in T-1502 and report: does the matcher read such a part as the kind it names, or as new work or `which`? Default in use: the part as typed, read by the matcher as it is; a part read as new work drafts a kind that stays offline until Rosie's team reviews it (S1-02's safe default).

## v1.12 (T-1504): an asked part sends the turn's one open question again, once

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): on staging both reads answered an owner's typed answer to S1-02 with `ask`, the gate answered the part `asked` before any limit ran (`actions/src/gate/framework/gate.ts` lines 296 to 298, its default 2), the commit wrote nothing for it (`actions/src/commit/commit.ts` lines 357 to 359; its header, lines 16 and 17), and the turn composes only the rows the commit names (`runtime/src/turn/compose.ts` lines 345 to 351), so the owner who answered got nothing (DL-1014, DL-1016; `ops/reviews/turn2-no-reply/read_ask.txt`). No contract said what an asked part sends: v1.8 records the silence for the unmapped end alone (defaults 143 and 145, open gap 129). The ruling on the silent second text (DL-1018, its section C; `ops/reviews/s102-answer-ruling/`) gave an asked part one row that sends the one open question again; its checker refused that first form (no cap, so an auto-reply gets one re-send per text; the question's send time moves; a row never composed holds back every later reply to the phone) and narrowed it in its card 6: no read-back question, the question's ask count raised, no row at two or more, the row proven composed and finalized. The lead's card (T-1504, from card 6) asks that S1-02's question be covered unless a source forbids it. **Kind:** additive, a minor version, and it changes no shape. `tool-api.v1.12.schema.json` holds every v1.11 definition under its own name, each by `$ref` to `tool-api.v1.11.schema.json`, which stays as it is, so every v1 to v1.11 exchange is a v1.12 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks every earlier accepted fixture against v1.12) and a consumer on v1.11 keeps working: v1.8's `gateAndCommit.committed` already takes an outbox row and a written open question on an answer whose parts are all `asked`, when the request holds no unmapped end. No call, key, error or definition is added. What v1.12 adds is the numbered defaults below, the fixtures that hold them and one named rule. It adds no words: the row carries the question's own step line, so Jay is not needed. **Not here:** any code (the commit's row and its count, T-1505; v1.12's contract test, T-1506: "Who reads v1.12"); the K-05 write that raises an open question's ask count, which no frozen K-05 version has (open gap 173); the words for the unmapped end (CQ-199); default 191's settled branch, which v1.12 does not touch, so open gap 167 stays open.

### Defaults (T-1504), numbered after T-1499's

192. **An asked part's one row: the turn's one open question, sent again.** A turn of decided texts sends its one open question again, in one outbox row, when all of these hold, each read as the commit reads it, the question's row on the call's transaction as the walk reads a question it closes (default 181 (1)). (a) Decide ended at least one part in one question, the verb `ask` or the part's outcome `oneQuestion` (K-02 v1 `resultPart`), so the gate answered it `asked` with limits 1 to 6 `notChecked` (gate.ts default 2), and every other part is answered `asked` the same way or `noAction`: no part is committed, stopped, depends on a stopped part or is held, and none is a part the gate passed that waits for the one question (commit.ts default 6). (b) The request holds no unmapped end (K-02 v1.2 `end`: v1.8 keeps it silent, CQ-199 and CQ-203) and no planner verb (`planner` null). (c) The turn's context holds exactly one open question (K-02 v1 `context.openQuestions`: every one sent to the turn's handle, arch 03 line 159), and each asked part is about it: an `ask` names no other question (its `questions` that one, or none), a `oneQuestion` part that chooses chooses one of its options, and a part of any other verb ended in one question is not about it. (d) That question, read on the call's transaction (K-05 v1.2 `openQuestionRead`, the read the walk's question port and limits 3 and 4 make: `db/repo/targets.ts`), is live (waiting or open), was sent to the turn's handle, is of the call's business, was asked of the call's person (its `person_id` the caller's `personId`), is about no booking, and is stored with no options and no read-back (default 193). (e) It has been asked once (`ask_count` 1: default 194). (f) Nothing open holds a routine reply back (default 195). (g) The request's turn carries its `service` (v1.5 default 135), the row's channel. **The row:** beside the turn's core actions, in its one commit, exactly one outbox row (K-05 v1 `outboxRowNew`), as the walk writes the row that first asked the question (`actions/src/commit/walk/writes.ts` lines 490 to 501): its id `outbox-<the question's step>-<turn key>-<n>` by default 131 (n 1, the turn's only row of that kind and step; `outbox-s1-02-sam-asked-1` in the fixture), to the turn's handle and the question's person, `businessId` the question's (the call's), `bookingId` null, `channel` the turn's `service` (default 132), send class `quick_reply` (default 133: a reply in the same turn, at any hour: RULES 4.3, Q-15), `openQuestionId` the question's id, `copyOfRowId` null, `versionsRead` empty (open gap 177), no plan; and the question's ask count raised (default 194). **At most one row a turn,** however many parts decide ended in one question (v1.8 default 145: one question for the turn). **The answer:** each asked part as the gate answered it (`asked`, `choiceId` the part's choice for choose, else null, limits 1 to 6 `notChecked`, no stop, no key, nothing it depends on); `written.outboxRows` names the row, beside any core action's line, so the turn composes it (default 197); `written.written` names the question's row at its version after the raise, and no other row of a part; `openQuestions` opens and closes nothing; no effect and no key. In every other case an asked turn is answered as before v1.12: no row and no raise (default 198). **Sources:** S13-25 rule 16 ("Ask once, plainly, with the choices. If language or access got in the way, try once more another way. Nothing changes while you wait": the row is that one plain ask after an unclear answer); K-02 v1 `ask` ("so Rosie asks which"); arch 09 line 490 (an open question keeps an ask count: "Rosie asks once; once more only after a language or access problem, or after answering a question back"); DL-1018 section C and its checker's card 6.
193. **Which questions (the card's Gives 3): a question about no booking, stored with no options and no read-back.** The stored row's `options` (K-04 `open_questions.options`, "the options as sent ... empty for an open-ended question": K-05 v1 `openQuestionNew`) is empty and its `readback` null, and its `booking_id` null. **Why not a read-back question:** the row names the question, so once a part of it goes, its send is the question's send time (`db/migrations/0037_core_question_went_out.sql` lines 55 to 62: the latest part gone of a row that asks it, to its own handle; K-05 v1.2 default 41), and limit 3 holds an exact yes to a read-back to texts sent since that time (`actions/src/gate/limit-3/exact-yes.ts` lines 425 to 427), so a yes already on its way would go stale. A read-back is asked fresh by limit 3's own outcome (`freshReadBack`), whose row is the limit's (commit.ts default 8). **Why not a question stored with options** (buttons, numbers or a poll, as sent): the row would carry them again, a poll as a new poll whose id becomes the question's (0037 lines 56 to 59; K-05 default 41), so a vote on the first poll would no longer answer its own poll (K-02 v1 `openQuestion.pollId`; arch 03 line 187). **Why not a question about a booking:** limit 4 sends a second conflict on one question to a person, and reads the ask count to know it (`actions/src/gate/limit-4/versions.ts` lines 321 and 322, its default 3; arch 06 line 305), so a re-send would send that booking question's next conflict to a person; and a booking question meets CQ-73's one question first (commit.ts default 6). Neither has a ruling here (open gap 175). **S1-02's question is covered.** The walk opens it with no options and no read-back (writes.ts lines 484 and 485; v1.5 default 134: "S1-02 is asked with no options, the kinds typed"; `commit-s1-01.json`'s stated question), about no booking, to the turn's handle and person, under the call's business. Its three option choices are the frontier's, built from the split's branches and never sent as buttons ("Just type them"); they are not stored on the question (K-02 v1 choice; `actions/src/frontier/sources/open-questions/open-questions.ts`). The checker's card 6 said "no options"; the sources make those the options as sent, of which S1-02's question has none, and no source forbids sending it again. S1-04's and S1-09's questions as the walk asks them today are covered the same way (default 179, difference 2: S1-04's question stores no options); once cards 20 and 21 store S1-04's options, its question leaves this cover with nothing more to change. **What moves, and why that is safe** (the checker's question). (1) _The question's row and version:_ the raise is an update, so the row's version rises by one (K-04 v1 rule 2). No reader holds a question's version from an earlier read: the walk closes it at the version its row is read at on the call's transaction (default 181 (1)); limit 4 compares the rows the question showed (`versionsRead`), never the question's own row (versions.ts `compareAndSetOf`); K-05's `armClock` is named by the question and compares none of its versions (K-05 v1 `armClock.request`); limit 1's sign-up rule reads the stored row on the call (`actions/src/gate/limit-1/allowed.ts` default 14 (b)); and the rows that ask it name no versions read. The ask count's readers are limits 4 and 6: limit 6's next failure on the question changes nothing instead of asking again (`actions/src/gate/limit-6/re-parse.ts` lines 385 to 395), which is S13-25 rule 17's "Still unclear ... change nothing"; limit 4 reads it only on a conflict in the rows the question showed, which the walk's questions do not hold (`versionsRead` empty), and where another opener's question holds some, it goes to a person, never to a write. (2) _Its send time:_ it becomes the re-send's, as K-05 v1.2 default 41 foresaw ("a question asked again (`ask_count` 2 or more) gives its newest send and poll"). For a covered question no check compares a text with it: limit 3 passes a plain question on the person's own reply (exact-yes.ts line 409) and compares send times only for a read-back; the frontier offers a question once a part of it has gone (open-questions.ts line 82), so the later time never hides it; the frontier's cap reads send times only for questions about a booking (`actions/src/frontier/cap/cap.ts` `targetBookingOf`); and decide's context shows the newest send, the one the person last saw (`ai/src/context/builder.ts`). (3) _Nothing else:_ its status, options, read-back, versions read, clock line, clock row and due time stay as they were (default 196), and it stays live and answerable (`refuse-s1-02-asked-question-closed.json`).
194. **The cap: once a question, ever.** The commit reads the question's `ask_count` on the call's transaction. At 2 or more no row is written and nothing is raised: the turn ends as before v1.12, silent (`refuse-s1-02-asked-twice.json`). At 1, the same commit that writes the row raises it to 2, compared and set at the version the commit read, all or nothing: a commit that fails writes neither (K-05 v1 `commitTurn`: one transaction). So a second unclear answer, or an auto-reply, finds 2 and gets nothing, and no reply can loop. After the re-send the question waits for its answer or its clock's safe default (S13-25 rule 17: "Still unclear, or no answer: do the step's safe default"; open gap 176). **The K-05 call that raises it: none is frozen.** K-05 v1's `stateWrite` never writes `open_questions` (its `stateTable` refuses the tables "that have their own list below"), `openQuestionClose` only closes (answered, defaulted or withdrawn), and K-05 v1.1 to v1.20 keep both as v1 has them (each refers to v1's `stateTable` and `openQuestionClose`; only v1 and v1.1 state `writes` in full, with `openQuestions` holding `open` and `close` alone). So the raise needs a K-05 minor version (open gap 173). T-1505 builds no row before that version and its `db/repo` build are merged: the checker refused a re-send with no cap (DL-1018 section C), so a row is never written without the raise beside it.
195. **Holds and unread texts hold the row back.** An `ask` part has hold class `none` (`actions/src/commit/hold.ts` `holdClassOf`: "a verb that writes nothing"), so no hold keeps it back and the gate answers it `asked` under an open safety hold or beside an unread text. The row is a routine message (it asks a setup question again), so the commit writes it only when what is open would let a routine part through (`isHeld("routine", ...)` false on the holds and unread texts the commit reads at its step 3, after the turn's core actions: commit.ts step 3, hold.ts): no open safety hold (arch 06 lines 315 to 327) and no unread text of the turn (CQ-65's DL-127 addendum: the next turn reads that text, which may answer the question). A turn whose core actions hold anything but its texts' safety outcomes (a STOP or a START, a join), or whose safety outcome opens a hold, sends no row.
196. **No clock and no stop read (the card's Gives 4).** The commit makes no clock row for the row, and cancels and moves none: the question keeps the clock row its first ask made (`clock-<question id>`, default 131), armed at the first delivery receipt of a row that asks it (K-05 v1 item 5). The re-sent row's first delivery receipt arms that clock row only when no receipt came before; otherwise `armClock` answers `repeated` and moves nothing ("a later receipt never moves it": K-05 v1 `armClock.response`; `runtime/src/clocks/arming.ts` step 1), so the question's due time and safe default stay as they were. No never-delivered row is made for it. The row reads no stop home, as the walk's question rows read none (writes.ts lines 466 to 501; open gap 165): a STOP text never reaches decide (the guard's one fixed reply: `actions/src/homes/stop/stop.ts` default 4; RULES 7.3), and limit 8 drops a row that is no safety line to a stopped number when it is sent (`runtime/src/dispatch/limit8/limit8.ts` step 1). **A rerun writes nothing:** the commit is keyed by the turn id, so a repeat answers the first commit and writes neither the row nor the raise again (K-05 v1 item 1; commit.ts lines 398 and 399), and the row's id is the same on a rerun (default 131).
197. **Its words, its composing and the phone.** The row names the question, so K-05's row read gives it the question's step and that step's own line (K-05 v1.3 `readOutboxRow`), and compose input builds its input as for the row that first asked (v1.6: the step's master line and its card's term facts, default 137; for S1-02, `compose-input-s1-02-v1.6.json`'s input with the row's own id): the question's own words again, with no preface and no new words (open gap 174). The row holds no words at commit (arch 01 line 41): neither the person's text nor decide's `unclear` is written into it. Finalize adds a first text's lines only to a first text: the opener only on Rosie's first text to the handle about the business, and the opt-out line only until a text that carried it is delivered (`actions/src/finalize/fixed-lines/insert.ts`; the line is finalize's, from the phone's row, as `actions/src/tool-api/turn-face/rows.ts` lines 134 and 409 read it), so neither repeats. The turn composes the row as it composes every row the commit names (compose.ts lines 345 to 351); it is no fixed line's row (compose.ts `fixedLineRowsOf`, lines 363 to 381). A row left with no plan holds back every later reply to that phone (`runtime/src/wiring/staging/tool-api-gate.ts` lines 73 to 75), so T-1505 proves the row composed and finalized, its plan written, on local PostgreSQL before it ships (the checker's card 7).
198. **What stays silent** (what CQ-199 and CQ-203 keep, and the rest). Each of these ends as before v1.12, with no row and no raise: the unmapped end (v1.8; open gap 129; CQ-199, CQ-203); a turn with no open question, or with several (the lettered "which?" of S13-12 and arch 03 line 187 stays CQ-199's lane); an ask about another question; a question with options as sent, a read-back or a booking (default 193; open gap 175); a question asked twice or more (default 194); an open safety hold, an unread text, or a core action other than the texts' safety outcomes (default 195); a part a writer or the walk refused, which fails the call with nothing written (open gaps 157 and 169); a part a limit stopped, whose outcome's rows are each limit's own (commit.ts default 8); a held part; a committed part, which writes its own rows; a part the gate passed that waits for the one question (commit.ts default 6); and a planner verb.

### Named rule, v1.12

`tool-api.rules.mjs` adds one, under a new name (rules are only ever added), and the constant `ASKS_BEFORE_SILENT` (2). It reads only the contracts' own files beside it, synchronously, as v1.8's does: the fixtures the exchange runs after.

- **`askedResendsQuestion`:** on a first commit (`committed`, `repeated` false) of decided texts in which decide ended a part in one question and every part is answered `asked` or `noAction`, each part decide ended in one question is answered `asked` with no limit run, and the answer opens and closes no question and writes no effect. The turn sends its one open question again exactly when default 192's conditions hold as the exchange shows them: no unmapped end, no planner verb, no part the gate passed waiting, no core action but the texts' safety outcomes and no safety hold among them, a service on the turn, exactly one open question in the context, each asked part about it, the question about no booking, of the call's business and asked fewer than `ASKS_BEFORE_SILENT` times, and its stored row, as a fixture the exchange runs after states it (`given.after`; that fixture's `given.stated.writes.openQuestions`, default 136), with no options and no read-back, sent to the turn's handle and asked of the call's person. Then the answer names exactly one outbox row that no core result names, in default 131's form for the question's step, and `written` names the question's row and nothing else. Otherwise it names no such row and does not write the question's row. The schema takes a row, a raised count and a closed question on any committed answer and cannot see a question's stored row, which neither the request nor the turn's context carries (K-02 v1 `openQuestion` has no options or read-back); the rule reads it where K-09's fixtures state it, so a re-send of a question no fixture the exchange runs after states is refused, as T-1505's code, which reads the row live, never writes one it cannot read.

### Fixtures, v1.12

Accepted (1): `commit-s1-02-asked.json` (rule `askedResendsQuestion`): Sam's second text, after `commit-s1-01.json` asked S1-02's question (`oq-s1-02-sam-1`, stored with no options and no read-back, at version 2 since its first delivery receipt opened it: K-05 v1 item 5), is "A bit of everything", which names no kind of work, so both Haiku reads agree on `ask` about that one question (K-02 README default 16: `ask` stays for a text that does not give what the question asked). The gate answers the part `asked` with no limit run; the commit writes `outbox-s1-02-sam-asked-1` and raises the question's ask count from 1 to 2, so `written` names its row at version 3; nothing else is written but Sam's text's safety outcome (No), which sends no line. The turn key's stand-in is `sam-asked` (default 131); `commitSeq` 2 follows the first row's 1. Refused (4), in `fixtures/invalid/`, each refused by the rule alone, the schema taking it: `refuse-s1-02-asked-two-rows.json` (two rows asking the question again; with the first alone it is the accepted fixture); `refuse-s1-02-asked-question-closed.json` (the same commit closing the question it sends again; with the closed list empty it is the accepted fixture); `refuse-s1-02-asked-twice.json` (the question already asked twice, ask count 2 in the context, and the row still written; with ask count 1 it is the accepted fixture, and with no row and nothing in `written` it is accepted too, silent); and `refuse-s2-22-asked-read-back.json` (S2-22's question, which reads back who takes bookings, the question of v1's `commit-limit-3-no-exact-yes.json`, answered "hmm": both reads ask and the answer sends it again; no fixture it runs after shows the question stored with no read-back, and it has one; with no row and nothing in `written` it is accepted, silent). The first three differ from the accepted fixture in that one field and their note alone (checked 2026-11-20); the fourth is built on `commit-limit-3-no-exact-yes.json`'s turn, with the service and chat id a v1.5 caller puts on the request's turn, so the read-back is its one reason.

### Who reads v1.12

Each consumer adopts v1.12 in a task that needs it; tasks already building finish on their version (build prompt 6.4). v1.12 changes no code and no validator: a call checked by v1.11's definitions is checked the same by v1.12's.

- **The commit** (C-087, gateAndCommit's handler; the action-layer engineer; T-1505, after T-1502, which shares the commit, and after open gap 173's K-05 version and its `db/repo` build). **Build:** in `actions/src/commit/commit.ts` (the loop at lines 352 to 370, after the gate and before `buildCommitRequest`), `actions/src/commit/types.ts` and `actions/src/commit/build.ts`: when default 192 (a) to (c) and (g) hold, read the one open question on the call's transaction and check (d) to (f): the walk's question read (`WalkQuestions.liveQuestion`, `LiveQuestion` in `actions/src/commit/walk/writes.ts`, which answers its id, step, business, options and version today) widened to K-05 v1.2 `openQuestionRead`'s `readback`, `askCount`, `personId` and `bookingId`, or a port of its own bound to the same `db/repo` read (`db/repo/targets.ts`); and `isHeld("routine", open.holds, open.unreadEventIds)` false on what is open after the core actions (default 195). Then push one unit with no part answer: default 192's row and default 194's raise, in the K-05 version's shape; its event-log entries are the commit's own for the outbox row, and the raise's as that version says. The unit's why names the step and "asked again", never the person's words. The answer is built as today from what K-05 wrote (`written`, `outboxRows`), so the question's row is in `written` at its new version. Nothing on the runtime side changes unless the read needs a binding (`runtime/src/wiring/real.ts` `turnCommitDeps`: the runtime engineer's, if so). **Proves it with:** unit tests in `actions/src/commit` ("one asked part, one open question: one quick_reply row and the raise, no close"; "asked twice: no row"; "an open hold: no row"; "an unread text: no row"; "a read-back question, a question with options, a booking question: no row"; "two open questions: no row"; "the unmapped end: unchanged"; "a rerun: nothing written"); and on local PostgreSQL (`actions/src/tool-api/turn-face/pg/sign-up.int.test.ts`), both reads ask on S1-02's question: one row, the question's row at its next version with `ask_count` 2 and its status, options and clock row unchanged; the row composed and finalized, its plan written; a second asked text on the same question: no row.
- **The runtime** (C-046; the runtime engineer): nothing to build. ## After v1.12, with no new version (T-1491, from T-1515's card): S1-04 is walked, not asked, for one kind

**Why** (DL-1032, fault 3; DL-1033, ruling 7; `ops/reviews/live-reply-faults/rule-onekind.json` card 1, with `check-onekind.json`'s corrected ruling; DL-1041 item 4, which folds T-1515's default into T-1491): on staging an owner who named one kind of work, "Plumbing", was asked S1-04, "Which of your kinds of work share one company, with one tax number?", which the master never asks such an owner (MASTER-FLOW-A line 90: "Asked only when Sam names more than one kind of work"). This default states both. **Kind:** this README's text alone, which the lead accepted as a README default with no new version (DL-1033 ruling 7, answering `check-onekind.json` point 4), with T-1499's default 191 as the precedent, since no schema shape changes. No version, schema, named rule, fixture, index entry or `frozen.json` changes, and no frozen file is edited. **Why it sits here:** after v1.12's section, as default 191 sits after v1.11's: it belongs to no version, and the next K-09 version that touches S1-04's walk restates it with a fixture (open gap 178). **Not here:** S1-04's one-kind settler and the S1-02 wrapper (T-1492), S1-08's writer (T-1482), the bindings (T-1493, T-1494) and the stop reads on staging (T-1516). The walk's code is T-1491's, on the same commit as this default.

### Default (T-1491, from T-1515's card), numbered after T-1504's

199. **S1-04's one-kind settler: the port, when the walk asks it and what it answers.** (1) **The port.** The commit's walk (C-087) takes buttons settlers from its binding by step id, in a new optional field, `WalkDeps.buttonsSettlers` (type `ButtonsSettlers`: given a step id, the settler bound to it, or null; `actions/src/commit/walk/buttons.ts`), handed in as `questions`, `writers` and `settlers` are. It is not `settlers`: T-1502's split-only port, which answers a branch label (default 191), and T-1503's binding of it stay as built, and a settler bound under `settlers` at a step with no branches is still never asked (default 191 says T-1491 "may" bind its settler there, which leaves room for a field of its own). A buttons settler is read-only code for one plain step whose message has buttons. It is handed the call's transaction and business scope (`repo`, `businessId`), the step as the turn's pinned graph holds it, the passed part, its choice, the turn (the same object the walk hands each writer as `writer.turn`, so the settler, a writer and a writer's wrapper can share one read for the walk), and the rows the walk has built so far for the part (`rows`: a copy of each list, in the walk's order: the versions read, the state rows and their entries, the questions opened and closed, the outbox rows and the keys given). It answers "ask" or the step's rows, or refuses; it writes nothing. (2) **When the walk asks it.** Only when the walk would stop at a plain step with no branches whose message has buttons (S1-04 in bundles v1 and v1.1), a buttons settler is bound for that step, and no hold keeps that step back (`writes.ts` `firstHeld`, as for any row the walk writes; STATE note 1). It asks once, after the rows before that step are built (the answered question's close, then each walked step's rows by its writer, in the walk's order) and before anything of that step is written. It is never asked for an answered S1-04 option (default 181's path): an answer's walk starts at the answered step and goes on from its next, so it never stops there. A walk that stops at a split, at a plain step whose message has no buttons, or at a step a hold keeps back asks none, and so does a part a hold keeps back, for which nothing is read. With none bound for the step, the walk is today's, byte for byte. (3) **What it answers.** "ask": the step is asked exactly as `writes.ts` default 10 asks it today (its open question with no options, no read-back and `clock` null, and the quick reply that asks it), and the walk stops there. The step's rows, in a step writer's shape (`StepRows`: state rows with their entries, the keys given, questions and outbox rows): the step is written as walked, its rows added in the walk's order as a writer's are, giving only the step's own keys (K-01 v1 `step.gives`; a key the step does not give is refused, `givesNotStep`, nothing written), and the walk asks no question for it. The walk then goes on from the step's next (arch 03 line 191), each step after it written as today with the holds re-checked for it, under the loop check and `MAX_WALK` (64 steps) counted over both stretches as one walk, to the first step that asks, waits or ends. A refusal or a failed read (the settler throws) fails the call closed with nothing written, as a writer's refusal does (open gap 157); the step is not asked either, and a failed read is never taken as "ask". Any other answer, neither "ask" nor rows in that shape, is refused the same way (`settledNotRows`, a refusal T-1491 adds to the walk's `WalkFault`, naming the step alone). A step after the walked one that writes something with no writer bound is refused as today (notBuilt), so the call fails closed. (4) **S1-04's settler** (T-1492). It counts kept picks by kind, as default 185 counts them: this walk's kept picks (in the rows built so far) plus those on file (K-05 v1.19 item 79), a not_booked pick excluded, and a kind already picked counted once (as `actions/src/tool-api/journey-1/kinds/answer.ts` default 3 keeps one pick per kind). **Exactly one:** the pick inserted in this walk is placed on its own insert by an S1-02 wrapper (around S1-02's writer, inside T-634's `kinds` wrapper), with `company_id` the company the turn runs under and `business_id` the call's business, and with its `company_kinds` row inserted first unless one is on file (K-05 item 72); a kept pick on file with no company is placed by an update at the version it was read at; a disagreement between the settler's recount and the wrapper's placement refuses, with nothing written; the step gives `companies`; the company is the one the turn runs under (the caller's person's own company whose business is the call's business: default 182). **Two or more:** "ask". **None** (no kept pick, which neither kept S1-02 branch leaves): refused with nothing written, as default 184 refuses an owner with no kept pick. (5) **S1-08 after it.** The walk goes on to S1-08, whose writer reads the owner through `readStop` on the stop reads handed in (frozen default 189): "texts" gives its one row; "noTexts" gives none, and the walk goes on to S1-09's question. With no stop reads bound the writer answers notBuilt and the part is refused with nothing written, so the pick is not saved and the owner gets no reply: the stop reads must be bound wherever this path runs (T-1516 binds them on staging's gate; T-1493 and T-1494 hand them to S1-08's writer). S1-09's question and its row are the walk's own (default 181), with no stop read of the walk's (open gap 165). A bundle that does not hold S1-08 (v1, v1.1) refuses a walked S1-04 at S1-08 (`stepNotInBundle`) with nothing written, so a binding hands S1-04's settler in only with a bundle that holds S1-08 and S1-09 (bundle v2, T-1477). (6) **The stand-in name, under default 130.** The name of default 130's business, the stand-in business head a roster phone's sign-up runs under until S1-09 gives `business`, never reaches a sign-up text before S1-09 (DL-964). This path's texts, S1-08's row and S1-09's question, are about that business, and finalize opens them with `signUpOpener`, "Hi! I'm Rosie, an automated assistant.", in place of `opener`, since their reader is read by the sign-up line as the owner and neither step needs `business` (K-01 README default 19, T-1510). (7) **Open gaps:** a one-kind commit fixture waits for the next K-09 version (open gap 178, as open gap 167 waits); which kinds count stays Jay's (open gap 154, CQ-359), with item (4)'s count in use.

### Defaults (T-1525), numbered after T-1491's

200. **S1-09's question, its options, and what decide fills** (the card's Gives 1). The walk asks S1-09 as any split asked of the turn's own writer by text (`writes.ts` default 10, T-1479): one open question with no options, no read-back and its card's safe default ("not yet an owner. Setup goes on, saved, and nothing goes live."), step 9's default clock, and one quick reply naming S1-09 and line 0, so v1.12's re-send covers it (default 193). Bundle v2 compiles S1-09 as a split, next S1-10, with three message branches, each carrying one answer field, `business` of type `id` (K-01 v1 `answerField`): "An owner; the listing matches" (no steps of its own, rejoins at S1-10), "Helps run it; not an owner" (S1-77, then S1-10) and "No match, or already listed" (S1-76, then S1-10); its message carries two buttons, "Yes, an owner" and "No, I help run it" (`bundles/v2/bundle/flow/A.json`, S1-09; T-1477's check 3). The frontier offers decide three option choices, one per branch in the card's order, each labelled with its branch word for word, defined by its Means line and listing `business` (id) (`actions/src/frontier/sources/open-questions/open-questions.ts` `cardChoices` and `fieldsOf`). K-02 README default 16 does not apply: its condition (c) fails, since the options differ by the owner's claim, which the text holds, as well as by the listing, which only code looks up, so decide reads them by meaning and asks when it cannot tell. **What decide fills:** one part, `choose`, S1-09's question and one of its three options; and, when the text gives the business's details, the `business` field, whose `words` are the details exactly as they stand in the text (the business's name and town as typed) and whose `value` is those same words, character for character: never an id, never a listing's words and never the claim's words. Limit 6 re-parses an `id` field as words held in its words (`actions/src/values/reparse.ts` lines 343 to 345), so such a value passes. No field carries the claim: decide carries it by its choice between the owner's option and the helper's (default 201). A text with no details leaves the field out ("Leave out a field the turn gives no words for": decide's `quotes_and_fields`), as a bare "1" or "2" does, which answers the claim alone (the buttons as sent: "Reply 1 Yes, an owner, 2 No, I help run it."). A part decide ends in one question is asked, and gets v1.12's one re-send of S1-09's own line, then silence until the clock's safe default (defaults 192 to 194; open gap 176). Decide-2 has no line for an `id` value and no rule for S1-09's span (open gap 180).
201. **S1-09's settler: the port, what it reads, and the branch it answers** (the card's Gives 1 and 3; the ruling's default 6; accepted as written by DL-1133, QA's point (a)). **The port:** default 191's, bound by step id beside S1-02's (`WalkDeps.settlers`; `actions/src/commit/walk/settle.ts`): read-only code for the split, asked by the walk once, only for a part the gate passed that chooses an option of S1-09's open question and that no hold keeps back, before the walk reads a branch from the graph or calls a writer, and handed the call's transaction and business, the step as the turn's pinned graph holds it, the part, its choice and the turn (the same object every writer of the run gets as `writer.turn`: default 199 (1)). It writes nothing. **What it reads:** (1) the `business` field's `words` (K-02 `fieldValue.words`), never its `value` and never the part's quote, which holds the claim's words, which a name must not take; (2) the owner's claim; (3) the Places lookup (default 202); (4) once a list is bound, the banned words (default 206). **The claim,** owner or helper, is decided by meaning ("they say they own it, in any words": line 118; RULES 2.2: "Every branch is decided by meaning"), which code cannot read from words, so it is the one thing the settler takes from the option decide named: the helper's claim exactly when that option's branch is "Helps run it; not an owner", else the owner's. **The settler never takes the branch from the option named, and never reads a listing match from it:** the model never sees a listing, so a named "An owner; the listing matches" claims no match, and a named "No match, or already listed" claims no mismatch; both carry the owner's claim, and the lookup alone says whether the listing matches. This narrows the ruling's "never from the option decide named" to the branch and the match, for the reason above, as default 181 walks S1-04's answer on the label decide chose; the lead accepted it (DL-1133). **Default 191's line, qualified for S1-09 (DL-1133):** default 191's "the same words give the same branch whichever of the question's options a read named" holds at S1-09 as: the same words and the same lookup give the same branch whichever of "An owner; the listing matches" and "No match, or already listed" a read named, and the claim, the one thing the settler takes from the option, follows "Helps run it; not an owner" against the other two, so the helper's option and an owner's option may give different branches for the same words. The walk itself still reads only what every option of the question shares (default 191); only the settler reads the option's branch, and only for the claim. **What it answers,** checked in this order: (i) no `business` field, or its words give no name (a claim alone, a bare "1", a photo with no caption): "ask" (`noDetails`; the ruling's default 3); (ii) a name with no town (default 202's split leaves no try): "ask" (`noTown`; the ruling's default 1); (iii) the lookup fails on any try (K-15 `unavailable`, `rateLimited` or `badRequest`, or the port throws): a refusal, `placesUnavailable` (K-09 v1 `lookupListing.errors`), so the call fails closed with nothing written and the question stays open, as default 191 treats a failed read; never "No match, or already listed", which would claim a check that did not run (K-15 README "Not found, no match and errors": "nothing is known about the listing"); (iv) the lookup's match (default 202: exactly one candidate held on the try that decides) carrying no banned word (default 206): the owner's claim gives "An owner; the listing matches", the helper's "Helps run it; not an owner"; (v) anything else: "No match, or already listed", whatever the claim, the helper's included (default 203 (b)), with its reason as a label: `noListing` (no try held a candidate: K-15 `noMatch` on each, or none of the candidates holds), `twoListings` (the deciding try held two or more) or `bannedWord` (the one held candidate carries a banned word: default 206). "Already listed" is never answered (default 203 (e)). **Its reading, held for the run:** the settler keeps what it read on the turn object, beside the lookup's outcome (default 202), as `actions/src/tool-api/journey-1/kinds/reads.ts` `walkMatcher` keeps its matcher (a WeakMap keyed by that object): the branch, the claim (`owner` or `helper`), the reason label (null on a match) and the name S1-09's writer writes (default 202, "The name S1-09's writer writes"). It holds no field of a candidate. S1-09's writer and S1-76's writer read that reading and nothing else of the words, the lookup or a list, and each refuses (`notSettled`) when none is held. **"ask" until T-1528 (DL-1133, QA's point (b)):** T-1502's port answers a label or refuses (`settledBranch`: any other answer is `settledNotInBundle`), so the bound settler refuses where S1-09's reading answers "ask" (`listingNeedsDetails`): the call fails closed with nothing written, the question stays open and the owner gets no reply to that text. T-1528 (open gap 179's card) lets the walk take a split settler's "ask": S1-09 is then sent again once through T-1505's ask-again path, with S1-09's own line as the words until Jay gives better ones (open gap 183), and a second such answer stays silent. A part decide itself ends in one question gets v1.12's re-send today (default 200). T-635a builds the reading with its three outcomes (a label, "ask" with its reason, a refusal) and the binding's refusal for "ask", so its tests hold the reading and the walk card changes only the binding. **With no settler bound** the walk is today's (default 191), and S1-09's writer refuses (default 204), so nothing is written on the option decide named.
202. **The lookup** (the card's Gives 2; the split restated and the marks folded by DL-1133, Fixes 3 and 4). **Four marks folded first (DL-1133, Fix 4):** before the split, the search and the hold, the settler folds the typographic quotes in the field's words to their plain forms, U+2018 and U+2019 to the apostrophe U+0027 and U+201C and U+201D to the quotation mark U+0022, and changes nothing else (case, spaces and every other character stay as typed), so an iPhone's smart punctuation, "Lee’s", is split, searched and held as "Lee's". `foldWords` folds both apostrophes again for the hold, which then changes nothing. **Name and town (DL-1133, Fix 3):** K-15 v1 searches by `nameAndTown`, both required (`port-places.v1.schema.json`; K-09 v1 `ListingByName`), and the field holds one span, so code splits it. A separator is a comma, or the word "in" standing alone (any case, with no letter or digit next to it). The tries, at most three, in this order: (1) the name is the words before the first separator, and the town the words after it up to the next comma, or to the end (the ruling's own "Lee's Barbershop in Springfield, I'm the owner" gives "Lee's Barbershop" and "Springfield", as "Lee's Barbershop, Springfield" and "Lee's Barbershop in Springfield" do); (2) the name is the words before the last separator, and the town the words after it (a name that holds "in" or a comma: "Cuts in Style in Springfield"); (3) the name is the words before the first separator, and the town the words after the last separator (the read-back's own form typed back: "Lee's Barbershop, 88 Elm St, Springfield"). Each side is trimmed of spaces and of a closing period and must hold a letter; a try with a side that does not, or with the same name and town as an earlier try, is skipped. No separator, or no try left, is a name with no town ("ask": default 201 (ii)). Why: the master asks only for "the business name" (line 115), K-15 needs both, and the tries read the forms an owner types and the read-back's own (a name, then "88 Elm St, Springfield": line 127), shortest name first; each fails safe: a wrong try searches for a listing that is not found or does not hold, which takes the next try and at last S1-76's person check, and no try asks again (open gap 181). **The search:** one K-15 `search`, `{"by": "nameAndTown", "name", "town"}`, a try, with the try's words after the fold, in the order above. The first try that holds a candidate (below) decides, and no later try is searched: one held candidate is the match, two or more no match (`twoListings`). A try that holds none (K-15 `noMatch`, or no candidate holds) goes on to the next; after the last, no match (`noListing`). A failed search on any try is `placesUnavailable` at once (default 201 (iii)), and no later try is searched. So a walk makes at most three searches, each for a pair no earlier try asked (their time: open gap 189). The outcome (the deciding try and its held candidates, or none) is held for the run on the turn object (default 199 (1)) with the settler's reading (default 201), and no writer searches again. `photoText` is never searched on this slice (default 207). **The name S1-09's writer writes:** the deciding try's name; with no try deciding, the name of the first try not skipped (try (1)'s when it ran). Why: the name comes first in each form the tries read, and the words before the first separator never hold the town or a comment after it; a wrong name is for S1-76's person check to see, and is fixed later (open gap 186). **Each candidate held to the typed words:** a candidate holds when every word of the try's name is a word of its `name` and every word of the try's town is a word of its `address`, words as `wordsOf` gives them (`actions/src/values/words.ts`, exported: each run of letters and digits after `foldWords`, so case, accents and punctuation aside), in any order and not necessarily next to each other, as limit 6 holds a text value to its words (`actions/src/values/reparse.ts`, its `text` case over `wordsOf`; `actions/src/gate/limit-6/re-parse.ts` default 4). Never `holdsWords`, which matches one unbroken run of words and is not exported (DL-1133, Fix 6). A candidate that does not hold is dropped, never read back. Exactly one holding is the match; two or more is no match: Rosie cannot tell which listing is theirs, and never guesses (open gap 182). **On the double:** it answers only a request equal to its fixture's, exactly, case included (`adapters/src/places/double/double.ts` `sameSearch`; K-15 README "The double"). "Lee's Barbershop in Springfield, I'm the owner", "Lee's Barbershop in Springfield", "Lee's Barbershop, Springfield" and "Lee's Barbershop, 88 Elm St, Springfield", each typed with "Lee’s" or "Lee's", reach `{"by": "nameAndTown", "name": "Lee's Barbershop", "town": "Springfield"}` on try (1) or (3) (`search-by-name.json`), whose one candidate, Lee's Barbershop LLC at 88 Elm St, Springfield, holds: the match, so an owner's claim gets the read-back (default 204). Any other request answers `noMatch`, "lee's barbershop in springfield" in lower case among them. **What the split leaves:** a comment after the town with no comma before it ("Lee's Barbershop in Springfield and I'm the owner") stays in the town, holds nothing and takes S1-76's person check, as do the claim's words before the name if decide's span holds them; decide's span rule (open gap 180) would leave both out. **Never logged:** the name, the town, or a candidate's name, address, phone or website, in a span, a log line or an error (K-15 README "No name, address, phone or words in a span or log"); what the settler throws names its fault and the step alone.
203. **The ruling's defaults 1 to 5 and its precedence rule, each standing until Jay answers** (the card's Gives 3; DL-1124's Jay defaults; the lead files them in QUESTIONS.md). (a) **A town with the name** (the ruling's default 1): the search runs only when the words give a name and a town (default 202); otherwise the settler answers "ask", which until T-1528 merges refuses with nothing written and no reply, and from then sends S1-09 again once (default 201, "ask" until T-1528; DL-1133). Standing until Jay answers, with how Rosie asks for the town (open gap 183). (b) **A helper's words with no listing match go to S1-76 first** (the ruling's default 2, its precedence rule): the master's three branches are exclusive and say nothing of both, so the settler answers "No match, or already listed" (a person on Rosie's team checks), the business is saved from the typed words (default 204), and the helper's claim is kept as the review item's label, so the team's done decision on it writes no control (default 205; DL-1133, Fix 1): nothing goes live either way. Standing until Jay answers (open gap 184). (c) **An owner's claim with no name and no photo** (the ruling's default 3): nothing is written, and the settler answers "ask" (`noDetails`), which until T-1528 merges gets no reply and from then sends S1-09 again once for the business's details (the master's own "Rosie asks for the business name": line 115; default 201; DL-1133). One commit per turn, so a half answer is never saved, and the claim is not kept for the next text. Standing until Jay answers, with (a). (d) **A photo before photo reading** (the ruling's default 4): default 207, standing until the photo path lands. (e) **"Already on Rosie"** (the ruling's default 5): not checked, since no frozen column keeps a listing's reference per business (K-15 README open gap "already on Rosie"; K-23 v1 `business_setup` has none), so S1-76 is never taken for it; safe because nothing goes live before S1-75's team check (K-15 default 1). Standing until the K-23 minor version lands (open gap 190).
204. **S1-09's writer: it writes the owner's typed words, never the listing as read, and nothing goes live** (the card's Gives 4; reconciled with K-15 README default 9 by DL-1133, Fix 5). **The port:** T-1461's step writer, bound by step id (`WalkDeps.writers`, "S1-09"), read-only; the walk calls it as the answered split's writer, after the settler and before any step of the settled branch, and hands it the settled branch as the branch chosen (default 191). It reads the settler's reading from the turn object (default 201) and refuses (`notSettled`) when there is none, so a binding that hands the writer without the settler writes nothing: the settler, this writer and S1-76's are bound together or not at all. v1's `SetupS1_09.answer` names the same three branches (owner, helper, noMatch). **Whose rows:** only the call's business (`caller.businessId`), and only when the caller's person owns the company the turn runs under, whose business is the call's business (default 182; S1-09 "needs companies": line 129); otherwise refused (`noCompany`), with nothing written. **Nothing of the listing is kept (DL-1133, Fix 5):** K-15 README default 9 says "the caller keeps what the owner confirms and the listing's reference, never the listing as read", resting on Google's terms (K-15 README "What may be kept": the rest is prefill, never kept as read). The master's own later lines name the business as typed, never as listed: "The owner of Lee's Barbershop" (line 151), "setting up Lee's Barbershop" (line 187), "To prove Lee's Barbershop is yours" (line 201). **Which later step keeps a listing's field, once the owner confirms it:** the address (B03) is K-23 `locations`, written at S1-43 from the owner's own answer (K-09 v1's step table). K-09 v1's step table names no later step that writes `listing_phone` (B04) or `website` (B05), so both stay null, which changes nothing today: S1-75 sends its code to the listing's email and, with none, opens the team's control check (K-09 v1's S1-75 row; K-15 default 1: a listing never carries an email), and S1-84 writes `about` and `profile_confirmed_at`, whose draft reads the website (line 122) through its own card. A later step that keeps either once the owner confirms it reads the listing again by its kept reference, which open gap 190's K-23 minor version adds (K-15: place ids may be stored indefinitely); none is these defaults'. **The rows, in this order** (K-09 v1's step table: "K-04 businesses; K-23 business_setup"): (1) K-04 `businesses`, the call's business head, updated at the version read: `name` (B01) and `display_name` (B02) both the name default 202 gives S1-09's writer (the deciding try's name, else the first try's), as typed with only the four marks folded, on every branch. ### Who reads defaults 200 to 208

Each reader moves in its own card; nothing built changes until the bindings carry S1-09's settler and writers.

- **S1-09's settler and writer, S1-76's writer and the lookup** (journey 1's listing; the action-layer engineer; T-635a, `actions/src/tool-api/journey-1/listing/**`, and `actions/src/domain/paging/review.ts` and `ports.ts` for default 205's rule for a helper's claim): defaults 201 to 206, with default 207's photo case. Its proofs, as the ruling's card 3 lists them, with round 2's (DL-1133): the demo request gives the read-back and the owner's branch, and so does the ruling's own "Lee's Barbershop in Springfield, I'm the owner", typed with "Lee's" and with "Lee’s" (defaults 202 and 204); no row holds a candidate's name, phone or website on any branch (default 204); a real-looking name and town gives no match, S1-76's row and one review item; no town, or no details, makes the reading answer "ask" and the binding refuse; the helper's words with a match give the helper's branch, and with no match S1-76, whose review item's summary carries `Claim: helper.`, on which a done decision with `controlProven: true` is refused `writeBackNotForKind` and one with `controlProven: false` writes nothing back (defaults 203 (b) and 205); a banned word handed in is never in the read-back, writes the typed name with null phone and website, and opens S1-76's review item with `Reason: banned word.` (default 206); a failed port call on any try refuses `placesUnavailable`; two held candidates give no match; nothing logs a name, address or phone. It reports what open gaps 185, 192 and 193 ask. Action-layer note 8 of round 1 (its integration proof needs a test graph or a direct commit until S1-10's slice) is the lead's, for T-635a's card (DL-1133).
- **The Places double** (the integrations engineer; T-434): K-15 as frozen; nothing here changes it.
- **Bundle v3** (the playbook engineer; the ruling's card 4): prints S1-09's options, fields and stakes, and S1-77's and S1-10's compiled shapes, which defaults 200 and 205 take from bundle v2 and the master until then.
- **The turn face** (the action-layer engineer; the ruling's card 7): binds S1-09's settler (`settlers`) and both writers (`writers`) together, from the Places port and the reads its caller hands in, or none of them.
- **Staging's gate** (the runtime engineer; the ruling's card 8): default 208.
- **Decide** (C-115; the AI engineer): default 200 and open gap 180. Nothing here changes K-02.
- **The walk** (the action-layer engineer; T-1528, carded from open gap 179 by DL-1133): a split settler's "ask", sending S1-09 again once through T-1505's ask-again path.
- **S1-10's chain** (DL-1125; its README defaults come after these): the walk after S1-09's rows (default 204), as defaults 209 to 215 state it (T-1530).
- **QA** (C-140): no contract test changes, since no schema, rule, fixture or index entry does. T-635a's own proofs hold these defaults.
- **The security reviewer:** the read-back's words go only to the turn's own handle and person, under the call's business, and no name, town, address or phone goes into a span, a log line, an error, the unit's why or a review item's summary; nothing of a listing is kept as read (default 204); a helper's claim never stands for an owner's control (default 205); banned words (default 206); the double on allow-listed phones only (default 208).
- **The architect** (later K-09 versions): open gaps 179 and 191.

### Open gaps, defaults 200 to 208

Numbered after default 199's. Each with its default where one is in use. For QUESTIONS.md, in these words:

179. **A split settler's "ask"** (default 201). **Carded by DL-1133 as T-1528**, so it is no question for QUESTIONS.md: a split settler's "ask" sends the question again once through T-1505's ask-again path, with S1-09's own line as the words until Jay gives better ones (open gap 183).
