## After default 208, with no new version (T-1530): S1-10 at the walk, and S1-77 on bundle v3

**Why** (DL-1125, the S1-10 sizing ruling's slice "6a-ask", `ops/reviews/s1-09-design/rule-s1-10.json`, its card S10-1 and its defaults D1 to D5; DL-1143; T-1526's prints, `ops/cards/T-1526.md`): every S1-09 branch rejoins at S1-10 (MASTER-FLOW-A lines 131 to 166), and bundle v3 (T-1526) compiles S1-10 as a walked plain message step with no buttons and no card (needs `business`; gives `id_checked` and `mobile`; tells the owner) whose next, S1-75, bundle v3 does not hold. So the commit's walk (`actions/src/commit/walk/walk.ts` `visit` and `from`) walks it and refuses every S1-09 answer `stepNotInBundle` at S1-75. Architecture 03 line 191 has the walk stop at "the first step that asks someone, waits on a clock or event, or ends the journey", but K-01 v1 has no step field that says a step waits on an event, and the walk stops only where a step asks (a split's message branch, or a plain step's buttons), a clock settles a split, a branch ends or the journey ends (`walk.ts` `Stop`). And bundle v3 compiles S1-77 with no buttons, since C9 rewrote its question ("What's the owner's name and mobile? You'll get their own link and QR to pass on.") and removed its buttons (`compiler/src/corrections/c9.ts` `helperOwner`), so the walk walks it instead of asking it, against default 205's reading. No contract said how the walk stops at S1-10, what S1-10's writer writes at the walk, how its secure link is minted and opened, what the link's page says while the upload is not open, what the walk does with S1-77 on bundle v3, or how S1-75's control item keeps a helper's claim from turning into control (T-1525 round 2's security note 2). **Kind:** this README's text alone, as defaults 191, 199 and 200 to 208 were (DL-1033 ruling 7). No version, schema, named rule, fixture, index entry or `frozen.json` changes, and no frozen file is edited: the wait is a rule of the commit's walk over K-01 v1's `step.gives` and a table in this README; S1-10's writer is a code port of the walk (`WalkDeps.writers`), which no schema holds; the link row is K-22 v1.1's `page_links`, issued as K-21 v1 "Links" says; and the code reaches the slot filler through the turn face's memory, as `held.ts` holds a turn's texts. No fixture is added: no frozen shape changes, and no exchange can show the walk until bundle v3 and the walk card are merged (open gap 202). **Why it sits here:** after T-1525's section, as that sits after default 199's. **Not here:** any code (each reader moves in its own card: "Who reads defaults 209 to 215"); the ID upload itself, with `signedUpload`, `finishUpload` and R2 (DL-1125's whole chain W1 to W10: T-521, T-515, T-437, T-438, T-1053); the owner's email and its code (D5; J-14); S1-75 itself (T-637, after its own ruling); the helper's answer to S1-77 (T-325, T-635c; CQ-354); the K-01 minor version open gap 194 names.

### Defaults (T-1530), numbered after T-1525's

209. **The wait at an event step: S1-10 ends the walk after its own rows, with no open question and no clock** (the card's Gives 1; D3). **The keys a turn never gives:** `id_checked`, alone in this table. It is given only when the owner's ID upload lands on the secure page (K-21 v1 `finishUpload`: "for an ID document, S1-10 is written (the ID confirmed, accepted as given)"; K-22 v1 `id_uploads`: a `sign_up` row is the ID confirmed), never in a chat (MASTER-FLOW-A line 183: "Never: take an ID photo in the chat"; MC C10: "The owner uploads a photo or file of an ID document on the secure page, never in the chat"), and its result is read at S1-32 (line 180: "The result comes back at S1-32"). A key joins the table only by a later README default naming its source. **The rule:** where the walk applies as a move a plain step with no gate and no buttons (walked on a list or on a chosen branch, or after a buttons settler's stretch: default 199) whose `gives` in the turn's pinned graph (K-01 v1 `step.gives`; `graph.ts` `GraphStep.gives`) holds a key of the table, the step is walked as any plain step is, its rows by its own writer (`writes.ts` default 9), and the walk stops right after it: a new stop, `waitsOnEvent`, naming the step and the table's keys it gives (arch 03 line 191: "waits on a clock or event"). The step's next is not read in that turn, so a next the bundle does not hold (S1-75 in bundle v3) is neither reached nor refused; every step the walk does reach is still read from the pinned graph, and one the bundle does not hold is still refused `stepNotInBundle` (K-01 README: "a step a bundle does not hold is not reachable"). No open question, question row or clock row is written for the stop (D3: `writes.ts`'s `asked` is not run for it, and S1-10 compiles with no card, so it has no Clock line either), and the part is answered committed with the keys its writer gave. **A writer never gives a table key:** one that does is refused (`eventKeyAtWalk`, a refusal the walk adds beside `givesNotStep`, naming the step and the key alone), with nothing written. With no writer bound for such a step the part is refused as today (notBuilt, `tells` or `gives`: default 9), with nothing written. **Why a rule, not a step id:** it reads only what a bundle holds (the step's own `gives`) and a table this contract states, it is the same for every bundle and every step, and it never relaxes a refusal, since it ends a walk earlier, never later. Bundle v3's S1-10 is the only step that gives `id_checked` (MASTER-FLOW-A line 188; K-09 v1's step table: S1-10 gives id_checked and mobile). **What comes after the wait:** the next text finds no open question of S1-10's (open gap 195); the walk on from S1-10 once the upload gives `id_checked` is the call's that writes it (K-09 v1 `SetupS1_10` through K-21 `finishUpload`) and S1-75's ruling's, not these defaults'. **A K-01 field** that says a step waits on an event would be the bundle's own word for the stop and retire this table for a bundle that carries it (open gap 194); it is not owed for this slice.
210. **S1-10's writer at the walk: the chat's own number as the mobile, one text with the secure link, never `id_checked`** (the card's Gives 2; D1, D2). **The port:** a read-only step writer bound by step id (`WalkDeps.writers`, "S1-10"; T-636 re-scoped for the text path as S10-3), which the walk calls as S1-10's writer whether S1-10 follows S1-09's owner branch or S1-76's (both rejoin at S1-10: T-1526's prints), handed the call's transaction and business, the step as the turn's pinned graph holds it, the part, its choice and `writer.turn`, the object S1-09's settler keeps its reading on (default 201). It writes nothing; the commit writes what it returns. **Whose rows:** only the call's business (`caller.businessId`) and the caller's own person (`caller.personId`), only when that person owns the company the turn runs under, as default 204 holds S1-09's writer (`noCompany` otherwise), and only when the turn's handle is that person's own (K-04 `person_handles`, which K-22 v1.1's `page_links_sent_to_fk` also requires), else refused (`noPerson`); either way nothing is written. **The claim:** `owner` or `helper`, read only from S1-09's reading on the turn object (default 201), never from the words, the option decide named or a row; with no reading held (a walk in which S1-09's settler did not run), refused (`claimUnknown`), nothing written, until a record keeps the claim (open gap 200). **The number (D1):** "Skipped on a plain text, where the number comes with the chat" (line 170). A chat carries the number when the turn's service is `imessage`, `rcs` or `sms` (K-06 v1 service; default 135) and the turn's handle is a phone handle (K-04 `handles`) whose number has its K-04 `phones` row on file (the row a texting number's chat and stop state live on: K-04 core "Phones"; `accounts.mobile` refers to it, `accounts_mobile_fk`). S1-10's writer writes no `phones` row at the walk: a number with none on file is refused (`noPhoneRow`), nothing written (open gap 201 asks the read). That number is the mobile, and S1-10's lines 0 and 1 (the mobile question, line 185's "Which mobile do only you use? It becomes your sign-in.", and C9's "Please text me from that mobile. Your text proves the phone is yours.": `c9.ts` `ASK_FROM_MOBILE`) are never written. Any other turn (an email turn, or an iMessage chat on an email address, line 185's "Apple Messages, which doesn't share your number") is refused (`noNumberInChat`), with nothing written and no reply (D1; open gap 199). No code is sent: the owner's own text from that number proves it, since a code is never texted to a mobile that has not texted in (MC C9; K-22 v1 `accounts.mobile_proven_at`: "by the person's own text from it to Rosie's number"). The line check (line 171) is not run (open gap 196), and the number is never one suggested from a card or a listing (line 175): it is the texter's own. **What it writes for the owner's claim, in this order:** (1) K-22 v1 `accounts`, the caller's person's row (scope none: one sign-in for every company; line 187: "Done once, for every company"), inserted with `mobile` the chat's number and `mobile_proven_at` the time of the turn's last text (its `sentAt` in the decided context) when the person has none; nothing written when the row on file already holds that number, proven; refused (`mobileOnFile`) when it holds another mobile, since a number change needs a signed-in session and a new ID check (line 181; S11-52), and refused (`mobileTaken`) when the number is another person's account mobile (`accounts_mobile_key`), naming no one; `email` and `email_proven_at` are never written (D5). (2) The secure link's K-22 v1.1 `page_links` row (default 211). (3) One outbox row naming S1-10 and line 2 (say[4], S1-10's third line from Rosie to the owner as bundle v3 compiles it: T-1526's slot print names say[0], say[2] and say[4]; K-05 v1.22 `lineNo`; `writes.ts` default 16; `compose-input/master-line.ts`: "no line from Rosie to this reader at the row's place" fails closed), the line "This is Rosie, an automated assistant, setting up {{biz.name}}. Your secure ID upload is below. These texts are only about your business on Rosie." with its card (MASTER-FLOW-A line 187 as C9 and C10 correct it; quoted whole as bundle v3's say[4] carries it: round 2, DL-1149, QA's note (b)), to the turn's handle and person, under the call's business, on the turn's service (default 132), send class `quick_reply` (default 133), no booking, no question and no plan (arch 01 line 41), written only when the stop home lets Rosie text this owner (`mayText` on the stop reads, `actions/src/homes/stop`; default 189). With no text allowed, neither the row nor the link is written, (1) still is, and the walk still waits (default 209). The row is no first text on this path (S1-02 and S1-09 already texted this handle about this business: K-05 default 50), so finalize adds no opener, and K-01 default 19's open gap on S1-10's first-text wording does not arise here. **What it writes for the helper's claim:** "On the helper path it sets only the helper's own sign-in. The owner's comes from S1-77's link" (line 182): (1) alone, the helper's own `accounts` row, by the same rules; no link and no row, so a helper never gets S1-10's ID text and no `setup_id_check` link is ever made for a helper (default 214). **What it gives:** `mobile`, never `id_checked` (D2; default 209's refusal). **No event-log entry:** K-08 v1.1 has no kind for an account's mobile or for a link issued (open gap 1; CQ-56; K-21 v1 open gap 10), so `stateEvents` is empty, as defaults 182 and 204 keep theirs. **Never logged:** the number, the code or the link, in a span, a log line, an error, the unit's why or a row's plain columns (BP rule 7; K-21 "Never in a span or a log"); what it throws names its fault and the step alone. **The reads and writes it needs:** open gap 201.
211. **The secure link: minted at the walk, kept only as its hash, handed to the slot filler in memory** (the card's Gives 3; K-21 v1 "Links"). **The code:** 16 bytes from a cryptographically secure random generator (`crypto.getRandomValues`), written as 32 lowercase hexadecimal characters: 128 bits, as K-21 default 18 asks, inside K-21's `linkCode` pattern (`^[A-Za-z0-9-]{3,64}$`: `page-api.v1.schema.json`; `web/src/link/open.ts` `isLinkCode`; `web/src/shell/config.ts` `linkUrl`), never "_" (T-512's security note c: the pattern refuses it, though `linkCode`'s description lists it), and in this one made form only, which staging's openLink checks before any read (default 212; T-728's security note e). **The link:** `https://<LINK_HOST><BASE_PATH>/<code>`, as `linkUrl` builds it from the Worker's vars (Q-51; K-21 default 5), `rosfield.com/test/<code>` on staging (DL-014, DL-023), read from the same two vars the web shell reads, never a constant. **The row that holds it:** one K-05 `stateWrite` insert into K-22 v1.1 `page_links`, in the same commit as the outbox row whose message carries the link (K-21 "How a link is issued"; arch 10 line 508): `id` the walk's row id of kind `link` (`writer.rowId("link")`, so `link-s1-10-<turn key>-1`: default 131), `business_id` the call's business, `person_id` the caller's person, `purpose` `setup_id_check` and `step_id` `S1-10` (`page_links_purpose_step`), `code_sha256` the code's sha256 in UTF-8 as 64 lowercase hex characters (`page_links_code_hash_form`), never the code, `sent_to_handle_id` the turn's handle, `issued_at` the writer's clock (its binding hands it in, as the paging service's clock is handed in; read once a walk), `expires_at` 7 days after it, and `ended_at`, `ended_why` and `ended_by_contact_change_id` null (K-22 rule 8: the Tool API inserts only a live link). **Its life:** 7 days, K-21 v1's fixtures' example (K-21 open gap 4; K-22 v1.1 `expires_at`), standing until Jay or the CTO sets it (open gap 197). It ends earlier once S1-10's keys are given (K-21 defaults 13 and 19, `step_done`), which never happens on this slice, or on a contact change (D75). A second S1-10 for the same person makes a second link, and the first stays as it is until it lapses or ends. **The code reaches the slot filler in memory only:** K-21 has code hand "the code to the slot filler (C-088), which fills K-03's `link.secure` with the host and the code when it finalizes that outbox row in the same run; the code is stored nowhere but in the message that carries it" (and K-04 v1.1: a code is only in a row's words, its plan and its final parts). No frozen K-09 shape carries it from gateAndCommit to finalizeMessage, and none is needed: the turn face holds it between those two calls of the one turn, as it holds a turn's texts for compose input (`actions/src/tool-api/turn-face/held.ts`; K-05 v1.3 default 47), keyed by the id of the outbox row that carries the link, in the isolate's own memory, bounded as `MAX_HELD` bounds the texts. Only the codes of the run whose rows K-05 committed are held, the commit's answer `committed` with `repeated` false: a run the gate ran again after a version conflict (`commit.ts` `GATE_RUNS`), a refusal, a throw or a repeat (K-05 v1 item 1: nothing written) drops its codes, since no link row holds their hashes. Finalize's slot facts for that row take `links.secure` as the link (`actions/src/finalize/slots/types.ts` `LinkFacts.secure`), and the face lets the code go once that row's finalize has answered (send, sendAndLog or passedOn). Compose input lists `link.secure` among the row's allowed links (K-03 `allowedLinks`; S10-3 adds it in `actions/src/compose-input` if none is given there today), since S1-10's line 2 carries a link, its card's and its plain text's. **The sample link is never sent:** S1-10's line 2 as compiled carries the master's illustration `rosfield.com/v/s01` (MC C6; T-1526's security review, "New tasks"), never a value; the only link the row may carry is the `link.secure` fact, and a final text with any other link fails the Values check (arch 07 line 409), so a fallback text that carries the sample path is never sent: it ends passed on. **A run that stops between the commit and the row's finalize** (a crash, or an isolate gone) has no code: the row composed again has no `link.secure` fact, the unfilled slot holds it to its fallback and then the passed-on line, the link row lapses unused, and nothing sends a new link (K-21 open gap 19, which stands; open gap 195). **Never logged:** the code and the link go nowhere but the row's final parts, which are the message (K-21 "Never in a span or a log"; BP rule 7), and the face's hold names neither in a span or an error.
212. **Staging's openLink: the link's server side** (the card's Gives 3; T-512's reports; T-728's security note e). Today `runtime/src/wiring/root.ts` (lines 847 to 881) joins only the test-phone page's two calls and the three sign-in calls on staging, so openLink answers 503 and no link page opens, and `web/src/link/routes.ts` `ROUTES` is still empty (T-512's gap). ### Open gaps, defaults 209 to 215

Numbered after T-1525's. Each with its default where one is in use. For QUESTIONS.md, in these words:

194. **A K-01 field for how a plain step stops the walk** (defaults 209 and 214; arch 03 line 191). K-01 v1 says a step asks only by a split's message branch or a plain step's buttons, and says nothing of a step that waits on an event, so bundle v3's S1-10 and S1-77 compile alike, as walked plain steps, though S1-10 waits on the ID upload (MC C10) and S1-77 asks the helper by its words (C9). 195. **What a text after S1-10's wait gets, and a new link** (defaults 209, 211 and 213). T-512's expired page tells the reader "If you still need the page, ask Rosie in your chat for a new link." (`web/src/link/pages.ts` `EXPIRED_WORDS`), which nothing answers on this slice, and a link lost to a run that stopped before its finalize is not sent again either (K-21 open gap 19). 197. **The secure link's life** (default 211; K-21 open gap 4). For Jay or the CTO: how long S1-10's link lives. Default in use: 7 days, K-21's fixtures' example; it ends earlier once S1-10's keys are given (K-21 default 19).

Your text proves the phone is yours."), which is a later card. Default in use: refused, nothing written, no reply.

## v1.13 (T-1539): a final refusal answers turnRefused, and notBuilt is final

**Why** (contract-change skill item 2): T-1529 (DL-1134) traced every walk refusal to silence. A refused part (`CompaniesRefusedError`, `WalkRefusedError`, `KindsRefusedError`) is thrown out of the gate, and only a `NotBuiltError` becomes an answer (`actions/src/tool-api/tool-api.ts` lines 595 to 598 and 608 to 613); the runtime takes any throw as no answer, resends once, reruns the turn at each 60-second lapse up to three tries, each try redoing the frontier and decide, and then parks it with a row that is never given words, so the owner hears nothing (`ops/cards/T-1529.md`, "Why": a two-company answer while the party flag is off; a step a bundle lacks). A notBuilt answer takes the same path, since the lease ignores how a run ended (the ruling's new fact: `runtime/src/actor/lease.ts` lines 740 to 753 and 1221 to 1233). The architect's ruling (`ops/reviews/refusal-reply/rule-refusal.json`, judged on main 34f0aaa9; DL-1142; its card 1 is this card, DL-1174) sorts the faults into final and transient (its ruling 1) and asks for a K-09 minor version, not a README default, since open gap 157's own text calls for one when a caller must tell refusals apart, and the runtime must (its ruling 2): the answer `turnRefused` {`from`, `fault`, `ids`}, labels only, and notBuilt, until now a package default of the Tool API base (`actions/src/tool-api/errors.ts` lines 1 to 7), adopted as final. **Kind:** additive, a minor version, v1.13, the next free number after v1.12 (`frozen.json`); no CTO OK is needed and the 7-day rule does not apply. `tool-api.v1.13.schema.json` holds every v1.12 definition under its own name, each by `$ref` to `tool-api.v1.12.schema.json`, which stays as it is; it restates two, `gateAndCommit.errors` (v1.12's by reference, or `turnRefusedError`, or `notBuiltError` naming gateAndCommit) and `gateAndCommit.exchange` (v1.8's keywords word for word, its outcome taking v1.13's errors), and adds two, `turnRefusedError` and `notBuiltError`. So every v1 to v1.12 exchange is a v1.13 exchange (`npm run contracts:check` checks every earlier accepted fixture against v1.13), and a consumer on v1.12 keeps working: neither answer comes before the base gives it (the ruling's card 5). No call or key is added, and no named rule: the schema itself refuses each of v1.13's refused fixtures. **Not here:** any code (the base answering `turnRefused`, card 5; the runtime's park on a final answer and the crash park's line, cards 6 and 7; QA's contract test, card 3; the end-to-end test, card 8: "Who reads v1.13"); K-05's park, which is K-05 v1.23's (T-1531, merged: `parkTurn` takes tries 1 to 3, a reason in this answer's form and the passedOn line); Jay's words for the park (K-05 open gap 103); the faults of the lists the ruling does not name (open gap 204).

### Defaults (T-1539), numbered after T-1530's

216. **`turnRefused`: what a final refusal answers, labels only, nothing written** (the ruling's rulings 2 and 3). gateAndCommit answers a final fault (default 217) with `{"error": "turnRefused", "from", "fault", "ids"}` (`turnRefusedError`), in place of the throw the base lets out today (`tool-api.ts` lines 608 to 613). `from` names the fault list, one of six (default 217). `fault` is the list's own label as its error carries it (`WalkRefusedError.fault` and the like). `ids` are that error's `ids` (for a read's error, the read's own name, its `read`), in their order, each once (a repeat left out, the first kept), and only those that are labels (K-08's `safeId`, the runtime's `isLabel`): an id that is not a label is left out, never sent. Each value is a K-08 v1 `safeId`, the three labels K-05 v1.23's `parkReason` takes field for field (K-05 v1.23 item 84; T-1531's runtime review), so no person's words, number or address can stand in it, and nothing of the refused part is in the answer: no quote, no field's value or words, no company's or business's name, no row's values (`refuse-turn-refused-fault-words.json`, `refuse-turn-refused-id-number.json`, `refuse-turn-refused-with-words.json`, `refuse-turn-refused-ids-twice.json`). Nothing is written: the call's transaction is rolled back, as for every gateAndCommit error and as today's throw already does (each refusing error's own words: "nothing was written"), so no part, walk row, core action, open question or clock row moves, and the open question stays at its version (the ruling's ruling 3). The base's span for the call names the answer and the fault as labels, as it names any refusal, never a value. Only gateAndCommit answers `turnRefused`.
217. **Which faults are final and which transient** (the ruling's ruling 1, each with its file line on main at this version). A final fault is one another try cannot change: the same request, on the same rows, bundle and binding, refuses the same way. The six lists `from` names: (1) `walk`: `WalkRefusedError` (`actions/src/commit/walk/errors.ts` lines 41 to 76). Transient: `questionNotLive` (line 53). Final: the other sixteen, `flowNotInBundle`, `stepNotInBundle`, `branchNotInBundle`, `pathNotInBundle`, `stepNotWellFormed`, `loop`, `walkTooLong`, `notDecided`, `businessNotInScope`, `noServiceOrChatId`, `noTurnKey`, `givesNotStep`, `settledNotInBundle`, `settledNotRows`, `rowNotStep` and `eventKeyAtWalk` (lines 42 to 58). (2) `commit`: `CommitRefusedError` (`actions/src/commit/errors.ts` lines 44 to 76). Transient: `wrongState` (line 57) and `stillChanged` (line 58). Final: `callerNotTurn`, `turnIdMismatch`, `traceIdMismatch`, `stateTable`, `outboxRowWithPlan`, `effectWithoutKey`, `effectKeyTwice`, `effectNotWellFormed`, `idTwice`, `askAgainNotAlone`, `noTurnKey` and `partNotGated` (lines 45 to 56). (3) `companies`: `CompaniesRefusedError` (`actions/src/tool-api/journey-1/companies/errors.ts` lines 81 to 121). Final: all nineteen (lines 82 to 100), `secondCompany` among them (line 100; thrown naming S1-04 at `companies/writer.ts` line 290). (4) `kinds`: `KindsRefusedError` (`actions/src/tool-api/journey-1/kinds/errors.ts` lines 59 to 86). Final: `wrongStep`, `noBranch`, `noWords`, `noPerson`, `branchNotWords`, `notBookedBesideKept`, `notAnswered` and `reviewWrites`, and `reviewRefused` (line 67) unless its ids carry `transient`, openReviewItem's own code (thrown naming S1-03 and that code at `kinds/writers.ts` line 229), when it is transient. (5) `kindsRead`: `KindsReadError` (`kinds/errors.ts` lines 11 to 39; its reads `readKindCatalog` and `readPickedKinds`). Transient: `transient` (line 20). Final: `notFound` and `noCatalog`. (6) `companiesRead`: `CompaniesReadError` (`companies/errors.ts` lines 10 to 43; its reads `readPickedKinds`, `readBusinessKinds` and `readTurnState`). Transient: `transient` (line 21). Final: `notFound`, `unknownFact` and `notWellFormed`. Every notBuilt is final (default 219). Anything else is transient and is thrown as today: an error of a list the ruling does not name (the listing's, S1-08's and the turn face's: open gap 204), K-05's transient, a handler's answer outside the contract (`ToolApiContractError`), and any unknown throw. A fault added later to one of the six lists is final, as the ruling's "every other fault in" those lists reads, unless a later K-09 minor version names it transient. The schema holds the list: `from` is one of the six, and each transient fault above is refused (`turnRefusedError`'s `not`; `refuse-turn-refused-transient-fault.json`, `refuse-turn-refused-commit-still-changed.json`, `refuse-turn-refused-read-transient.json`, `refuse-turn-refused-review-transient.json`, `refuse-turn-refused-from-unclassified.json`).
218. **The runtime never resends or reruns a final answer: before the commit it parks the turn at once, but never ahead of a STOP's own commit, and after the commit it cannot park** (the ruling's ruling 2; arch 11 line 576's park, applied sooner; K-05 v1.23; round 2, DL-1177). **Before the commit.** The turn face's calls in turn order are buildFrontier, gateAndCommit, then composeInput and finalizeMessage over the committed row (`actions/src/tool-api/turn-face/turn-face.ts` lines 6 to 14). When gateAndCommit answers `turnRefused` or `notBuilt`, or buildFrontier answers `notBuilt`, the handle actor makes no second call of it for that turn and the lease runs the turn no more: it parks the turn at once through K-05 v1.23's `parkTurn`, on the try it reached (1 to 3), with the reason `turnRefused`'s three labels field for field, or, for `notBuilt`, `{"from": "toolApi", "fault": "notBuilt", "ids": [the answer's call]}`. The park writes, in one transaction, the turn parked, K-37's `parked_turns` row for Rosie's team and one row to the turn's own handle whose words are K-01's passedOn fixed line word for word, the standing default until Jay gives the park's words (K-05 v1.23 item 84 and its default 216; K-05 open gap 103). Rosie's team is paged only through T-319's paging, as the passed-on path is; no new page rule. The turn never commits after its park (v1's `turnParkedError`). **A STOP in the turn commits first** (DL-508 and DL-521, as built: `runtime/src/turn/turn.ts` lines 453 to 459, 466, 722 to 728 and 740 to 741). When the turn holds a STOP and its decided commit or its frontier fails, a final answer included, its keyword texts still commit core-only on that run, under the keyword texts' own caller, before any park, since a park records no core action (K-05 v1 `parkTurn` has none: `contracts/data-access/data-access.v1.schema.json` lines 2268 to 2289, and v1.23's form adds none; DL-593, CQ-274). That core-only commit is the one further gateAndCommit a turn makes after a final answer. It records the STOP and closes the turn committed, so the turn is never parked (`parkTurn` answers `alreadyCommitted`) and no passedOn line is written for it. A turn holding a STOP is never parked at once: if its core-only commit does not commit either, it keeps today's path (below). So the passedOn line never goes to a number whose STOP is in the turn: no park at once comes before the STOP's commit, limit 8 then drops at send every row to a number whose stop is recorded (K-05 v1.23 item 84, step 3; RULES 7.2), and card 7's crash park keeps the same rule for a turn whose STOP never committed. **After the commit.** composeInput and finalizeMessage run only over a committed turn's rows (`turn-face.ts` lines 13 and 14; `runtime/src/turn/compose.ts` lines 1 to 3), and a committed turn is never parked: K-05 `parkTurn` answers `alreadyCommitted` (`data-access.v1.schema.json` line 2309; arch 11 line 576: after the commit only rows without a plan are composed). So a `notBuilt` or a refusal from either cannot park, and no passedOn line is written for it. The turn stays committed and the row stays `planned` with no plan, as today: the lease gives the reply up after its third try (`lapseOf`'s `giveUp`: `runtime/src/actor/lease.ts` lines 1221 to 1233), and once the row's turn committed more than K-05 default 225's window (10 minutes) before a claim's `now`, the claim passes the row (T-1536), so later rows to that person still go. The person gets no line for that row, and nothing is written for it. **A transient answer or a throw** before the commit keeps today's path: one resend, a rerun at each lapse up to three tries, then the crash park, which card 7 gives the same line.
219. **`notBuilt`, adopted as K-09's own, and final** (the ruling's rulings 1 and 2). The base's answer `{"error": "notBuilt", "call"}` (`actions/src/tool-api/errors.ts` lines 34 to 38, a package default its lines 1 to 7 leave for a K-09 minor version to adopt; `tool-api.ts` lines 595 to 598) is K-09's from v1.13, `notBuiltError`, in the same shape, so the base changes nothing for it. A call answers it when its handler, or a seam the handler reaches, is not built: every `NotBuiltError` (the walk's `WalkNotBuiltError`, `walk/errors.ts` lines 78 to 137; the commit's, `commit/errors.ts` lines 7 to 23; the kinds', `kinds/errors.ts` lines 88 to 115; the listing's, `listing/errors.ts` lines 97 to 122; S1-08's, `plan/errors.ts` lines 34 to 57), with its transaction rolled back and nothing read or written. It names the call alone, never the seam, the step or a value. Final: no retry binds what is missing, only a deploy does (`commit-s1-04-not-built.json`). v1.13 adds it to gateAndCommit's errors, naming gateAndCommit (`refuse-not-built-other-call.json`); every other call answers it as the base does today, and those calls' errors are not restated here (open gap 205).
220. **What the owner and Rosie's team are told** (the ruling's ruling 2 and its defaults). The words for every final answer are passedOn (default 218), whatever the fault: a second company, a step the bundle lacks and a "which?" are not told apart in words until Jay gives them (open gap 208). The labels reach Rosie's team only in the `parked_turns` row's summary built from them (K-05 v1.23 default 220), never in the owner's text. An owner's answer that a person could put right by answering again (a mix of kinds S1-02 holds no branch for, a shared-kinds word that names no pick) is parked with the line like any final refusal (the ruling's fourth default; open gap 209).

Of `commit-s1-02-refused-step-not-in-bundle.json`, as an exchange: `refuse-turn-refused-transient-fault.json` (the walk's `questionNotLive`, transient, answered `turnRefused`; with `stepNotInBundle` it is the accepted fixture). ### Who reads v1.13

Each consumer adopts v1.13 in a task that needs it; tasks already building finish on their version (build prompt 6.4). v1.13 changes no code: until card 5 merges, the base still throws a refused part and answers `notBuilt` as it does today.

- **The Tool API base** (C-074; the action-layer engineer; the ruling's card 5, after T-1528, T-635a and S10-2 merge, so its every-fault test covers their faults): in `actions/src/tool-api/tool-api.ts` (the catch at lines 585 to 614) and `errors.ts`, an error of the six lists whose fault default 217 holds final is answered `turnRefused` (`from` by its error class, `fault`, `ids` its labels each once), with the call's transaction rolled back as for any refusal; a transient one, and any other throw, is thrown as today; `notBuilt` is answered as today. The answer is the base's own, as `notBuilt` is: it is never held to the handler's check, which reads gateAndCommit's errors at v1.5 (`validator.ts`), unless that check moves to v1.13's, which is the action-layer engineer's choice. **Proves it with:** each final fault of the six lists answers `turnRefused` with its labels and nothing written; each transient one is thrown; a two-company answer with the flag off answers `commit-s1-04-refused-second-company.json`'s outcome; a repeated id and an id that is no label are left out.
- **The runtime** (C-046, the turn; C-180, the park; the runtime engineer; cards 6 and 7): default 218, in `runtime/src/turn/turn.ts` (no resend on a final answer; a STOP's core-only commit before any park kept as DL-508 and DL-521 built it), `runtime/src/actor/lease.ts` (no rerun; the park at once on the try reached, for an answer before the commit only, the reason kept with the turn while it parks: T-1531's runtime note 1) and `runtime/src/turn/park.ts` (`parkRequestOf` in K-05 v1.23's form, the reason this answer's labels field for field, or default 218's for `notBuilt`); card 7, the crash park's line. The answers' types come from v1.13's generated module (`npm run contracts:types`). The runtime engineer's notes for card 6 (round 1 review): (a) a HELP or START turn that also holds a danger read gets DL-864's `notBuilt` from the keyword door (`turn.ts` lines 650 to 657), so it now parks at once with the passedOn line instead of after three tries, a path card 6 tests; (b) the reason stays with a parking turn across a lapse (T-1531's runtime note 1).
- **K-05 v1.23's park** (C-152; the database engineer; card 4): takes the reason as this answer gives it; nothing here changes it.
- **QA** (C-140; card 3): `tests/contract/tool-api.v1.test.ts` selects entries by version (DL-125), so v1.13's thirteen fixtures join its generic checks now; v1.13's own part is card 3's (below).
- **The Tool API mock** (C-186): covers the versions it was last generated from until it is generated again (open gap 123; `contracts/mock/generate.mjs`); no two accepted v1.13 fixtures share a request with each other or with an earlier fixture, so it can take them.
- **Decide, the composer and the pages** (C-115, C-118; the AI engineer; the frontend engineer): no change; a page's or an agent's call answers `notBuilt` as before.
- **The architect** (later K-09 versions): open gaps 204 and 205.

**Contract test, v1.13 (QA, its own task: the ruling's card 3).** `tool-api.v1.13.schema.json` holds every v1.12 definition by `$ref` but the four this section names, and every earlier accepted fixture is accepted by v1.13 at its own `$defs` entry; the index holds exactly the thirteen fixtures "Fixtures, v1.13" names, each accepted or refused as listed, and each refused one, mended as its entry says, is accepted (at gateAndCommit's exchange, `turnRefusedError` or gateAndCommit's errors); the schema refuses copies with each transient fault of default 217 (the walk's `questionNotLive`, the commit's `wrongState`, a `kindsRead` and a `companiesRead` `transient`, kinds' `reviewRefused` carrying `transient`), a `from` outside the six, a `fault` or an id with a space or an at sign, and `notBuilt` with a seam or a step beside its call, and accepts a copy with each final fault the default lists (one per list at least); each accepted `turnRefused` answer, less its `error`, is accepted by K-05 v1.23's `parkReason`; `commit-s1-02-refused-step-not-in-bundle.json`'s labels equal `park-turn-sam-refused-at-once.json`'s reason field for field; the requests of `commit-s1-04-refused-second-company.json` and `commit-s1-04-not-built.json` differ from `commit-s1-04-some-share.json`'s and `commit-s1-04.json`'s only in their turn's ids; and default 217's list agrees with the fault lists as the code holds them (each list's union read from its file, every fault named final or transient, none left out), so a fault added to a list without a word here fails the test and is classified in a later version.

Numbered after T-1530's. Each with its default where one is in use. For QUESTIONS.md, in these words:

## After v1.13, with no new version (T-1557): a text that fits nothing, or a question asked twice, is passed on, not left silent

**Why** (DL-1213, DL-1214; `ops/reviews/phone-test-nov23/diagnosis.json`, the none_fits finding with its skeptic's corrected answer and fix, which win): on the user's iPhone the owner's fourth text, sent just after S1-09's question was asked again, got nothing at all. Both reads agreed on `none_fits`; meaning search failed (on staging `turnFace` is closed, `runtime/src/wiring/picks.ts` line 111, and the `closestEntries` handler is a notBuilt stub, `actions/src/tool-api/tool-api.ts` line 363, since T-297 is unbuilt), so no re-read ran and the planner stayed null (C-117: T-371 and its verbs, T-316, unbuilt; `runtime/src/turn/turn.ts` lines 789 to 805 and 863 to 867); the gate answered the part `noAction` with no limit run (`actions/src/gate/framework/gate.ts` lines 26 to 28 and 300 to 302, its default 2); the commit wrote only the text's safety outcome, since v1.12's ask-again unit needs a part answered `asked` (`actions/src/commit/ask-again.ts` lines 110 to 118) and S1-09's question was already at ask count 2 (default 194's cap); so nothing was composed (`runtime/src/turn/compose.ts` lines 397 to 459), and a committed turn is never parked (default 218). Frozen K-09 v1 does not allow that silence: it gives a `none_fits` part `noAction` only when "the planner took" it (`tool-api.v1.schema.json` line 1644), and a part the planner does not settle "take[s] the passed-on path instead, which the turn's commit writes ('I've passed this on.': arch 02 line 123)" (lines 890, 894 and 1973). So the silence is a gap in the build, not something the contract allows; default 4's "the turn goes on to the planner" names a planner that does not exist yet. The sibling silence is a part decide itself ends in one question at ask count 2 or more, which default 194 leaves with nothing and open gap 176 (CQ-372) left "silent until the clock fires", a clock unwired on staging (DL-1011). The masters say neither may end in silence: RULES 5.9 ("An unclear or missing answer: ask once, then the step's safe default; ...; then tell the owner"), S13-25 rules 16 to 18 ("Ask once, plainly", "Still unclear, or no answer: do the step's safe default", "Then tell the owner, or Rosie's team, what happened"), S13-23 rule 4 ("Don't know? Say who will answer ... Pass it on."), S13-29 rule 54 (a person "when you asked and still can't tell") and arch 03 line 189 ("Still nothing: the planner ...; a reply to its question that fits nothing goes to a person"). The lead ruled (DL-1214): a stand-in for the passed-on path while the planner and meaning search are unbuilt, reusing the park at once of default 218, and one re-ask for a question asked once. **Kind:** this README's text alone, as defaults 191, 199 and 200 to 215 were (DL-1033 ruling 7). No version, schema, named rule, fixture, index entry or `frozen.json` changes, and no frozen file is edited: the park is K-05 v1.23's `parkTurn` as frozen, whose `parkReason` takes the two new reasons as they are (`from`, `fault` and `ids` are free K-08 `safeId`s: `contracts/data-access/data-access.v1.23.schema.json` lines 207 to 229), and gateAndCommit's schema already takes default 222's row and raise, as it "takes a row, a raised count and a closed question on any committed answer" (v1.12, "Named rule, v1.12"). **Why it sits here:** after v1.13's section, whose default 218 it extends. **Not here:** any code (the runtime's park, T-1558; the commit's re-ask, T-1559: "Who reads defaults 221 to 223"); the planner, meaning search and the re-read after it (T-371, T-316, T-297, T-1159; M3b), whose verbs retire defaults 221 and 223; the words of the line (open gap 208); an id field's value at S1-09 (K-02 README default 24).

### Defaults (T-1557), numbered after T-1539's

221. **A decided turn that fits nothing, with no planner to settle it, is parked at once before the commit, with the passedOn line** (the card's Gives 2; the lead's ruling, DL-1214). **When:** while the planner (C-117) and meaning search (`closestEntries`, T-297) are unbuilt, a turn of decided texts is parked at once, after meaning search and any re-read and before gateAndCommit (in the runtime's preparation of the decided request, `runtime/src/turn/turn.ts` `prepareDecided`), when all of these hold, each read from the turn's decided result and context: (a) every part of the decided result is `none_fits`, `no_op` or `redact` with outcome `go` (K-02 v1 `resultPart`), and at least one is `none_fits`: no part would be committed, asked, stopped or held; (b) no entry from meaning search settled the part, for any reason: meaning search gave no entries; or it answered any error (`indexUnavailable`, `searchedAlready`, `notBuilt` or any other: default 4), threw, or answered badly, each of which `closestEntries` turns into null (`turn.ts` lines 863 to 879); or it did not run at all, because the turn's one re-read was already spent (the reads disagreed, or a part was unmapped, so the re-read with why `disagreed` ran and the meaning search branch is skipped: `turn.ts` lines 784 to 789; or the decided result came already re-read); or the re-read with why `noneFits` ran on the entries search added and still answers `none_fits` (round 2, the runtime engineer's point; DL-1227). Read at the end of `prepareDecided` as built, after search and any re-read, (b) holds whenever (a) does, so the runtime need tell none of these cases apart, and a part left `none_fits` with outcome `go` is never a builder's choice between a park and silence; (c) `planner` is null; (d) the turn holds no keyword text at all, no STOP, START or HELP, which the runtime reads where it has it: `runFrom` passes the turn's keyword count (the guard's `keywords`, `turn.ts` line 436 on main) into `prepareDecided` as one more argument, since the run carries only `stopAsked` (line 433) and no HELP or START, and the turn is parked only when that count is 0; (e) the context holds no open question, or several, or its one open question has `askCount` 2 or more (K-02 v1 `openQuestion.askCount`, `contracts/model/model.v1.schema.json` lines 507 to 513, filled by the frontier: `actions/src/frontier/sources/open-questions/open-questions.ts` line 222); (f) no open safety hold (arch 06 lines 315 to 327; K-34 v1 `safety_holds`), none opened by the turn's own texts' safety outcomes, and no text of the turn unread (its safety result missing, or a danger text left out of the records: `turn.ts` lines 448 to 459; CQ-65's DL-127 addendum), as default 195 asks of the ask-again row; what the runtime cannot read of these it takes as holding the line back, and does not park. **What it writes:** the run ends not committed, its `why` label `passedOnNoneFits` (DL-1227), with its final reason set, as T-1552's park at once does for a final answer before the commit (default 218; `parkIfFinal` on T-1552's branch), so no gateAndCommit call is made for the turn and the turn is parked on the try it reached (1 to 3) through K-05 v1.23's `parkTurn`: in one transaction, the turn parked, K-37's `parked_turns` row for Rosie's team and one row to the turn's own handle whose words are K-01's passedOn fixed line word for word, the standing default until Jay gives the park's words (K-05 v1.23 item 84 and its default 216; K-05 open gap 103; open gap 208). **The reason:** `{"from": "decide", "fault": "noneFits", "ids": [the step of the context's one open question]}` when the context holds exactly one open question, else `{"from": "decide", "fault": "noneFits", "ids": []}`: labels only, which reach Rosie's team in the `parked_turns` row's summary (K-05 v1.23 default 220) and never the owner's text. Rosie's team is paged only through T-319's paging, as the passed-on path is; no new page rule. **Why each condition:** (a) a part that commits, is asked, stops or is held has its own path, and a turn of `no_op` alone (a thank-you) stays silent, as it asks nothing of anyone; (b) arch 03 line 189's order, the 3 closest entry steps and the one re-read before the planner, so a part the re-read enters goes as any part (T-1159), and a turn has one re-read (Q-41), so once it is spent, whichever why spent it, or once search adds nothing, only the planner is left to settle the part; (c) once the planner is built, its verbs settle the part, the contract's own way (frozen K-09 v1 lines 890 and 894), and this default no longer applies; (d) a STOP's keyword texts commit core-only first and such a turn is never parked (default 218; DL-508, DL-521), and the runtime commits keyword texts ahead of a park only when a safety result is missing or a STOP is asked (T-1552's `runFrom`, `turn.ts` lines 520 to 531 at a8f02d69), so a park beside a HELP or a START would lose HELP's fixed reply or the START record (the skeptic's correction): such a turn commits as today; and since `runFrom` hands back a not-committed `prepareDecided` as it is when no safety result is missing and no STOP is asked (`turn.ts` line 466 on main), only the keyword count passed in keeps a park from going out beside a HELP or a START (the runtime engineer's note 1); (e) a question asked once is asked once more by the commit (default 222; S13-25 rule 16: "Ask once"), which a park before the commit would take over, while with no open question, or several (the lettered "which?" stays CQ-199's lane, default 198), there is nothing to ask again; (f) a routine "passed on" line never goes while a crisis hold is open, and an unread text may answer the question in the next turn (default 195). **Sources:** frozen K-09 v1 `tool-api.v1.schema.json` lines 890, 894, 1644 and 1973 first; arch 03 line 189; RULES 5.9; S13-25 rules 16 to 18; S13-23 rule 4; S13-29 rule 54; default 220's precedent (an answer a person could put right by answering again is parked with the line); DL-1214. **Costs written down:** (1) like every park, it writes no safety outcome of No for the turn's texts (open gap 207); a danger read is still recorded on arrival (v1.1, "The safety record on arrival"), with its line (v1.1 default 36). (2) The contract's own home for this, a passed-on unit in the turn's commit (frozen v1), cannot reuse built code: `sendFixedLine` sends only HELP's reply in a turn (`actions/src/domain/fixed-lines/fixed-lines.ts` lines 30 to 37, its default 1) and the commit has no passed-on unit, so it waits for the planner or a later K-09 version (open gap 210). (3) Nothing of the turn is committed: the open question keeps its version, ask count and clock row, and its clock's safe default fires when that clock runs (S13-25 rule 17).
222. **Default 192 (a), widened: a none_fits part about the one open question counts as asked** (the card's Gives 3; S13-25 rule 16). In default 192 (a), a part with the verb `none_fits` and outcome `go`, which the gate answers `noAction` with limits 1 to 6 `notChecked` (gate.ts default 2), counts as a part decide ended in one question when it is about the one open question; so does a part with the verb `none_fits` and outcome `oneQuestion`, which the gate already answers `asked` (default 192 (a)) but default 192 (c) called "not about" the question, being of another verb, so it was left silent (round 2, the action-layer engineer's note a; DL-1227). A `none_fits` part names no question (K-02 v1 `noneFits`: its verb, quote and why), so with either outcome it is about the context's one open question exactly when the context holds exactly one (default 192 (c), widened for it); with none or several it is about none, and default 192 (c) fails as before. Every other condition stands as default 192 states it: (a)'s "every other part is answered `asked` the same way or `noAction`", no part committed, stopped, held or waiting; (b) no unmapped end and `planner` null; (c); (d) a question about no booking, stored with no options and no read-back (default 193); (e) asked once (default 194); (f) nothing open holds a routine reply back (default 195); (g) a service on the turn. **Then** the commit writes default 192's row and default 194's raise: S1-09's question, for one, is sent once more, its ask count raised from 1 to 2 in the same commit. The `none_fits` part's own answer stays the gate's, with no limit run (`noAction` for outcome `go`, `asked` for `oneQuestion`), `written.outboxRows` names the row and `written.written` the question's row at its new version, as for an asked part. At ask count 2 or more no row is written (default 194), and the turn is parked before the commit: by default 221 when every such part has outcome `go`, by default 223 when one has outcome `oneQuestion`. **Why:** S13-25 rule 16 asks once more after an unclear answer, and a text that fits none of the question's options is one; T-1159 designs a part still `none_fits` after the `noneFits` re-read to "end in one question", and this is that end while meaning search is unbuilt. **What stays silent:** a `none_fits` at ask count 1 to a question default 193 does not cover (options as sent, a read-back or a booking: open gap 175), or under an open hold or beside an unread text (default 195), gets no row, and neither default 221 (e) nor default 223 (c) parks it, as today (default 198). **No exchange shows it:** `askedResendsQuestion` reads only a turn with a part decide ended in one question (`tool-api.rules.mjs` lines 1648 to 1655), so no fixture or rule changes, and open gap 211 asks for a K-09 minor version that restates default 192 (a) with a fixture.
223. **The sibling: a question asked twice and still unclear is passed on, not left silent** (the card's Gives 4; a LEAD RULING over CQ-372's "silent until the clock fires", per RULES 5.9: ask once, then the safe default, then tell the owner or Rosie's team; DL-1214). **When:** a turn of decided texts is parked at once before the commit, exactly as default 221 parks it, when all of these hold: (a) decide ended at least one part in one question (the verb `ask`, or the outcome `oneQuestion`: K-02 v1 `resultPart`) about the context's one open question, as default 192 (c) reads "about", widened as default 222 widens it: a `none_fits` or `enter` part with outcome `oneQuestion` names no question (K-02 v1 `noneFits`; `enter` names a door, an any-time row or an entry step), so it is about the context's one open question exactly when the context holds exactly one (round 2, closing the silence the runtime engineer's note 3 and the action-layer engineer's note a named; DL-1227), and every other part is such a part or is `none_fits`, `no_op` or `redact` with outcome `go`, so no part would be committed, stopped or held, and any `none_fits` among them meets default 221 (b); (b) the decided result holds no unmapped end (K-02 v1.2 `end`, which v1.8 keeps silent: CQ-199, CQ-203) and `planner` is null; (c) the context holds exactly one open question, and its `askCount` is 2 or more (default 194's cap); (d) and (f) as in default 221. **The reason:** `{"from": "decide", "fault": "askedTwice", "ids": [that question's step]}`, the run's not-committed `why` label `passedOnAskedTwice` (DL-1227). Defaults 221 and 223 never both hold: 221 (a) admits only parts with outcome `go` and no `ask`, and 223 (a) needs a part with the verb `ask` or the outcome `oneQuestion`. **Why:** RULES 5.9 and S13-25 rules 16 to 18 ask once, then take the step's safe default, then tell the owner or Rosie's team, and S13-29 rule 54 sends to a person "when you asked and still can't tell"; default 194's cap keeps a re-send from looping, and the park tells without looping, since a parked turn sends one line and asks nothing. **What it does not do:** it takes no safe default at once: the question stays open, at its version and ask count, and its clock's safe default fires when that clock runs (S13-25 rule 17). It reads no question kind: unlike default 222's re-send, the passedOn line names no question and carries no options, read-back or poll, so default 193's reasons do not arise. Default 194 stands for the commit, which still writes no row at 2 or more; this default acts before the commit, so a runtime that builds it never hands such a turn to the commit. **Answers open gap 176 (CQ-372) by the lead.**

### Who reads defaults 221 to 223

Each reader moves in its own card; nothing built changes until then.

- **The runtime** (C-046, the turn; the runtime engineer; T-1558, after T-1551 and T-1552 merge): defaults 221 and 223, in `runtime/src/turn/turn.ts` `prepareDecided`, with one argument more, the turn's keyword count, which `runFrom` passes at its one call (default 221 (d); DL-1227: T-1558's "prepareDecided only" allows that one argument and that call): when either default's conditions hold, set the run's final reason and end the run not committed, its `why` label `passedOnNoneFits` (221) or `passedOnAskedTwice` (223), so T-1552's `parkIfFinal` parks at once. Its proofs, as T-1558's card lists them with this round's: `none_fits` at ask count 2 parks with one passedOn row to the turn's own handle, whether search gave nothing, answered an error or did not run because a disagreed re-read spent the turn's one re-read; `ask` at ask count 2 the same, reason `askedTwice`, and so `none_fits` or `enter` with outcome `oneQuestion`; `none_fits` at ask count 1 is not parked (the commit re-asks: default 222); HELP or START beside `none_fits` commits as today (the keyword count); `no_op` alone stays silent; `none_fits` beside a committed part commits as today, and beside a part asked about the one open question goes as default 222 (ask count 1) or 223 (2 or more) says; a STOP turn commits core-only and is never parked; an open safety hold or an unread text: no park.
- **The commit** (C-087; the action-layer engineer; T-1559): default 222, in `actions/src/commit/ask-again.ts`. Its proofs: `none_fits` at ask count 1 re-asks once, with outcome `go` or `oneQuestion`; at ask count 2 writes nothing; several open questions or none: nothing; beside a committed part: as today; a rerun writes nothing.
- **K-05 v1.23's park** (C-152; the database engineer): takes both reasons as they are; nothing here changes it.
- **Decide** (C-115; the AI engineer): no change.
- **QA** (C-140): no contract test changes, since no schema, rule, fixture or index entry does; T-1558's and T-1559's proofs hold these defaults.
- **The security reviewer:** the line goes only to the turn's own handle, never ahead of a STOP's commit, under an open hold or beside an unread text, and the reason holds labels only.
- **The architect** (later K-09 versions): open gaps 210 and 211.

### Open gaps, defaults 221 to 223

Numbered after v1.13's. Each with its default where one is in use. For QUESTIONS.md, in these words:

210. **The passed-on path's own home: a unit in the turn's commit** (defaults 221 and 223; frozen K-09 v1 lines 890, 894 and 1973). Frozen v1 has the turn's commit write the passed-on path, so the texts' safety outcomes and the line go in one commit; the stand-in parks the turn instead, which writes no outcome of No (open gap 207). For the architect, with the action-layer engineer and the runtime engineer: a K-09 minor version (with the planner, C-117, or before it) adds a passed-on unit to the commit (which `sendFixedLine`'s default 1 does not allow in a turn today), and retires the park for these turns. Default in use: the park at once (defaults 221 and 223).
211. **No frozen exchange shows defaults 221 to 223.** For the architect, with the lead: a K-09 minor version restates default 192 (a) with a commit fixture of a `none_fits` answer to S1-09's question at ask count 1 (the row and the raise), with `askedResendsQuestion` reading it, and K-05 park fixtures with the reasons `noneFits` and `askedTwice` (as open gaps 167, 178 and 191 wait for theirs). Default in use: these defaults' words and T-1558's and T-1559's proofs.

- No new version, round 2, 2026-11-22 (T-1525, before the merge; this README alone, so no schema, rule, fixture, index entry or `frozen.json` changed and no frozen file was edited): round 2 closed round 1's reviews under the lead's rulings (DL-1133): QA's NEED, the security reviewer's FAIL (gap 1, note b) and the action-layer engineer's FAIL (its gap, notes 2, 3, 4 and 6). Default 201 stands as written (QA's point (a)) and restates default 191's "the same words give the same branch" for S1-09: the claim alone follows the helper's option; the settler keeps its reading (branch, claim, reason label, the name to write) on the turn object for both writers. A split settler's "ask" refuses with no reply until T-1528 sends S1-09 again once (QA's point (b); defaults 201, 203 (a) and (c), 207; open gap 179 carded). Fix 1: S1-76's review item's summary is one of six fixed sentences carrying the claim and the reason as labels, and `review.ts` `writeBackOf` takes `controlProven: true` on a `listing_match` item only for an owner's sentence, refusing it (`writeBackNotForKind`) on a helper's, which T-635a builds with the summary read (default 205). Fix 2: a held candidate with a banned word is no match for every reader, with `Reason: banned word.` read from the settler's reading (default 206). Fix 3: default 202's split is at most three tries in a stated order, the first that holds a candidate deciding, so "Lee's Barbershop in Springfield, I'm the owner" reaches the double's demo search on try (1). Fix 4: U+2018, U+2019, U+201C and U+201D are folded to their plain forms before the split, the search and the hold. Fix 5: S1-09 keeps nothing of the listing as read on any branch (K-15 README default 9): the business head takes the typed name, `business_setup` is inserted with its listing columns null only when none is on file, the address stays S1-43's, and a later step keeps the listing's phone or website only once the owner confirms it, by the listing's reference (default 204; open gaps 185 and 190 restated). Fix 6: default 206's website wording, `wordsOf` in place of `holdsWords` (default 202), and S1-76's review item's caller names the call's business for its zone (default 205). Reviews: QA, the action-layer engineer and the security reviewer, as round 1's.
